# PYQ Vault — project notes

**Brand:** the product is **PYQ Vault**, live at **https://www.pyqvault.com** (rebranded from "Question Bank" 2026-06-04 — the repo/folder/Vercel-project are still named `question-bank`; in code the brand is `SITE_NAME` in `layout.tsx`). Lowercase "question bank" in prose still means the question corpus, not the brand.

Free, public past-year-question paper builder for Indian entrance exams. Anyone can filter and preview the public bank by exam/chapter/subtopic/difficulty/PYQ year; **downloading the Question Paper + Answer Key as Word files requires a TEACHER account** (org member; the tagged nda-tracker `.xlsx` sheet is likewise staff-only) — a downloadable Word paper is a teacher artifact, so students get the online product (preview, timed mocks, notes) and a non-staff visitor sees a **"request teacher access"** CTA → `/request-access` (lead capture; superadmin triages at `/superadmin`, migration 0060). Anon browse/preview stays fully open so SEO is untouched (server-enforced `resolveExportAccess`; **teacher-gated 2026-07-18** — was "any signed-in account" from 2026-07-11; see the Decisions entries + [[signup-gate-placement]] + [[project-teacher-access-gate]]). Admins (org members) upload Excel batches privately, then flip individual questions PUBLIC. Sibling app `MHT_CET_AI` posts finalized mocks to `/api/sync/mock` for cross-app dedup. Currently MHT-CET + NDA + **JEE Mains** (**7,418 q PUBLIC — Maths 3,677 · Physics 3,482 · Chemistry 259; Maths AND Physics both COMPLETE** across EVERY sitting on disk 2021-**2026** (`C:\Vilas\LWS_Pune\JEE_Mains\PYQs\2021-2026`); **2026 added 2026-07-25 — all 19 Jan+April per-shift sittings, +475 Maths (380 MCQ + 95 NAT)** via the SAFE lane; MCQ + the bank's **Section-B NAT** numeric-answer format (migration 0061 + `extract --numeric`); `scripts/jee/` pandoc pipeline plus a **two-lane completion pipeline** — SAFE (trustworthy source key → classify+solve+VERIFY agents → `assemble-safe`) vs BLIND (broken/absent key → solve-from-scratch agents that IGNORE the key → `assemble-blind` uses the derived answer), routed by per-paper numbering triage; `paper.skip[]` drops corrupted Qs, `sol-clean`/`scan-flip` clean+gate. **PHYSICS COMPLETE 2026-08-01 — 3,482 q PUBLIC / 7 PRIVATE across 88 papers**, every sitting on disk, now level with Maths; 0 SOLN-vs-KEY across 2,559 Physics PYQ MCQs. Geometry is TRUTHFUL not schematic and independently audited from the spec data: every inscribed vertex exact on its circle, the cone's semi-vertical angle exactly 30°, both shadow rays exactly through the head; Internal choice ("attempt any two of four") → **all** alternatives ingested. `scripts/mh-ssc-10/` pipeline: `render` → `dump-text` → vision/hybrid transcribe → `merge` → `commit` → snapCrop figures → `flip-public`; `config.ts` `CATALOG` HARD-validates chapter, soft-flags subtopic. **Board QPs ship no answer key, so answers are DERIVED/AUTHORED + `reviewFlag:true`** — BUT 11 of the publisher's source PDFs **bundle an official printed solution**, and a 2026-07-27 cross-check of all 295 of those questions found **279 AGREE / 14 BOOK-WRONG / 0 OURS-WRONG** (see the Decisions log). **Un-ingested on disk: the 3 LANGUAGE subjects (Hindi 5 · English 3 · Marathi 3) — a product-scope question, not just work** (unseen-passage comprehension, grammar transformation, letter/essay writing) — plus the **2026-07-29 exclusion: the only 2023 Social Sciences PDFs are MARATHI-medium prints** (`N 964` GEOGRAPHY PAPER-II **(M)**, `N 956` PAPER-I **(M)**); a translation must never be ingested because `content_hash` is stem-derived and translated rows can never dedup against the real English paper. **Per-chapter handouts:** `scripts/mh-ssc-10/build-chapter-pyq.ts` writes a `<chapter>_PYQ.docx` + `_PYQ_Key.docx` pair into each textbook chapter folder via the same `buildQuestionPaper`/`buildAnswerKey` path as a `/browse` download — **56 chapters / 112 files / 1,459 q** (registry in `chapter-pyq.ts`, current-syllabus chapters only). **TEXTBOOK layer added 2026-08-02 — a SECOND pipeline `scripts/mh-ssc-10-text/` writing into the SAME exam and the SAME chapters**: the Balbharati Class-10 textbook exercises as `question_kind='practice'`, so a chapter now carries its board PYQs and its textbook exercises together and the `/browse` PYQ/Practice toggle separates them. **5 History + Political Science chapters / 60 q PUBLIC** (Historiography: Development in the West · Historiography: Indian Tradition · Applied History · Working of the Constitution · The Electoral Process — 12 q each, 12 MCQ + 48 subjective, 2 concept-map figures). Born-digital text layer ⇒ **text-first** (`dump-text.ts`), not vision. Two rules earned here: `chapterName` must match the DB row EXACTLY (the book prints "Historiography : Development in the West" with a space before the colon and the DB has none — the book's spelling would auto-create a duplicate chapter and split the corpus), and **subtopics are the TEXTBOOK's own section headings** (user's call) with the six drifted PYQ-era subtopics renamed to match, so PYQ and practice rows share one axis per chapter. Like the Class-9 humanities book this one ships **NO answers section at all**, so the step-6 cross-check cannot run — same compensating regime (chapter-grounded authoring · all 12 MCQ keys blind-re-derived, 0 mismatches · `audit-grounding.ts` · `derived_model`/`derived_at` + `pyq_note` stamped on all 60). See [[mh-ssc-10-ingestion]]) + **Maharashtra State Board Class 9** (exam `mh-sb-9`, id `2030d309-…` — the Balbharati **Class 9** textbooks; **practice-only + boardExam** (Class 9 is not a board year, so there are no PYQs ever, unlike mh-ssc-10). Own `scripts/mh-sb-9/` pipeline whose `lib.ts` re-exports the `stateboard` pure core VERBATIM; whole-book PDFs so every chapter sets a 0-based `pages` range. **491 q PUBLIC across 4 subjects** + **Geography 217 — the whole 12-chapter `9th_Geog_SB.pdf` COMPLETE** (2026-08-02; Endogenetic 26 · Trade 23 · Urbanisation 22 · Distributional Maps 20 · Precipitation 20 · Sea Water 19 · IDL 18 · Tourism 18 · Exogenetic-1 16 · Exogenetic-2 16 · Transport 11 · Economics 8). Structurally the HARDEST of the three books: **only 5 of its 217 questions are four-option MCQs** (all in Ch.7), so the blind re-derivation that anchors History/PolSci barely applies and the grounding audit carries the weight; two "tick the correct option" blocks print just THREE choices and ship as free-response with the choices in the stem (no invented fourth, no change to the shared MCQ core). All 5 MCQ keys blind-re-derived, 0 mismatches. **18 figures**, each crop verified individually — and Geography forced two durable fixes: (1) **a question's figure set can span a page break** (Precipitation's Q2/Q3 read figures (A) on printed p48 and (B),(C) overleaf), so `attach-images.ts` now accepts a pre-made **`file`** in the manifest and those six rows share one stitched composite; (2) **near-full-page colour art blows the 1 MB storage cap** — the landform panorama rendered at 7.2 MB and the upload simply threw, so the size-aware step-down-then-JPEG crop was ported from `scripts/mh-ssc-10` (7.2 MB → 458 KB). Where the book is thin the answers SAY so: Transport names no airport, port or highway number so 8 of its 11 rows carry a `[Note:]`; Tourism's Q2 matching chain is unanswerable from the book (fig 12.1 checked at zoom prints NO State names), so 28 of 30 cells stay unsettled with the student redirected to an atlas — the chapter's own printed instruction. Also: **Mathematics 78** (Ch.1 Sets, 2026-07-17 — 68 subjective + 10 MCQ, 5 subtopics, 8 Venn figures re-cropped through snapCrop after 7 broken-image reports; the Maths books DO ship an ANSWERS section so the step-6 cross-check ran, catching a figure-transcription error + 2 book-key errata) + **History 117 + Political Science 79 — the combined `9th_Hist_SB.pdf` is COMPLETE, all 16 chapters** (2026-08-01, in two passes: 5 chapters/58 q then the remaining 11/138 q; 40 MCQ + 156 subjective. History ch.1-10 = Sources of History 8 · Events after 1960 12 · Internal Challenges 10 · Economic Development 11 · Education 16 · Empowerment of Women 10 · Science and Technology 15 · Industry and Trade 14 · Changing Life 1 13 · Changing Life 2 8; PolSci ch.1-6 = Post World War Political Developments 14 · India's Foreign Policy 14 · Defence System 12 · The United Nations 15 · India and Other Countries 14 · International Problems 10. 5 figures — a Foreign-Policy concept map, a Science-and-Technology timeline, the UN chronology + tree diagram, and an environmental-degradation concept map. **139 transcribed, 138 committed**: International Problems Q2(2) is a verbatim repeat of India and Other Countries Q2(2) — same statement, same instruction, a book editing slip — and `content_hash` dedup correctly dropped it). **The History/PolSci book ships NO answers section at all — the FIRST State Board source with none — so the mandatory step-6 answer-key cross-check gate cannot run**; compensating controls are answers authored strictly from the chapter's own prose, all 40 MCQ keys independently blind-re-derived (0 mismatches across both passes), `derived_model`/`derived_at` + a `pyq_note` clause stamped on all 196 rows (`reviewFlag` in the sibling pipelines is only a build-time console message and persists nothing — see [[verify-flag-persists]]), and a purpose-built **`audit-grounding.ts`** standing gate: it pulls every year and proper noun out of each stored answer and diffs them against that chapter's text, because with no key the failure mode is not a wrong answer but a *fluent invention* no reader would question. Across all 196 rows it found exactly ONE genuine unsourced claim (an answer citing India's 2005 RTI Act, absent from the chapter) — removed. It is TRIAGE, not proof: it is blind to an invention phrased in lowercase, and the script says so. Its two reusable contracts, `HUMANITIES_BRIEF.md` (transcription) and `HUMANITIES_SOLUTION_BRIEF.md` (authoring), carry the rules forward. Extraction there is **text-first, not vision** — the prose text layer is clean and carries no math, unlike the Maths books whose unicode math flattens to `�` — hence `dump-text.ts`, which writes `out/<id>.text.md` as a SIBLING of the render dir because `render.ts` rmSync's `out/<id>/`. Chapter-end "Projects" + inline activity boxes are deliberately NOT ingested (open-ended, no determinate answer). `/board` outlines are a single `exercise` block per humanities chapter — that book has no solved-example block. See [[mh-sb-9-ingestion]]).

**The end-of-book answer-key cross-check is now a mandatory workflow GATE (step 6 in `scripts/stateboard/README.md`)**: every chapter is diffed against the Balbharati ANSWERS section, our authoring errors fixed + book-key errors flagged with `[Textbook…]` brackets → aggregated by `errata.ts` (**151 items across 12 chapters** — the Balbharati key is wrong ~4× as often as our authored answers; Ch.3 Indefinite Integration alone added 28 answer-key errors, the dirtiest single-chapter key yet). **The cross-check's trustworthiness rests on a THIRD independent ground truth** (mpmath/sympy differentiation of the actual stem, testing BOTH our answer and the book's against it — comparing the two to each other lets a shared error hide), and on the rule that **`simplify()` returning non-zero is NOT evidence of inequality** (constraint-curve identities) — extended 2026-07-16: **a sympy `solve`/`solveset` MISS is not evidence either** (on App-of-Derivatives it returned no roots in (0,2π) for a trig row and dropped 5π/4 on another — principal-branch artifacts that would have manufactured 2 bogus OUR-ANSWER-WRONG findings; test the claimed value directly + scan numerically. A CAS returning "nothing" is a question, never a verdict); ~20 apparent disagreements per chapter dissolve on equivalence testing. Subjective answers authored via parallel agents → `apply-solutions.ts` (hash-safe); `scripts/stateboard/` hybrid text+vision pipeline — see [[state-board-ingestion]]); IPMAT, CUET on the roadmap.

**Board textbook-solutions reader (`/board`) shipped (2026-07-06):** a book-faithful, exercise-by-exercise reader for school-board textbook exams (State Board + CBSE Class 12 today) — each chapter renders in **physical book order** (§ Solved Examples → Exercise → Miscellaneous → future PYQs), a distinct **book-structure axis** (`section_kind` enum `solved_example|exercise|miscellaneous` + `section_group`/`section_label`/`section_seq`, migration 0043) that is ORTHOGONAL to the conceptual `subtopic` used by `/browse`+`/notes` — a single book Exercise is deliberately split across subtopics, so the reader CANNOT group by subtopic without shattering it. Per-chapter **PDF-verified** outlines in `scripts/stateboard/sections.ts` → `assignSections` (pure, TDD) → `backfill-sections.ts` → `npm run board:lint` (gate: section completeness + `section_seq` contiguity per chapter). Routes `/board` → `/board/[examSlug]` → `/board/[examSlug]/[subjectRoute]/[chapterSlug]`; data layer `src/lib/board/query.ts`, client reader `src/app/board/BoardReader.tsx` (collapsible section/block accordion, attempt-first per-question reveal, `breakSentences` sentence-per-line, answers rendered via `BlockText`). Always-visible **Board** nav tab (like Notes; `boardExam` flag on the exam registry). A new chapter appears automatically once its rows carry `section_seq` — the surface is data-driven, no per-chapter code. See the 2026-07-06 Decisions log + [[board-reader]].

**Timed PYQ mock tests (`/mock`) shipped (2026-07-10; NEET added 2026-07-17):** real past papers served whole as **timed, auto-graded, online** tests — NOT blueprint-sampled mixes ("use PYQPs as is"). A mock = the exact PYQs of one sitting, reconstructed from the bank, delivered NATIVE on pyqvault.com. **44 mocks PUBLIC**: 18 NDA Paper I Mathematics (120 q · 300 marks · +2.5/−0.83) + 18 NDA Paper II GAT (150 q · 600 marks · +4/−1.33; English 50 + General-Knowledge 100 across 8 subjects), every NDA sitting 2017→2026, 150 min each; **+ 8 NEET (UG)** (2021·2022·2023·2024·Re-2024·2025·2026·Re-2026 — Physics/Chemistry/Biology(=Botany+Zoology), +4/−1; 180 q/180 min for 2025+, 200 q/200 min pre-2025; see the 2026-07-17 Decisions entry). Migration 0044 = `mock_tests` (immutable ordered question-REF snapshot + exam-pattern config, deterministic `slugToUuid` id, service-role writes) + `mock_attempts` (per-user own-row RLS, retakes = multiple rows, partial-unique = one in-progress per mock) + `attempt_answers`. Pure core `src/lib/mocks/` (`blueprints` registry · `reconstruct` paper→snapshot · `attempt` grade+timer+palette · `perf` aggregation — all TDD; **generalized for NEET 2026-07-17**: optional per-section counts (soft-count), an edition-aware slug (`neet-2024` vs `neet-2024-re`), a per-question `grace` flag (officially dropped/bonus → full marks to all, no penalty), and per-sitting include-overrides — NDA path byte-identical); `service.ts` = attempt lifecycle; `MockRunner` (refresh-resistant timer → auto-submit · question palette · flag · autosave · resume) + results/review reuse the `/browse` KaTeX/BlockText render path (grace rows show an amber "awarded to all" disclosure). Exam-gated **"Mocks"** nav tab (`hasMocks` flag on NDA + NEET). Admin **`/dashboard/mocks`** (per-mock performance → drill-down to every attempt) + **`/dashboard/students`** (self-serve signup roster). Built by `scripts/mocks/build.ts` (NDA year+month discovery loop + a NEET `source_file`-keyed sitting registry, self-discovering, idempotent). See the 2026-07-10 + 2026-07-17 Decisions log + [[mock-tests]].

## Stack

- **Next.js 14** App Router · TypeScript · Tailwind 3 · shadcn-style UI primitives
- **Supabase** Auth + Postgres + RLS, accessed via `@supabase/supabase-js` (no Prisma, no ORM)
- **Vitest 2** for tests · `tsx` for scripts
- **Word export:** `temml` (LaTeX → MathML) → `mathml2omml` → `docx` + `jszip`
- **Browser math preview:** `katex` + `react-katex`
- **Deploy:** Vercel, git-integrated

## Multi-tenancy + visibility model

**Platform brand vs. tenant org-name — do NOT conflate (and don't "purge" `"LWS Pune"`).** The public brand is **PYQ Vault** (`SITE_NAME` in `layout.tsx`; Footer, JSON-LD publisher, all anon/student-facing copy) — that's what everyone sees. Separately, **`organizations.name` is tenant data**, and `"LWS Pune"` is the **founding org customer (tenant #1)** that owns the seeded content. An org name is rendered ONLY on staff-gated surfaces (AppHeader, `/dashboard/*`, `/uploads`, `/account`, `/papers`) — always behind a `member` (`org_members` row), so **anon + self-serve students never see it**. When a second school onboards, they get their own org row and *their* staff see *their* name; nobody's org name is ever the public brand. Consequently the `"LWS Pune"` string in `api/sync/mock/route.ts` (`DESTINATION_ORG_NAME`), `tests/global-teardown-helpers.ts` (`REAL_ORG_NAMES`), the DB-integration tests' `.eq("name","LWS Pune")`, and scripts' `sourceFile`/`pyqNote` provenance are all **correct as-is** — legitimate tenant identity + attribution, NOT stale branding. A public-facing `LWS` literal (hardcoded in JSX/copy, not read from the org row) IS a branding bug → use PYQ Vault / "our team" (the 2026-07-12 sweep already fixed the known set). Rule of thumb: reads from `organizations.name` / routes by org identity → keep; hardcoded brand string shown to the public → PYQ Vault.

Per-school orgs, RLS-enforced. **Role model overhauled 2026-07-15 (migrations 0055–0057) — content editing collapsed to superadmin-only; teachers became branch-scoped paper-builders. The old "ADMIN does everything / TEACHER edits content" (migration 0025) is SUPERSEDED — see [[project-member-management]].**
- **Service role** (seed + ingestion scripts, server-only admin actions) bypasses RLS by design — never expose to client code. **The primary content add/edit path** — the whole bank is built here.
- **SUPERADMIN** (platform staff above orgs — `platform_admins` table, migration 0056; seeded = `connect.lwspune@gmail.com`) — the ONLY identity that can add/edit question CONTENT (questions/options/taxonomy/tags/embeddings/uploads), across ALL orgs. Cross-org console at `/superadmin` (list orgs · onboard a new org · provision its admins/teachers). **Also the ONLY identity that sees the platform-wide `/dashboard` surfaces** (students · entitlements · feedback · activity · mocks · quizzes · leads) — those read via the service-role client with no `org_id` filter, so they're cross-tenant by construction and were superadmin-gated 2026-07-20 (see the Decisions log). NOT an `org_members` row; detected via `getSessionSuperadmin()`/`requireSuperadmin()` (service-role read of `platform_admins`).
- **ADMIN** — own-org *management*: create branches + teachers, assign teachers to branches, build papers, triage reports, manage members. **Cannot add/edit content** (superadmin-only since 0056), and **cannot see the platform-wide dashboard surfaces above** (entitlements/students/feedback/activity/mocks/quizzes/leads are superadmin-only — 2026-07-20). The org-scoped dashboard cards an admin DOES get: Members, Branches, Batches, Papers, Question reports, Concept reports.
- **TEACHER** — assigned branch(es) ONLY: read the bank + build papers/batches for their branches (with the per-batch no-repeat warning). Branch-scoped via `branch_members` (0057). **Cannot edit content**, manage members, or manage branches.
- **anon** (no JWT) can read PUBLIC questions/options + all taxonomy. Cannot write anything.
- **STUDENT** (signed-in, self-serve, **no `org_members` row** — added 2026-06-01) registers at `/signup` (Google OAuth or email/password). By RLS construction they get zero private data; what an account unlocks is in-app question reports today + premium content via the entitlement axis below. Org-less = student (admins always get an `org_members` row atomically), so `/dashboard` redirects them to `/browse`.

**Cohorts: Org → Branch → Batch (migrations 0054/0055).** A **branch** (`branches`, admin-managed) is a campus (e.g. "FC Road"); a **batch** (`batches`) is a cohort ("NDA 2026 Morning") filed under a branch; a **paper** optionally targets a batch. The point of batches: the paper builder soft-warns when a question was already used for the SAME batch (per-batch non-repetition, derived from `paper_questions` joined to `papers.batch_id`; scope = per-batch, enforcement = soft-warn). Teachers are scoped to their `branch_members` branches for batches/papers; admins see all org papers; collaboration is branch-mediated. See [[project-member-management]].

**Premium entitlement axis (orthogonal to orgs, migration 0026).** Paid/comped access lives in `public.entitlements`, keyed on `auth.users(id)` — NOT org-scoped, because a student has no org. A grant unlocks a `scope` ('all' = full premium); `source` ∈ `razorpay | comp | manual` (the gate is **source-agnostic** — `userHasAccess` just asks "active entitlement for this scope?"; staff/org-members bypass the paywall). Writes are **service-role only** (no write RLS); reads are own-row only. Consumers: the `/notes` preview-gate (`tier:"paid"` chapters), `/pricing`/`/account`, the Razorpay webhook, and the `/dashboard/entitlements` comp UI. See the 2026-06-01 Decisions log entry + [[project-paywall-build]].

Each question has a `visibility` enum (`PUBLIC | PRIVATE`, default **PUBLIC** as of migration 0022 / 2026-05-17 — was PRIVATE prior). Read RLS is the union of two permissive policies: `(visibility = 'PUBLIC')` for everyone, plus `(org_id = private.current_user_org_id())` for authenticated org members on their own org's PRIVATE rows. Authenticated users see PUBLIC across all orgs + their own PRIVATE; anon sees only PUBLIC. Admins flip per-question visibility from the edit page.

Each question also has a **`question_kind` enum** (`pyq | practice`, default `pyq`, migration 0036) — the axis that separates the past-year corpus from the supplementary **practice** bank (the product is PYQ-first; **3,040 practice q live across 29 NDA Maths chapters as of 2026-06-13 — ALL 5 source folders ingested (Algebra + Trigonometry + Calculus + 2D Geometry + 3D Geometry; the 3D folder fed both the Vectors and 3D Geometry chapters)**, separate from the PYQ counts above). `/browse` exposes it as a **PYQ / Practice / All** toggle (default PYQ; `kind` filter in [filters.ts](src/lib/questions/filters.ts) → `query.ts` + `p_kind` on the facet RPCs). PYQ-only surfaces stay clean two ways: **tag-gated** surfaces (guide principles, notes drills, worked examples, quiz harvest) are PYQ-only *by construction* (practice questions are never principle/concept-tagged — doing so would leak them in), while the four **raw count-by-taxonomy** queries (`examHomeStats` + the 3 notes landings) carry an explicit `question_kind='pyq'` guard. Practice ingestion is its own pipeline + runbook — see [[practice-ingestion]] + `scripts/practice/README.md`.

Each question also has a **`question_format` enum** (`mcq | subjective | numeric`, default `mcq`, migrations 0041 + 0061) — the axis separating standard MCQs (4 options, exactly one `is_correct`) from **subjective/free-response** questions (State Board textbook content) and **numeric (NAT)** questions (JEE Section B — no options, integer/decimal answer in `questions.numeric_answer`, hashed via `numericContentHash`), the latter two carrying **zero option rows** (subjective's model answer lives in `questions.solution`). Subjective support: `subjectiveContentHash(stem, context)` in [hash.ts](src/lib/upload/hash.ts) (namespaced so it can't collide with an MCQ; context-aware so set-based siblings with the same bare sub-item text don't dedup-collide; excludes `solution` so answer-backfill doesn't change the id); `commitStaged` writes `question_format` + emits no options for subjective; `/browse` QuestionCard renders subjective as stem + "Show model answer" (no A–D); the Word answer-key prints the model answer instead of `(?)`. `question_format` is optional on the `QuestionRow` view-model (absent ⇒ MCQ). **Deferred (non-critical — no subjective content reaches them by construction):** a `/browse` format-filter toggle, subjective in the admin edit *form* (answers are backfilled by script, not the UI), and quiz-harvest / RAG one-correct-option guards. See [[state-board-ingestion]].

RLS helpers live in the **`private` schema** (not exposed by PostgREST): `current_user_org_id()`, `current_user_is_admin()`, `current_user_is_superadmin()` (0056), `current_user_can_edit_content()` (0056), `current_user_can_edit_questions()`, `current_user_branch_ids()` (0057). **The semantic split matters (0056):** `can_edit_content()` = **superadmin-only**, gates all CONTENT writes (questions/options/taxonomy/tags/embeddings/uploads); `can_edit_questions()` is UNCHANGED (admin OR teacher) and now gates **paper/batch building** ONLY (papers/batches/paper_questions), NOT content — do not conflate them (redefining `can_edit_questions` would break the paper builder). Use `current_user_is_admin()` for org-management (branches, members, reports triage, entitlements); `current_user_branch_ids()` scopes a teacher's batches/papers to their assigned branches.

## File layout

The full file-by-file map — the `src/` tree (routes, components, lib helpers), `supabase/` migrations + seed, `scripts/`, and `tests/` — lives in **[ARCHITECTURE.md](ARCHITECTURE.md)**, extracted to keep this file's always-loaded payload lean. **Append a new file/component/route there, not here**; update the high-level shape below only when the top-level structure changes.

High-level shape:
- `src/app/` — Next.js App Router pages: `/browse` (paper builder), `/questions` (**cacheable, indexable per-chapter landing pages** — `/questions/<examSlug>/<subjectSlug>/<chapterSlug>`, 317 of them, derived from the taxonomy; the SEO + caching counterpart to the dynamic `/browse`), `/guide` (10 NDA strategy guides), `/notes` (cross-exam index → `/notes/<examSlug>` per-exam hubs → 20 teaching-notes chapters), `/board` (book-faithful textbook-solutions reader for board exams), `/mock` (timed PYQ mock tests — catalogue · instructions · runner · results), `/dashboard` (admin, incl. `/dashboard/syllabus` the **cross-book syllabus map** + `/dashboard/mocks` performance + `/dashboard/students` roster + `/dashboard/activity` usage-shape + `/dashboard/branches` campus CRUD + `/dashboard/batches` cohort CRUD + `/dashboard/members` incl. teacher↔branch assignment), `/superadmin` (superadmin-only cross-org console — list/onboard orgs + provision admins), `/upload`, `/signup`·`/login`·`/pricing`·`/account`·`/saved` (bookmarked questions), plus the `/api/*` route handlers.
- `src/lib/` — server/pure helpers by domain: `supabase/`, `auth*` (+ `isSuperadmin`/`requireSuperadmin`/`getSessionSuperadmin`), `questions/`, `guide/`, `notes/`, `links/`, `export/` (Word/OMML), `branches/` + `batches/` (cohort model: types + pure validate/split + service-role admin), `members/` (+ `setMemberBranches`), `superadmin/` (cross-org `listOrgsWithStats`/`createOrg`), `papers/` (collaborative builder + per-batch `usage` soft-warn), `entitlements/`, `billing/`, `mocks/` (timed mock tests: blueprints · reconstruct · grade/timer/palette · attempt service · perf/adminStats), `syllabus/` (the **cross-book syllabus map** — `summary.ts` pure core: `buildAlignmentRows` (three-book crosswalk, authored-pairings-only) · `dominantSbByChapter`/`sbBookOrder` (order an exam's chapters along a book) · `rollUpChapterStatus`/`tallyByExam` · `SPINE`/`BOOK_OF_EXAM` · `examOfSpine`/`isExamSpine` ("NDA bank taxonomy" → "NDA", anchored to the END so a book spine passes through unchanged); `query.ts` loaders — `loadSyllabusMatrix`/`loadMappingRows`/`loadAlignmentRows`/`loadExamSpineSummaries`/`loadNcertGaps`/`loadOldSyllabusByExam`, each scoped by **BOTH `source` and `subject`** — source alone merges two subjects' Ch.1, subject alone merges three books' Ch.1, and every spine numbers chapters from 1), `relevance/` (the **cross-exam syllabus-fit screen** — `config.ts` holds the audience, the blocking-tool list and the `REVIEWED_CHAPTERS` scope pinned **by chapter UUID** so a rename can't silently rot it; `fit.ts` is the pure core — `parseFit`/`isFitExam`/`fitCoverage`), `activity/` (the **engagement spine**: `events` pure core + `service` best-effort writer + `shape`/`classifyUsageShape` + `adminStats` — the A3 usage-shape readout), `students/` (signup roster), `reports/`+`notes-reports/`, `upload/`, `tags/`, `quiz/` (the **Quiz-Factory core**: `atoms`/`daily`/`quizPayload`/`stemLint`/`verdict`/`coverage` pure helpers + `assemble.ts` shared `assembleNextQuiz` (snapshots questions onto the quiz row) + `deleteQuiz.ts` (delete + propagate to nda-tracker) + `admin.ts` dashboard reads — the `scripts/quiz/` CLIs import these; `coverage.ts` powers `quiz:coverage`).
- `src/components/` — `AppHeader`/nav, `math/` renderers (`KatexRenderer`·`RichText`·`BlockText`·`parseTableBlocks`), shadcn `ui/` primitives.
- `supabase/migrations/` — `0001…0066` (append-only; **0066 = `syllabus_concept_exams.covered_by`** — WHERE a concept is covered, not just whether: comma-separated target section refs (`"11.4, 11.5"`, `"XI:2.5, XII:1.2"` — the `XI:`/`XII:` prefix marks a cross-YEAR mapping, the common case). Free text, never joined on in SQL; resolved to titles in `src/lib/syllabus/query.ts` against whichever book the row's `exam` names. *Applied via MCP 2026-08-02; the .sql file was missing and was recreated 2026-08-03 — see the Decisions entry.* **0065 = `syllabus_concepts` + `syllabus_concept_exams`** — the syllabus-map spine: one row per section of a BOOK (`source` = 'MH State Board' | 'NCERT') or per subtopic of an EXAM bank taxonomy (`source` = '<exam> bank taxonomy'), plus a per-(concept, exam) `status` (`full`|`partial`|`not`) + `note`. **`source` is load-bearing**: every spine uses subject 'Chemistry' and numbers chapters from 1, so a query filtered by subject alone silently merges three different books. **0064 = public chapter index** (the `/questions` landing pages); **0063 = written_papers**; **0062 = `question_audience_exclusions`** — the cross-exam **syllabus-fit screen**: one row per JEE question a student taught the NDA ∪ CET syllabus CANNOT solve, with `blocking_tool` naming the out-of-syllabus technique. Deliberately an EXCLUSION list, not a verdict-per-question table (~95% of JEE questions pass, so only the failures carry information); the *reviewed scope* — which chapters have been adjudicated at all — lives in `src/lib/relevance/config.ts`, **NOT** the DB, so "passed" and "never looked at" stay distinguishable. Read open (it's a public `/browse` control over already-PUBLIC questions), writes superadmin-only per 0056. See [[syllabus-fit-screen]]; **0061 = numeric (NAT) answer format** — adds `numeric` to the `question_format` enum + `questions.numeric_answer` (nullable) for JEE Section-B numeric-answer questions (no options; `numericContentHash` dedup key); `/browse` QuestionCard + docx answer-key render the numeric answer; **0060 = `teacher_access_requests`** — public "request teacher access" leads (name/institute/email/mobile/city/message/consent/status), RLS-enabled with NO policies → service-role-only (the `platform_admins`/`entitlements` locked pattern); written by `POST /api/teacher-access`, triaged at `/superadmin`; **0059 = email**; **0058 = `paper_questions` branch-scope** — replace `paper_questions_write_editor` with `paper_questions_write_scoped` (the parent paper must be accessible: admin OR own OR the paper's batch's branch ∈ mine), mirroring `papers_update_scoped` — closes the 0057 blind-write gap; **0057 = `branch_members`** — teacher↔branch junction (PK pair, RLS read=org-member/write=org-admin) + `private.current_user_branch_ids()` + rewrite of the batches/papers SELECT/UPDATE (+batches INSERT) policies to branch-scope teachers (is_admin OR own OR branch∈mine); **0056 = superadmin content lockdown** — `platform_admins` (locked RLS-on-no-policy lookup table, seeded `connect.lwspune@gmail.com`) + `current_user_is_superadmin()`/`current_user_can_edit_content()` helpers + repoint EVERY content-write policy (questions/options/chapters/subtopics/upload_jobs/embeddings/*_tags) to superadmin-only cross-org; **0055 = branches** (first-class campus entity, admin-managed) + `batches.branch_id` FK (drops the 0054 free-text `branch` label); **0054 = batches** (cohort under a branch; `papers.batch_id`) for per-batch paper non-repetition — see [[project-member-management]]; 0053 = **`get_activity_shape(p_days)` RPC** — the A3 usage-shape aggregate over `user_activity` (by-kind volume · active-users · per-user distinct-active-DAYS + gap distribution/median · recency · daily-active), computed in SQL so it's cap-safe past 1000 rows (the `get_dashboard_stats` idiom); `SECURITY DEFINER` + `search_path=''` + EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC/anon/authenticated, granted service_role-only (reads across all own-row-RLS activity → admin-only); consumed by the admin `/dashboard/activity` readout via `src/lib/activity/adminStats.ts` + the pure `classifyUsageShape` (daily vs burst → streak-vs-goal-progress call); 0052 = **user_activity** — the engagement activity spine: one **append-only** fact per signed-in student action (`kind` CHECK'd to a learning-anchored allowlist — mock_submitted · answer_wrong/correct · chapter_mastered · note_checkpoint · question_bookmarked · quiz_taken · drill_completed; NO vanity kinds), own-row **SELECT + INSERT only** (no update/delete = immutable history), `metadata jsonb` for extensible payloads, + a full `UNIQUE(dedupe_key)` making the backfill idempotent (NULLs-distinct so live events aren't deduped). The substrate every later engagement mechanic reads from — see the **Engagement engine** section + [[project-engagement-engine]]; 0051 = **user_feedback** — append-only "voice of user" (NPS `kind='nps'` score 0–10 + feature-request `kind='feature'` message), own-row RLS insert/select + coherence/score CHECKs; powers the engagement-gated NPS + feature form on `/me` and the `/dashboard/feedback` rollup (Phase 4) — see [[project-progressive-profile]]; 0050 = **whatsapp opt-in + `mock_feedback`** — `student_profiles.whatsapp_opt_in`/`whatsapp_prompted_at` (CAPTURE-ONLY, no dispatch) + a `mock_feedback` table (one row per attempt, own-row RLS + parent-attempt EXISTS check, difficulty rating CHECK); Phase 3 of the progressive profile — see [[project-progressive-profile]]; 0049 = **student_profiles detail fields** — `medium`/`academic_stream`/`city`/`goal` (all nullable, enum + length CHECKs) powering the never-gated `/account` profile completion meter (Phase 2); 0048 = **student_profiles onboarding fields** — `target_exams text[]`/`stage`/`onboarded_at` (+ `mobile` dropped NOT NULL) for the `/welcome` post-signup intent capture (Phase 1); 0047 = **question_bookmarks** — per-student saved questions, own-row RLS (select/insert/delete), **real FK** `question_id → questions(id)` cascade (unlike notes' soft slug refs); powers the `/browse` bookmark button + `/saved` page via the app-wide `BookmarksProvider`; see [[project-saved-and-reveal-meter]]; 0046 = **notes_progress** — per-student `/notes` track layer (bookmark · mark-mastered · latest checkpoint self-score · last_viewed) keyed on `auth.users(id)` + the editorial `subtopic_slug` (denormalized `chapter_slug`/`subject_route` — notes are TS modules, no FK), RLS **own-row** all verbs (student writes via JWT), CHECK on checkpoint coherence; see [[project-notes-signin]]; 0045 = **student_profiles** — per-student post-signup contact mobile keyed on `auth.users(id)`, RLS **own-row** SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE (student writes their own row via JWT); powers the mock-result phone-capture gate — see [[project-student-mobile-capture]]; 0044 = **timed mock tests** — `mock_tests` (immutable ordered question-ref snapshot + exam-pattern config; service-role writes, published readable by all) + `mock_attempts` (per-user own-row RLS, retakes, partial-unique one-in-progress-per-mock) + `attempt_answers` (parent-attempt EXISTS RLS); see [[mock-tests]]; 0043 = **book-structure section columns** for the `/board` reader — `section_kind` enum (`solved_example`|`exercise`|`miscellaneous`) + `section_group`/`section_label`/`section_seq` (all nullable; only board/textbook rows carry them); orthogonal to the conceptual `subtopic` axis — see [[board-reader]]; 0042 = **`questions.solution_image_url`** (nullable) — an optional per-question SOLUTION diagram, shown in the `/browse` "model answer" reveal + the docx answer-key solution block only (never the question paper); reuses the existing `image_url` storage/render path; 0041 = **`question_format` enum** (`mcq`|`subjective`) for subjective/free-response questions (State Board textbook content — zero options, answer in `solution`); 0040 = **RAG grounding layer** — `pgvector` (extensions schema) + `questions.plain_text`/`solution_json`/`derived_model`/`derived_at` + generalized `embeddings` table (unsized `vector`, no index) + `match_chunks` RPC (JOIN-guarded PUBLIC+pyq); see the 2026-06-19 Decisions entry + [[rag-grounding-layer]]; 0039 = **collaborative paper builder** — `papers` + `paper_questions` junction, org-scoped RLS, finalize-snapshot; 0038 = **per-exam dedup** — swap the `questions` unique key from `(org_id, content_hash)` to `(org_id, exam_id, content_hash)` so CDS↔NDA shared UPSC questions keep both copies; 0029 = `subtopics.order_index` for /notes teaching order; 0030 = `quiz_atoms` Quiz-Factory pool; 0031 = `quizzes` + `quiz_atoms_map`; 0032 = `quiz_atoms.theme`; 0033 = `quizzes.public_slug` + `quiz_leads` (public-quiz funnel); 0034 = `record_quiz_lead` retake-aware upsert RPC; 0035 = `quizzes.questions` jsonb + `theme` (immutable quiz snapshot — display decoupled from the live atom pool); 0036 = `questions.question_kind` enum (`pyq`|`practice`); 0037 = `p_kind` on the facet RPCs for the /browse PYQ/Practice toggle). `scripts/` — seeds, `notes-lint`, `notes-latex-audit`, `notes:order` sync, `jee/` ingestion, `practice/` (NDA practice-question ingestion — `render` → vision-transcribe → `commit` → `preview`; runbook in `scripts/practice/README.md`, pure core in `lib.ts`), `quiz/` (Daily Quiz Factory CLI runners — `quiz:harvest` → `quiz:sync` → `quiz:verify` → `quiz:lint` → `quiz:assemble` → `quiz:delete` → `quiz:push`; pure core lives in `src/lib/quiz/`), `syllabus/` (the syllabus-map pipeline + its probes. **Spines:** `ingest-*-spine.ts` load a book/exam spine (exam spines PRUNE: section numbers are positional, so removing a subtopic strands the last as a ghost) — `ingest-bank-spine.ts` deliberately writes concepts with **NO link rows**, so an unruled subtopic stays genuinely unassessed. It is the ONLY exam-spine path: its predecessor `ingest-exam-spine.ts` defaulted an unruled subtopic to `full` and was **retired 2026-08-04** once that default was found to have shipped as a false verdict for 169 subtopics. **Rulings** are authored as DATA in `data/<subject>-<exam>-rulings.json` and written by `commit-bank-rulings.ts` under FOUR guards — section_no exists · the recorded `subtopic` still matches the spine (positional refs shift) · every `coveredBy` ref resolves to a live section of the named book · note ≤500 chars, mirroring the DB CHECK so the dry run catches it rather than the INSERT. **Evidence:** `dump_{chem,physics}_corpus.py` build a PER-CHAPTER book corpus and `search_corpus.py` searches it (prefix-anchored by default — see [[corpus-search-for-coverage-rulings]]); `dump-sections-handout.ts` emits the citable-section list from the LIVE spine so it cannot drift; `RULING_BRIEF.md` is the contract agents author to. **Probes:** `audit-alignment.ts` re-measures the inferred-vs-authored pairing split and FAILS if any paired row loses its backing, `audit-spine.ts` finds sub-section holes + prose-swallowed titles, `audit-directions.ts` asserts no `status='not'` row is pointed at by a live exam subtopic, `smoke-page-data.ts` drives the shipped page's own loaders (the page is auth-gated `ƒ`, so a green build proves only that it compiles). **Exports:** `export-alignment.ts`/`export-chapter-map-{docx,xlsx}.ts` render the maps for teachers; `export-html.ts` is a prototype whose `rendercheck.js` executes the page's OWN emitted JS — it caught an empty table, a lost sort and a regex mangled by the template literal), `grounding/` (RAG grounding + key-audit pipeline — `backfill-plain-text` → `export-batch` → [blind agents] → `commit` → `apply-fix`; pure core `lib.ts`, runbook `README.md` — see [[rag-grounding-layer]]). `tests/` — Vitest unit + DB-integration. The `/dashboard/quizzes` admin page (read-only: pool stats + filterable list + Publish-to-public; assembly/delete are CLI-only) + `/dashboard/leads` (quiz-lead rollup) + the public `/quiz/[slug]` funnel are the UI over this.

## Commands

```sh
npm run dev                # next dev (local)
npm run build              # production build
npm run lint
npm run typecheck          # tsc --noEmit (covers test files too — keep this clean)
npm test                   # vitest run
npm run test:watch
npm run db:seed            # idempotent taxonomy seed (service-role)
npm run extract:taxonomy   # regenerate supabase/seed/taxonomy.json from a reference Excel
npm run notes:lint         # validate /notes editorial modules against live taxonomy + DB tags (read-only)
npm run notes:latex        # audit /notes for LaTeX-in-plain-text leaks + delimiter imbalance + unicode (read-only)
npm run notes:order        # populate subtopics.order_index from each /notes chapter's subtopicOrder (teaching order on /browse); --dry previews
npm run notes:coverage [subjectRoute] [chapterSlug]  # triage probe: math in a chapter's PUBLIC solutions but NOT in its notes (formula-gap candidates); default nda-maths statistics. Noisy — human-review list, not a gate
npm run tags:audit         # principle-tag audit (read-only)
npm run audit:underlines   # scan PUBLIC questions for \underline{...} segments not covered by the docx-export bypass
npm run audit:text [srcSubstr]  # standing probe for long-form text defects every other gate is blind to. FOUR classes: LITERAL_NEWLINE (a 2-char `\n` where a line break belongs — kills GFM pipe-tables on web AND in Word) + TABLE_NO_SEPARATOR (pipe rows with no `|---|`, so GFM refuses to build a table) + DROPPED_SYMBOL (a math zone opening with a bare `=` because the object it defines was lost upstream — `Let \(= I + adj(A)…\)`; anchored on the preceding FUNCTION word so `least count \(= 0.001\)` doesn't fire) + OPTION_LEAK (the option block never split off the stem — needs BOTH a label run AND ≥3 of the row's own option values echoed, else every "spot the error" GAT stem fires). Uses the REAL helpers (`normalizeNewlines`, `parseTableBlocks`) + pure probes in `scripts/lib/textProbes.ts` so a hit is a genuine disagreement, not a probe artefact. Triage, exits 0. Run after every ingest
npm run audit:omml         # scan the bank for math zones the docx exporter can't convert to OMML (→ raw-LaTeX fallback); report generated-papers/omml-sweep.md. Run after a new ingest — see [[docx-omml-export-pipeline]]
npm run audit:keys [filter]   # zero-LLM structural probe over PRACTICE MCQs: flags solution-concludes-a-different-letter-than-key + duplicate options + not-exactly-one-correct. Optional source_file substring filter. Triage (source-verify before flipping) — catches the vision-ingest option-scramble/dup class; see [[practice-pdf-vision-ingestion]]. JEE `pyq` variant: `npx tsx scripts/jee/audit-keys.ts [Subject] [srcSubstr]` (reuses the same `auditRow` core; ran clean 2026-07-25 — 0 wrong keys across 2,642 MCQs)
# — Daily Quiz Factory (see Decisions log 2026-06-08 + [[daily-quiz-pilot]] memory) —
npm run quiz:harvest [route/chapter ...]   # /notes ConceptUnits → MCQ atoms JSON (scripts/quiz/atoms/*.json); default 3 sample chapters
npm run quiz:sync          # upsert harvested atoms → public.quiz_atoms (staleness-aware: preserves verified, re-flags changed)
npm run quiz:verify [name] # promote needs_review atoms → 'verified' from scripts/quiz/verify/<name>.ts (Claude-authored distractors)
npm run quiz:lint [route] [chapter]   # flag quiz-atom stems that aren't self-contained (back-refs, criterion-less "which is correct?", fragments, generic auto-harvest "formula for <X>?" template) — paginates the full ready pool; triage, run before publishing a quiz public
npm run quiz:coverage [route] [chapter] [--review]   # flag formulas/traps TAUGHT in /notes prose but not harvestable (formula.latex empty / <12 traps) — the theme-coverage guardrail; triage, not a gate
npm run quiz:assemble <route> <chapter> [max] [size] [-- --theme=X]   # ready atoms → quiz → record (quizzes+map) → push nda-tracker draft
npm run quiz:delete <slug>   # delete an assembled quiz here + propagate the delete to nda-tracker (drafts only there)
npm run quiz:push [daily/<slug>]   # push a single DraftQuiz module to nda-tracker (default: the hand-authored classical quiz)
npm run stats              # report live bank size (PUBLIC by exam/subject) + taxonomy + /notes chapter & diagram counts — for verifying the header lines instead of hand-maintaining them
# — Outbound email (Resend; migration 0059). Runbook in OPERATIONS.md —
npm run email:preview -- --html   # render both templates (no DB, no key, no send) → generated-papers/email-preview.html
npm run email:send                # DRY RUN by default — prints who'd get what, writes NOTHING. Add `-- --apply` to send (npm needs the `--`), `-- --only=<email>`, `-- --limit=N`. Idempotent: a UNIQUE dedupe_key means a re-run can't double-send; re-run it after the 7-day cooldown and each next_mock student gets the next unattempted paper.
npm run board:lint         # /board section-structure gate: fails if a PUBLIC board (question_kind='practice') row lacks section_* fields or a chapter's section_seq isn't contiguous 1..N (scripts/stateboard/lint-sections.ts)
npm run db:types           # supabase gen types (requires SUPABASE_PROJECT_REF env)
npm run prepush            # the chained gate, MIRRORS CI: typecheck → lint → notes:lint → notes:latex → test → build; run by .githooks/pre-push
npm run gate [target]      # wrapper that runs `npm run <target>` (default prepush) and prints a GATE_RESULT: PASS/FAIL sentinel as its LAST line — so the result survives `| tail` even though a pipe masks the exit code
```

> **Pre-push gate.** `.githooks/pre-push` runs `npm run prepush` before any push — which now **mirrors CI exactly**: typecheck → lint → notes:lint → notes:latex → **test** → build (single source of truth: the `prepush` script). So a green pre-push means a green CI. Widened 2026-06-02 from the old build-only chain after a broken test passed pre-push and only failed in CI (it didn't run `npm test`). Cost: ~3–4 min (full suite + build). Activated by the `prepare` script (`git config core.hooksPath .githooks`) on `npm install`. `.gitattributes` pins LF on `.githooks/*` so the shebang resolves on Linux/WSL contributors. Escape hatch for WIP pushes: `git push --no-verify` — don't reach for it unless you know why.

> **CI gate.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs the **full** gate (typecheck → lint → `notes:lint` → `notes:latex` → `npm test` → build) on every push to `main` + every PR. The local pre-push hook now runs this same chain (see Pre-push gate above), so CI is a backstop rather than the first place these checks run — it still matters because it runs on a clean checkout with repo secrets even if someone `--no-verify`s. Requires three repo secrets (`NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`, `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`); without them the build + DB steps fail and the DB tests skip. Runs against the single prod Supabase project (test rows auto-swept by `global-teardown`) — point the secrets at a staging project if write traffic gets noisy. Vercel still owns deploys; CI is the correctness gate.

## Conventions specific to this project

- **Spacing scale.** Page wrappers `p-8`, content cards (CardContent default) `p-6`, compact cards (FilterBar etc.) `p-4`, nested option blocks `p-3`. Pick the smallest tier that contains breathing room — don't mix `p-4`/`p-6` in the same visual rhythm.
- **Typography.** UI text uses `font-sans` (Inter, default). Question/option/solution body text uses `font-serif` (Source Serif 4) — it's how teachers tell content apart from chrome. Both are loaded via `next/font` in `src/app/layout.tsx` and exposed as Tailwind utilities.
- **Color / brand (indigo, 2026-06-01).** The brand accent is **indigo** — chosen to unify with the `/notes` content palette. Three CSS vars in `globals.css`: `--brand` + `--brand-foreground` (fills — indigo-600 + white, AA-safe as a button bg in both themes) and `--brand-accent` (text/icon/border — indigo-600 light / indigo-400 dark, AA-legible as small text in both). `--ring` points at the brand so focus rings are branded. Tailwind exposes `bg-brand` / `text-brand-foreground` / `text-brand-accent` (+ a `brand` Button variant). **Application is SELECTIVE**: brand goes on chrome the eye lands on (primary CTAs, active nav, header mark, hero, stat numbers, table heatmap/peak-highlight, focus ring); near-black `--primary` stays the workhorse for secondary/admin buttons. **Off-limits:** the `/notes` + `/guide` *content* colors (concept-card indigo, amber traps, sky self-check, violet practice, emerald checkpoints) are content semantics — do NOT fold them into the brand token or recolor them. See [[brand-indigo-tokens]] + the 2026-06-01 Decisions-log entry.
- **Icons.** Use `lucide-react` for all icons. Never emoji or text glyphs (▾, ‹, ›) for UI affordances. Standard sizes: `h-3.5 w-3.5` for inline-with-text, `h-4 w-4` for buttons, `h-5 w-5` for the AppHeader logo.
- **Toasts.** `import { toast } from "sonner"`. Fire on every async user-initiated success or failure (save, upload commit, export download, sign-out). Setting an inline `error` state is fine, but never *replace* a toast with it — both should fire so the user can't miss the result while scrolled.
- **AppHeader.** Every page wraps its `<main>` in `<><AppHeader />…</>`. Don't re-add ad-hoc back-links, theme toggles, or sign-out buttons on individual pages — theme lives in the `ThemeToggle` island in the header (visible to anon + authed), sign-out lives in the UserMenu popover (authed only).
- **No Prisma.** Supabase RLS is the security boundary — every read/write goes through `@supabase/supabase-js` so the user's JWT carries through. Reach for `createSupabaseAdminClient()` (service-role) only in server-only contexts (seeds, cross-org admin) — it bypasses RLS by design.
- **Migrations are append-only.** New SQL goes in a new `000N_<name>.sql`. Apply via Supabase MCP `apply_migration`. Never edit a previously-applied file.
- **`.mcp.json` is gitignored.** Holds Supabase + Vercel personal access tokens. Don't commit.
- **Idempotent seeds + uploads.** `scripts/seed.ts` and the upload commit both check existence before insert; re-running is safe.
- **Content hashing for upload dedup.** `content_hash = sha256(normalised question text + sorted options + answer)` plus a unique index on **`(org_id, exam_id, content_hash)`** (migration 0038 — **per-exam**: was org-wide `(org_id, content_hash)` until 2026-06-15). Re-uploading the same file inserts 0 rows; the SAME question may recur across exams (CDS ↔ NDA share UPSC GAT English questions — org-wide dedup silently dropped the CDS copy). `commitStaged` upserts on `org_id,exam_id,content_hash`; `applyMockSync`'s content_hash lookups are exam-scoped to match.
- **Text normalization at write boundaries.** Long-form user-authored text (question, context, solution, options) goes through `normalizeNewlines` in the upload parser and the edit validator. The DB never carries literal `\n` (backslash+n) past the boundary; math zones are preserved verbatim. Any new write path that accepts long-form text must call the helper before insert/update — otherwise the render layer can't distinguish "real line break" from "literal text". **Enforced at the choke point since 2026-07-27:** `commitStaged` now **REJECTS** a row whose text/context/solution carries a literal `\n` (`literalNewlineFields` in [textGuard.ts](src/lib/upload/textGuard.ts)) — it does NOT silently repair it, because `content_hash` is computed by the CALLER from the pre-normalisation text, so rewriting at insert would leave stored text ≠ the hash's preimage and the next re-ingest from a corrected source would hash differently and duplicate the row. Fix the SOURCE. Detection is `normalizeNewlines(v) !== v` (not a second regex), so the guard can never disagree with the normaliser. See [[reject-dont-normalise-when-hash-upstream]].
- **OMML + math defaults via post-processing.** Two `docx`-library limitations land on the same workaround. (1) `ImportedXmlComponent` wraps imported XML in an `<undefined>` element Word rejects, so we emit placeholder runs and swap them for OMML after `Packer.toBuffer`. (2) The library doesn't expose `<m:mathPr>` for settings.xml, and Word's built-in math defaults (`defJc=centerGroup`, `wrapIndent=1440`, non-zero margins) shift any paragraph containing a 2-D math element (`<m:f>` etc.) ~1″ right of plain-text paragraphs — option `(A) -3/4` ends up out of column with option `(A) -1`. `patchZip` in `src/lib/export/docxBuilder.ts` handles both: marker → OMML in `word/document.xml`, and `<m:mathPr defJc=left wrapIndent=0 lMargin=0 rMargin=0>` injected before `</w:settings>` in `word/settings.xml`.
- **Restart `npm run dev` after adding new routes.** Tailwind JIT can miss files created mid-session. Stop dev, `rm -rf .next`, restart before UI smoke tests.
- **`/notes` LaTeX in body prose only.** Four `SubtopicNote` / `ConceptUnit` fields render as plain text (no KaTeX) AND feed SEO `metadata.description` / JSON-LD where KaTeX would be invalid: `note.title`, `note.oneLineDefinition`, `note.whyItMatters`, `concept.name`. LaTeX in any of these leaks through as raw `\(\hat{i}\)` markup on the page AND in page metadata. **Keep math only in body fields** (`intuition`, `definition`, `formula.latex`, `authoredExample.{prompt,steps,answer}`, `trap.{title,body}`) — those go through `KatexRenderer` correctly. Use Unicode (`î`, `ĵ`, `k̂`, `Σ`, `°`) or plain prose for the four plain-text fields. **Body fields also support `**bold**`** (renders via KatexRenderer / RichText — use it for key terms), and the **`definition` field additionally supports `- ` bullet lists** (rendered by `RichText`): bullet *list-shaped* definitions (term/type/property enumerations), keep narrative ones as prose. The plain-text fields render `**`/`- ` literally — never use them there.
- **Word export: lowercase option labels at render only.** Bank stores `A/B/C/D` uppercase (Excel + DB convention); the `.docx` export renders them as `(a)/(b)/(c)/(d)` in both question paper and answer key (`opt.label.toLowerCase()` in docxBuilder). Browse cards + edit form still show uppercase — only the rendered Word doc is lowercased. If a future surface needs to render bank-style labels, follow the same boundary: lowercase at render, never in the DB.
- **Word export: underline bypasses the math pipeline.** DB convention is `\(\underline{\text{word}}\)` (or `\(\underline{\textit{word}}\)` for italic+underline — biology taxonomy questions). KaTeX renders these in the browser; the docx export detects them via `UNDERLINE_BYPASS_RE` in [src/lib/export/ommlBuilder.ts](src/lib/export/ommlBuilder.ts) and emits a native Word run (`TextRun({ text, underline: { type: "single" }, italics? })`) instead of routing through `temml → mml2omml → <m:borderBox>` — Word doesn't reliably draw the bottom border on inline-math borderBox under our injected math defaults. Trailing punctuation INSIDE the delimiters (`\(\underline{\text{x}}.\)`) is split off into a separate text segment. Any pattern outside the regex (bare `\underline{word}`, chained `\text{}\text{}`, `\textbf` variants, block math) falls through to the OMML pipeline and renders without an underline in Word — run `npm run audit:underlines` after each new English / Biology batch to catch fall-throughs.
- **Tables in question content use GFM pipe-tables (2026-06-02).** A table inside a question `text` or `context` is authored as a standard Github-flavored markdown pipe-table — a header row **plus a `|---|---|` separator row**, then data rows. The separator row is mandatory and is what distinguishes a real table from inline `|·|` math (determinant `\(|A|\)`, abs value `|x|`, cell notation `Zn|Zn²⁺`): a line of pipes with **no** following separator stays prose. `parseTableBlocks` in [src/components/math/parseTableBlocks.ts](src/components/math/parseTableBlocks.ts) (pure, math-masked via `maskMathZones`, TDD in `tests/parse-table-blocks.test.ts`) splits text into prose + table blocks; cells keep their `\(...\)` math and `**bold**`. Rendered by `BlockText` (`src/components/math/BlockText.tsx`) on `/browse` (expanded card only — collapsed preview stays plain `KatexRenderer` so a table can't blow past `line-clamp-2`) + the editor Preview + the `/notes` mastery-check / `/guide` worked-example cards (`WorkedExampleCard`'s `example.context` + `example.text`, 2026-06-04), and as a native Word `<w:tbl>` in `docxBuilder` (the question NUMBER rides on the first paragraph; a stem opening with a table gets a numbered empty paragraph first). **No DB primitive** — it's all in the existing `text`/`context` columns; `normalizeNewlines` is compatible (it upgrades an Excel-authored literal-`\n` table into real newlines). **Future-only**: the existing 13 Statistics (lossless 2-row linearization) + 37 Match-Lists were NOT backfilled — they still render as linearized text. Options don't support tables (v1). For a genuine ≥3-column data grid that can't be a markdown table, fall back to a question image like a diagram. **Any surface rendering a long-form field that MAY contain a pipe-table (a solution/model-answer, not just stem/context) must render via `BlockText`, not `KatexRenderer`** — `KatexRenderer` prints a table as raw `| p | q |` text. The `/board` + `/browse` solution reveals both shipped on `KatexRenderer` and rendered truth-table answers as raw pipes (fixed 2026-07-06); `BlockText` fast-paths to `KatexRenderer` when there's no table, so it's a safe drop-in. **The same contract binds the WORD EXPORT, and it drifted for a year:** `parseTableBlocks` was wired into docx's stem + context paths but never the solution path, so a table in any solution printed as raw pipes in every downloaded answer key — 123 PUBLIC rows (fixed 2026-07-27 via `solutionBlocks` in [docxBuilder.ts](src/lib/export/docxBuilder.ts)). **The rule that prevents the next drift: the long-form field list (`text`, `context`, `solution`) × every renderer is a CONTRACT, and it is asserted by a golden test** ([docx-solution-table.test.ts](tests/docx-solution-table.test.ts)) that checks the same fixture through both surfaces. Fixing one renderer and not the other is the failure mode — it happened twice.

## Recurring pitfalls

Patterns that have bitten this codebase more than once. Each one earned its place by causing a real production regression — read before writing new code that resembles the shape.

- **PostgREST 1000-row cap on raw `.select()`.** Any `client.from(...).select(...)` that derives a count, a distinct set, a sort, or any aggregate from the *row payload* (rather than response headers via `count: "exact"` or a SQL aggregate via RPC) is suspect once the table can exceed 1000 rows. PostgREST silently truncates at row 1000 — no error, just wrong answers. **Already bit us three times:** dashboard totalQuestions (migration `0018` get_dashboard_stats), PYQ-year filter dropping older years (migration `0019` get_pyq_years), and upload commit dedup races (fixed by switching to `.upsert(..., { onConflict, ignoreDuplicates: true })` — let the DB unique index enforce dedup, not an in-memory `Set`). **Audit checklist before writing any new `.select()`:** (1) Is the row payload itself the answer, or are you computing something from it? (2) If computing, can the underlying table exceed 1000 rows ever (today or as the bank grows)? (3) If yes, use `count: "exact", head: true` for counts, or write a `security invoker` RPC that does the aggregate in Postgres. Never trust a row-derived count from `.select()` past the first 1000 rows. **Bit us twice more 2026-06-11:** `quiz:lint` used a bare `.limit(2000)` which PostgREST still caps at 1000, so it silently scanned only 1000 of ~1762 ready atoms (fixed by paging in 1000-row `.range()` windows); and **the sibling self-imposed-limit trap** — `/dashboard/quizzes` `listAssembledQuizzes` had a hard `.limit(60)` and a "Quizzes built" stat derived from `list.length`, so once the table crossed 60 quizzes (now 109) the oldest silently vanished from the filtered admin view AND the stat under-reported. Rule: a hard `.limit(N)` on a list hides rows once the table exceeds N, and any stat from `list.length` lies — derive counts from a true `count:"exact"`, and raise/paginate the list.
- **`next dev` is more forgiving than `next start` for server↔client prop serialization.** Functions and other non-serializable values passed from a Server Component to a Client Component compile cleanly under `next dev` but throw at SSR time under `next start` ("Functions cannot be passed directly to Client Components"). This bit us on the 2026-05-13 Pagination revert. **Mitigation:** the pre-push hook in `.githooks/pre-push` runs `next build` before allowing a push, so this class of bug is caught locally rather than after a Vercel deploy. Don't `git push --no-verify` unless you know exactly why.
- **`next build` does NOT exercise a DYNAMIC (`ƒ`) page's render — so the build gate is blind to its render-time throws.** Only static/SSG (`○`/`●`) routes are prerendered at build; a server-rendered-on-demand page (any page that awaits cookies / a per-request query — most `/guide/*` + `/notes/*` pages that call `resolveTaxonomy` via the anon client) is never executed until a real request, so a render exception passes `typecheck` + `lint` + `next build` + the full pre-push gate and only 500s in production. **Bit us 2026-06-14:** the new `/guide/nda-physics/ncert-map` page named a `<NcertChip>` prop `ref` — React **reserves** `ref` (it's never passed to a plain function component, so the value was `undefined` at render → "Refs cannot be used in Server Components"). Typecheck passed because the prop was explicitly typed `{ ref: NcertRef }`; the build passed because the page is `ƒ`. **Three mitigations:** (1) never name a component prop `ref` (or `key`) — they're React-reserved regardless of your prop type; (2) **curl/browser smoke-test every new DYNAMIC route before claiming done** — the build gate's green is necessary but NOT sufficient for `ƒ` pages (it IS sufficient for `○`/`●` pages, which prerender). A data-only test (like the ncert-map taxonomy test) doesn't render the page, so it can't catch this either. (3) **Confirm the smoke test actually REACHED the page** — an anon curl to any route under the `src/middleware.ts` matcher (`/dashboard/*`, `/account/*`, `/upload*`, `/uploads*`) is bounced by **middleware** before the page compiles, so the 307 tells you nothing about your code. Check the dev log for a `○ Compiling /your/route` line; no line = the request never got there. Bit us 2026-07-15 (the paper-editor rebuild — a "clean" 307 was pure middleware). For an auth-gated `ƒ` page there is **no headless proof of render** (the repo has no jsdom/testing-library/Playwright): say so plainly and hand the browser check to the user rather than implying the route was verified. See [[probe-must-reach-the-code]]. (4) **The same blindness covers any UI behind an INTERACTION, even on a public `○` page that curls clean.** Bit us 2026-07-28: a checkbox added to `/browse`'s `DownloadDialog` rendered *below* the footer and swallowed a sibling control, through a full green gate — the dialog is a Radix portal that doesn't exist until a click, so `/browse` returning 200 with correct HTML says nothing about it. Modals, popovers, sheets, accordions and anything else gated on a click are **as unverifiable here as an auth-gated `ƒ` page**; the build proves they compile, never that they lay out. Say "compile + logic proven, render not" and hand the click to the user.
- **One `cookies()` read in a SHARED SHELL component silently de-caches the ENTIRE site — and the build's route table will not tell you.** `AppHeader` is on every page. It resolved the session and read the `qb_exam` cookie during server render, so Next marked every route dynamic: **there were literally ZERO prerendered HTML files on disk**, and the `revalidate` settings on 77 `/notes` chapters, 10 `/guide` subtrees, the homepage, the `/mock` catalogue and the `/questions` landing pages had **never once taken effect** — for months. Every page served `Cache-Control: private, no-cache, no-store`. **The route table is NOT the check:** a route with `generateStaticParams` still prints `●` (SSG) even when every prerender bailed, so the build output looked correct throughout. **The only reliable probe is the filesystem** — `find .next/server/app -name '*.html' | wc -l`. If that number is 0, nothing is cached, whatever the legend says. Corollaries: (a) audit the shared shell (`layout.tsx`, `AppHeader`, any always-rendered component) for `cookies()`/`headers()`/`auth.getUser()` **before** optimising any individual page — a page-level fix is worthless while the shell de-caches everything; (b) `createSupabaseServerClient()` reads cookies internally, so calling it counts; (c) once fixed, prove cache SAFETY from the artifacts, not from reasoning — grep every `.html` **and `.rsc`** for emails / org names / authed-only controls, since a cached page is one copy served to everyone (2026-07-29: 622 pages, 0 leaks). See [[shell-component-decaches-site]].
- **Making a page cacheable BREAKS anything that relied on a server re-render, and the failures are silent.** Two fired on the same day: (1) `ExamPill` wrote the exam cookie then called `router.refresh()` so the SERVER would recompute the nav hrefs — on a cached page a refresh just re-serves the same copy, so the switcher would have appeared to do nothing (fixed by moving the active slug into client state). (2) `useSearchParams()` anywhere in the tree **bails a static prerender out to client rendering** — `QuestionList`'s bookmark button did this, silently costing the landing pages the caching they exist for; the fix is a `<Suspense>` boundary, and the symptom is only visible as a missing `.html` file. Grep for `router.refresh()` and `useSearchParams()` before making a route static. See [[next-caching-traps]].
- **Source-source subtopic name collisions during bulk chapter merges.** When canonicalizing N source chapters into one target chapter, a bulk `UPDATE subtopics SET chapter_id = '<tgt>' WHERE chapter_id IN ('<src1>', '<src2>', ...)` can fail with `23505` on `subtopics_chapter_id_name_key` even if a pre-flight source-vs-target collision check came back clean — because two sources may share a same-named subtopic that collides only once both are reparented. Bit us on the NDA Physics E&M cluster (5 sources → "Magnetic Field Inside a Solenoid" appeared in two of them). **Mitigation:** process sources sequentially via a `DO $$ FOR mapping IN (VALUES (...)) LOOP ... END $$` block; each iteration redirects colliding questions, deletes colliding source subtopics, reparents the rest, updates remaining question chapter_ids, and deletes the source chapter — so by the time source 2 merges, source 1's content is already in target and the standard collision-handling kicks in. Pattern + full template in `[[reclassification-sql-pattern]]`.
- **Catch-all chapters and subtopics drift until you reclassify per-question.** Buckets named "Mechanics", "Physics", "Error Identification in Sentences" collect every question that doesn't cleanly fit a more-specific name. They look harmless during a chapter-level merge but rot the bank's usefulness for technique drills. **Mitigation:** when a chapter or subtopic holds ≤~50 mixed-topic questions, read each stem and produce a `(question_id, target_canonical_id)` mapping; execute as a `DO $$ FOR m IN (VALUES (qid, tgt)) LOOP ... END $$` that (a) looks up the question's source subtopic, (b) finds same-named subtopic in target chapter, (c) redirects+deletes on collision, otherwise reparents+moves chapter_id. Precedents: Spotting Errors 29 q (2026-05-15 NDA English); Mechanics 19 q + Physics 26 q catch-all chapters (2026-05-15 NDA Physics). Beyond ~50 q the user prefers chapter-level merge + subtopic-cleanup as a follow-up pass.
- **`/notes` subtopic slugs are GLOBALLY unique, not chapter-scoped.** `question_concept_tags` keys on `(question_id, subtopic_slug, concept_slug)` with **no chapter or subject column**, and `loadConceptDrills` / the mastery-checkpoint query filter by `subtopic_slug` ALONE. So two chapters that both use a slug like `foundations` will cross-contaminate each other's drill lists + checkpoints. Bit us 2026-05-30: MHT-CET Indefinite Integration reused Sound's `foundations` slug — notes-lint caught it as "DB tag has unknown concept_slug" because the other chapter's concepts don't match. **Mitigation:** every notes subtopic slug must be unique across ALL chapters in `NOTES_CHAPTERS` (the URL segment can repeat per subject route, but the slug used as the concept-tag key cannot). MHT-CET's foundation subtopic was renamed `foundations` → `fundamentals`. notes-lint surfaces the collision but only *after* tags are inserted — pick distinctive slugs up front.
- **Renaming a SHIPPED chapter silently rots its `/guide` + `/notes` cross-refs.** The DB taxonomy and the guide/notes editorial TS are joined only by NAME at request time (`resolveTaxonomy`), so a Phase-D rename/merge leaves the TS pointing at a dead subtopic — the "Drill the N →" CTA resolves to an EMPTY `/browse` filter, no error. Bit us twice on 2026-05-30: the Sound notes 5→4 redesign broke the `/guide/nda-physics` Sound playbook + strategy `mustDrill` + CHAPTER_TABLE prose; the 2026-05-29 Probability merge ("Probability with Dice" → "Probability via Counting") broke two `nda-maths` principle drills + the Probability tier. **Mitigation:** after any rename/merge of a shipped chapter, sync `/guide` `_data` (playbooks `subtopics[]`, strategy `mustDrill`/`skipSubtopics`, principles `drill`, compounds filters, prose counts) + `/notes` `_data` + concept tags, then run `npm test` — the 7 playbook guides + CA themes + Maths each have a `describe.skipIf(!HAS_ENV)` live-taxonomy resolution test (`guide-nda-<subject>-playbooks.test.ts`; `guide-nda-maths-taxonomy.test.ts` for Template A) that fails on drill-target drift. Full procedure: [[shipped-chapter-rename-downstream-sync]]. The `/browse` backlink chip (`getQuestionResources` name lookup) + the single-page Economics chapter CTA are the cross-link surfaces still untested.
- **Integration-test fixtures leak into the LIVE bank when `afterAll` is interrupted — and only `org_id` reliably catches them.** Every DB-integration test inserts throwaway rows (org, questions, taxonomy) into the single prod Supabase project and cleans them in `afterAll`; a parallel/killed/throwing run skips that cleanup, stranding PUBLIC fixtures on `/browse` and inflating the bank counts. Bit us 2026-06-11: 18 fixture questions (11 PUBLIC) + 12 orgs leaked. **No content signal is complete** — `source_file`+`upload_job_id` both NULL misses fixtures that set a test `upload_job_id`; text-pattern misses the `goodXlsxBuffer` upload-flow fixtures (realistic stems, **real sha256 `content_hash`, under canonical taxonomy**); `content_hash !~ '^[0-9a-f]{64}$'` (0 real rows) misses those same ones. **The one universal signal is `org_id`:** every fixture belongs to a throwaway org whose name carries an 8-hex run-id (`randomUUID().slice(0,8)`). **Mitigation:** `global-teardown` is now an org-keyed cascade sweep (`isTestOrgName`, [[test-data-leak-org-signal]]) — but it's a SAFETY NET that only runs at the end of a full `vitest run`. A subset/watch run that creates fixtures and is then killed leaves them until the next full suite. After any interrupted DB-test run, sweep manually or run a full `npm test`. To clean: clear `question_reports` (its `org_id` FK is RESTRICT) then delete the test orgs (CASCADEs questions→options/tags/reports + org_members + upload_jobs).

## Design axes

Load-bearing design distinctions for the project. Each one earns its place by being structural — getting the axis wrong has cost real work, and the answer to "which axis does this live on?" is non-obvious for the named feature areas.

### Tag taxonomies — principles (horizontal) vs concepts (vertical)

**`/guide` principles** and **`/notes` concepts** are different kinds of tag and must NEVER share a schema or be merged into one taxonomy.

| | Principles | Concepts |
|---|---|---|
| Axis | Horizontal — cross-subtopic | Vertical — within-subtopic |
| Cardinality | ~20 in NDA Maths | ~5–10 per subtopic × hundreds = thousands at scale |
| Storage | TS modules (`/guide/.../principles.ts`) + curated `extraQuestionIds` | DB table `question_concept_tags` (migration 0021) |
| Authoring | Editorial — hand-curated, slow update cadence | Per-upload — admin tagging UI or chat session, fast cadence |
| Value comes from | **Selectivity** (only the top-20, hand-picked) | **Coverage** (every relevant question tagged) |
| Identity question | "What unifying lens does this question demand?" | "What specific technique within this chapter?" |

**The wall — non-negotiable:**
- DON'T migrate `/guide` principles to a DB table. Hand-curation is the signal-to-noise mechanism; "scaling" them dilutes their value.
- DON'T bundle principles into `question_concept_tags` via a `tag_kind` discriminator. Flattens the strategic distinction.
- DO share infrastructure at the seam: one admin tagging UI on `/dashboard/questions/[id]/edit`, one notes-lint validator, future LLM-suggest can suggest both kinds. Sharing UI ≠ sharing schema.

**Why this earned a section:** The initial concept-tags proposal (Phase 1, 2026-05-17) was a unified `question_tags` table. User pushed back; I conceded. Both axes shipped separately (principles still in TS, concepts in migration 0021) and the wall has held. This section exists so future tag-related features (admin UI, LLM-suggest, AI tutor grounding, MHT_CET_AI integration) don't re-litigate it from scratch.

### Cross-link backlinks — chapter-level (automatic name lookup) vs tag-level (manual DB-tagged override)

The `/browse` QuestionCard chip row (Tier 1 + Tier 1.5, 2026-05-18) renders **two distinct classes of backlink** and the wall between them matters. Same dropdown of chips, two completely different authoring loops and data sources.

| | Chapter-level chips | Tag-level chips |
|---|---|---|
| Examples | "Playbook: Sound" · "Concept notes" | "Lever: Vieta" · "Concept: Weighted Mean" |
| Source | Name-based lookup at render time | DB row in `question_principle_tags` / `question_concept_tags` |
| Authoring cost | **Zero per question** — purely a function of `(exam, subject, chapter, subtopic)` | **Manual per question** — chat tagging session, INSERT with `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` |
| Coverage | 100% of questions whose chapter has a guide / subtopic has notes | Only questions explicitly tagged (today: 488 principle tags · 262 concept tags) |
| Failure mode | Silent break when DB chapter renamed (no lint catch yet — see ROADMAP "notes-lint guide-side rename validation") | Silent fallback to generic chip when slug doesn't resolve via `getPrincipleName` / `getConceptName` |
| Override behaviour | The default; renders for all qualifying questions | When present, **replaces** the chapter-level chip for the same axis (more specific wins) |
| Resolver | `getQuestionResources({exam, subject, chapter, subtopic})` in `src/lib/links/questionResources.ts` | `getQuestionResources({...}, tags)` — same function, second arg from `getResourceTagsForQuestions` batch |

**The wall — design intent:**
- DON'T expand the chapter-level lookup to do "fuzzy" matching when a chapter rename happens. The failure mode (chip disappears) is the correct signal — fix the rename, don't paper over the drift.
- DON'T attempt to back-fill tag-level chips from the chapter-level data (e.g. auto-create a `Lever: <chapter-name>` chip when no principle tag exists). That destroys the curated-vs-automatic distinction and floods low-leverage questions with meaningless tag chips.
- DO let tag-level chips quietly fall through to chapter-level chips when the tag's slug doesn't resolve. Better a generic "NDA Maths strategy" chip than a broken `/principles/<unknown-slug>` link.
- DO share infrastructure at the seam: both axes flow through the same `getQuestionResources(input, tags?)` function, both render via the same `<ResourceChip>` component in QuestionCard. The two-tier system is a function-signature distinction (optional second arg) not two separate code paths.

**Why this earned a section:** The 2026-05-18 Tier 1.5 ship added tag-level overrides AFTER the Tier 1 chapter-level chips were in production. The temptation in the moment was to refactor into "one unified backlink table" or to have tag-level chips deduplicate against chapter-level chips. Both ideas are wrong — they conflate two different authoring loops (curated tagging session vs no-touch render-time mapping) and two different coverage shapes (long-tail per-question vs blanket-per-chapter). The split shipped cleanly because the function signature preserved the asymmetry: `tags?` is optional, the resolver short-circuits chapter-level chips when a more-specific tag-chip exists, and untagged questions still get the generic chip — graceful fallback throughout. Sister wall to the tag-taxonomies axis above.

### Guide strand axis — execution-mode (chapter-partitioning) vs tier-style (chapter-tiering)

Within Template B variants, the **strand split** is the strategic axis the strategy page renders against. There are two fundamentally different ways to construct it, and they are NOT interchangeable — picking the wrong one produces a strategy axis that sorts questions by nothing meaningful.

| | Execution-mode strands | Tier-style strands |
|---|---|---|
| Subjects | NDA English (R/Rule/Reason), NDA Chemistry (R/Rule/Calculate), NDA Biology (R/Apply/Verify), NDA Geography (R/Apply/Verify) | NDA History (Cornerstone/Foundation Recall/Quick-Win) |
| Strand semantics | Each strand is a SKILL bucket (recall a fact / apply a rule / trace a mechanism / evaluate a statement). Chapters get grouped into a strand by their dominant execution mode. | Each strand is a STRATEGIC TIER (cornerstone weight / foundation recall / quick-win). Chapters get grouped into a strand by their bank share + %HARD profile. |
| Recall slot | Stable — there's always a Recall strand (named-fact recall is universal in Indian entrance exams). | Stable in spirit (Foundation Recall) but the slot SEMANTICS bent — it now groups chapters by tier, not by execution mode. |
| When to pick | Execution modes PARTITION chapters: one cluster of chapters is 60%+ dominated by one mode, others are 20%- on that mode. | Execution modes are UNIFORM across all chapters (25–45% everywhere) — can't partition. AND/OR one chapter has dominant bank share (40%+) that warrants its own Cornerstone tier. |
| Decision probe | For each candidate mode (recall/rule/apply/verify/calculate/reason/multi-statement/date-anchored), count `q × chapter_spread` AND per-chapter %.
