  - **The BLIND lane repeatedly justified itself.** Papers were triaged SAFE (trustworthy numbering) vs BLIND (key untrustworthy → derive every answer, ignore the key). The decisive case: **2023-feb01's ENTIRE shift-2 key block is displaced by +2**, confirmed against 18 independent derivations — trusting it would have shipped ~13 wrong answers from one paper. Twelve further single keys were proven wrong, each re-derived before acceptance: torque `[ML²T⁻²]` gives 25% not 5%; two Carnot engines in series match one ("same", not "always less"); constant velocity makes the contact force `Mg`, not `Mg cosθ`; a plane EM wave's electric and magnetic energy densities are EQUAL so the ratio is 1, not 2; Young's modulus DECREASES with temperature; 7 mA less a 1.5 mA load leaves 5.5 mA in the Zener, not 10; `a sinθ = 2λ` gives a 6e-3 rad divergence where the key read 60; AM bandwidth is `2f_m` (the booklet wrote the right expression then evaluated `2ω_c`). In nearly every case the booklet's own worked solution contradicted its own printed letter.
  - **A whole paper was nearly lost.** A completeness audit diffing every `papers/*.json` against the DB found **2023-jan30 with ZERO Physics rows** — never dispatched, and the subject would have been declared finished with a sitting missing. It had to reconcile BOTH directions: the 11 legacy 2021 papers legitimately have DB rows but no paper-file classification. → [[completeness-reconcile-both-ways]].
  - **`scan-flip` published rows it had no business touching.** It matched on `source_file` alone, but a JEE file carries all three subjects, each ingested in its own pass — so flipping Physics for 2023-Jan29 also published **Maths Q67/Q70**, which the Maths pass had deliberately withheld for corrupted options. Both restored to PRIVATE; every recorded withhold across the exam was then audited (apr10 Q171/175, jan30 Q176, jul28 Q69, Paper9 Q84, p19 Q18 — all correctly PRIVATE, Jan29 the only breach). `scan-flip` now takes `--subject`, scoping both the scan and the UPDATE, and warns on an unscoped flip. → [[scope-writes-to-your-pass]].
  - **A build failure whose obvious diagnosis was wrong.** `next build` began failing on the `/questions` landing pages with "canceling statement due to statement timeout", a DIFFERENT handful each run. The natural read was a missing index — but **EXPLAIN showed the chapter query runs in 13 ms** on an existing index. The real cause is contention: Next prerenders 300+ of those pages concurrently and this session's ~1,600 new rows tipped the burst over Postgres' `statement_timeout`. An index would have fixed nothing. `queryQuestions` now retries ONLY a cancelled statement (SQLSTATE 57014, with a message fallback since PostgREST does not always pass the code), twice with a short backoff; failing-twice → clean.
  - **Structured repairs beat prose.** The BLIND solvers were correctly diagnosing dropped-digit stems but recording the repair as *prose*, which nothing can apply — a question would have shipped with a correct answer and a still-unanswerable stem. The prompt now demands a structured `stemFix`/`optionFix`, and the applier **refuses any fragment that does not match exactly once** rather than force-applying a near-miss (~300 repairs applied). `assemble-blind` also now merges into an existing paper file instead of clobbering it (every BLIND paper already carried a shipped Maths block) and treats a row whose options were absorbed into the stem as shippable when all four labels are supplied — `commit.ts` could already synthesize them, but the assembler was dropping such rows first.
  - **Render cleanup, bank-wide:** 351 escaped fill-in blanks (`\_\_\_\_` renders literally outside math), 54 empty math zones, 19 KaTeX-breaking zones (a body ending in a lone `\`; `\text{∮}` → `\oint`), and finally 8 Maths rows where pandoc split a set `\{…\}` across zones and orphaned the braces — reconstructed by hand, then **persisted into the paper files as overrides** so a later `resync` is a no-op rather than a silent revert (→ [[persist-fix-to-source-of-record]]). **KaTeX-broken: 47 → 0 of 7,436**; render-corruption 221 → 6.
  - **Judgement calls made AGAINST the agents, in both directions.** Rejected jan31 Q22 (its uniqueness argument only tried integer thicknesses; `K = t/(t−1)` is equally clean for t = 1.5, 4/3, 1.25) and Q92 (its own note conceded the opposite reading gives another option) — jan31 being the one paper with **no solution document at all**, so it ran under a stricter brief (double-derive, falsify distractors, per-row confidence, skip readily). Conversely I nearly rejected p24 Q29, whose *stated* justification was an aesthetic "drop-pattern" prior — until the booklet's own working showed `250 + 150 = 400`. → [[classify-findings-by-evidence]] sharpened: an agent's stated reasoning is not its evidence, in either direction.

### 2026-07

- **2026-07-29 — MH-SSC-10 Social Sciences COMPLETE: Geography + History + Political Science, 10 papers → 372 q PUBLIC (mh-ssc-10 1,147 → 1,519), and the pipeline learns that ONE paper can be TWO subjects (merges `0641acd` + `12e08b5`).** The ask was "complete geog and hist"; analysing the papers first changed the job three times before a line was written.
  - **Neither subject is the subject its filename claims.** The "History" PDF is **Social Sciences Paper I — History AND Political Science**, and says so in its own printed note ("Question Nos. 1 to 5 are based on History and Question Nos. 6 to 9 are based on Political Science") in **both** syllabus eras. The 2016-2018 "Geography" PDF is likewise **Paper II — Geography AND Economics**. So `subject` became a **per-question field** (like `chapter`) and `buildPaperRecords` takes an ORDERED catalog list; a chapter is validated against its **OWN** subject's catalog, never the union, so a History chapter named under Political Science fails loudly instead of silently passing. Passing a single catalog is unchanged and **all 11 pre-existing lib tests stayed green untouched** — that is the proof the 39 shipped papers cannot be affected. Every one of the five History sittings split **23 History / 11 Political Science** and summed to the printed 40 marks, so the model held across years, not just on the pilot.
  - **Scope narrowed by the user to the REVISED COURSE only**, which deleted the Economics subject entirely (it exists only in the out-of-scope 2016-2018 Paper II) and took the job from 18 papers to 12. Catalogs were lifted verbatim from the split Balbharati textbooks on disk (Geography 9 chapters · History 9 · Political Science 5) rather than invented, so the hard-validated chapter axis matches the book students read. Geography uses the **`geog-`** id prefix — `geo-` is already GEOMETRY, and that collision would have clobbered both paper ids and data files.
  - **TWO of the 12 papers are NOT ingested, and that is the load-bearing decision.** The only 2023 Social Sciences PDFs on disk are **Marathi-medium** (`N 964` "GEOGRAPHY PAPER-II **(M)**", `N 956` "PAPER-I **(M)**") — the medium is on the COVER, not in the filename. An agent drafted a complete English translation of geog-2023 and **refused to merge it**; it was discarded. The reason is mechanical, not stylistic: **`content_hash` is computed from the stem, so a translated row can never dedup against the real English paper and would permanently duplicate the sitting.** `config.ts` records the reasoning inline so a later session does not re-add the years blindly.
  - **Dates come from the printed cover code, never the filename** (`<year> <month-roman> <day> <time>`), and two are not March: **geog-2020 is the JULY 2020 sitting** (Paper II was postponed past the COVID lockdown while Paper I was not — `N 452` = 2020 III 21) and **hist-2022 is `N 752` = 1 April 2022**. The four already-shipped 2020 Maths/Science papers were checked and are genuinely March, so nothing needed backfilling. A `MONTH_OVERRIDE` map keyed by paper id carries the two exceptions.
  - **One wrong key found and fixed — and only a CROSS-PAPER comparison could see it.** `hist-2026 Q1(A)(3)` keyed *Ranke* as the founder of modern historiography; `hist-2020` asked the identical question with identical options and keyed *Voltaire*. The prescribed Balbharati textbook settles it verbatim — *"Thus, it is said that Voltaire was the founder of modern historiography"* — and the board question is copied word-for-word from that chapter's own exercise. Fixed via the existing `scripts/grounding/apply-key-fix.ts` (collision-guarded `content_hash` recompute) in the DB **and** the source JSON. Generalised into a standing probe: match repeated MCQs on their **option SET** (survives differing instruction prefixes, which exact-text matching does not), then adjudicate. Of its 5 hits, 1 was the real conflict, 2 were false positives (punctuation; two genuinely different questions sharing four options — *cheapest* mode vs *most common in Brazil*, both correct), 2 genuinely agreed. See [[cross-paper-stem-consistency]].
  - **55 figure crops** across the Geography papers (maps, a population pyramid, bar/line/pie graphs), each anchored from a **measured ink profile** rather than eyeballed and verified visually — the `ok` flag only means the anchors sat in whitespace. Two real catches: the 2020 pyramid first cropped without its title or X-axis scale, which `Q6(B)(5)` ("percentage of female aged 55-59") cannot be answered without; and a skewed scan whose instruction line dips below its own row-average band, so excluding it would have crossed into the map's top border — a sliver of instruction text is the better trade. **`attach-images.ts` is now size-aware**: 4× is right for a small geometry diagram, but near-full-page maps and dense graph-paper grids blew past the 1 MB storage cap, so it steps the render scale down and falls back to JPEG; small figures still take the 4× PNG path unchanged.
  - **Then the per-chapter handout registry was extended 33 → 56 chapters** (`chapter-pyq.ts`), building **112 files / 1,459 q / 213 figures**. Per-subject totals reconcile exactly against the bank with nothing dropped — the Social Sciences papers are revised-course only, so none of the old-syllabus exclusions that keep ~60 rows out of the Maths/Science handouts apply. History and Political Science are two bank subjects **sharing one parent folder** (the board prints them as one paper) and both number chapters from 01, so the dir PREFIX is the only thing separating their output paths — hence two distinct helpers and a test asserting the routing. The explicit-registry choice paid again: the bank spells the Historiography chapters with a colon while the folders use an underscore.
  - **Verified before writing into the content tree:** a pilot build showed 12 `<w:drawing>` elements against 2 media entries — the docx library dedups identical bitmaps by content hash, so every figure-question renders. Answer keys carry no question figures, which matches the already-shipped chapters exactly (Circle: 26 media in the paper, 0 in the key) — established behaviour, not a regression, though it means a Geography map-reading key shows the questions without the map.
  - **Honest gaps:** all 372 answers are derived/authored + `reviewFlag` (board papers ship no key); the scans carry a previous reader's pencil marks that were **wrong 3 times out of 5** on hist-2026 and once each on hist-2024/geog-2024, so they were treated as non-evidence throughout; and `geog-2026 Q6(B)(4)/(5)` remains the weakest row — a bar graph with no numeric labels where two independent verification probes of mine failed (they locked onto the graph-paper grid instead of the tick dashes), so the hedged agent reading stands rather than a manufactured correction.

- **2026-07-29 — THE SITE HAD NEVER CACHED A SINGLE PAGE. Root-caused to `AppHeader`, fixed, and 622 pages now prerender — plus `/questions`, 317 indexable per-chapter landing pages (merges `ae0fbed` + `a9b9a08`).** Started as "analyse this Vercel Active-CPU chart", ended as the largest perf/SEO finding in the project's history. Three shipped pieces, in the order they were discovered.
  - **The chart read (4h 03m Active CPU / 30 days, ~95% of the Hobby allowance, run-rate doubling).** Cross-referencing Web Analytics gave the number that mattered: **7,638 page views ⇒ ≈1.9 SECONDS of Active CPU per page view**, against a 50–200ms norm, on only ~255 views/day. Composition: function 94.2% / middleware 5.8% (so the [[middleware-auth-scope]] fix HELD — middleware is no longer the cost), nodejs 89.6% / edge 10.4%, iad1 92.2%. **The `edge` line minus the `middleware` line ≈ 11m/month is the seven `opengraph-image.tsx` routes** (`runtime="edge"`, satori+resvg — CPU-dense, cache busted per deploy); the non-iad1 regions ≈ that same edge work running near the viewer, which is a LATENCY signal (users in India, functions in Virginia), NOT a cost one, since Active CPU excludes I/O wait. **Rule banked: Active CPU counts CPU, not waiting — so DB-latency work does not move this meter; only doing fewer renders does.**
  - **Level 1 — `/browse` per-request work.** It resolved the viewer THREE times (`getSessionMember` + `getSessionUser` + `getSessionSuperadmin`, each with its own client and its own `auth.getUser()`, sequentially) and re-fetched the exam/subject/chapter/subtopic lists for every visitor. Now one `getPageIdentity()` with a **free cookie-name short-circuit** (no Supabase cookie ⇒ no session ⇒ zero auth work, the path most traffic takes), plus `unstable_cache` on the taxonomy reads. **Deliberately NOT cached: `get_chapter_facets`/`get_subtopic_facets`/`get_pyq_years`/the question list** — all four are `security invoker` (0019/0020/0037), so caching them against the anon client would hide staff's own PRIVATE rows from their filter counts. Visibility is untouched: the question query still runs on the cookie-bound client, and the identity booleans only drive UI.
  - **Level 2 — `/questions`, 317 per-chapter landing pages.** Every question sat behind a UUID query string and the sitemap offered Google **exactly one URL** (`/browse`) for ~24k questions; analytics showed **~196 of 7,638 page views (2.5%) arriving from Google search**. Now `/questions/<exam>/<subject>/<chapter>` for every chapter with ≥15 PUBLIC questions, plus a `/questions` crawl hub and 318 sitemap entries. Slugs are **derived** from taxonomy names (DB stays the source of truth; a new chapter gets a page free) with `dedupeBySlug` guaranteeing one-URL-one-page, and names with no ASCII letters (future Marathi/Hindi subjects) yielding an empty slug that is **skipped rather than romanised**. Counts come from the existing `get_chapter_facets` aggregate, never row-counting (the 1000-row cap). `landingFilterParams()` is the single definition of "which questions is this page about", feeding both the page query and its `/browse` deep link so they cannot disagree. **Routing conflict on the way:** Next forbids two differently-named dynamic segments at the same position, so `/questions/[id]/edit` blocked `/questions/[examSlug]/…` → **the admin editor moved to `/dashboard/questions/[id]/edit`** (where admin surfaces belong) with a **config-level** 308 for bookmarks — config rather than a route file precisely so it doesn't recreate the conflict. `robots.txt` then dropped its blanket `/questions` disallow, which existed only for that editor and would otherwise have hidden all 317 pages.
  - **THE FINDING — discovered only because the new pages refused to prerender.** `AppHeader` renders on every page and resolved the session + read the `qb_exam` cookie during server render. Both are per-request, so **every route was dynamic: zero prerendered HTML files existed, and `revalidate` on 77 notes chapters / 10 guides / the homepage / the mock catalogue had never once taken effect.** The build's route table hid it — a route with `generateStaticParams` still prints `●` even when every prerender bailed. Fixed by moving all per-visitor state to the browser (`HeaderBar` client island + a no-store `/api/me/header`), leaving only the **slug→exam-uuid map** on the server because it is public taxonomy identical for every visitor. **Result: 622 prerendered pages** (456 notes + 113 guide + 40 landing + …); `/notes` and `/guide` now serve `x-nextjs-cache: HIT` in 16–236ms; a landing page goes 458ms → **13ms** on its second hit. `/browse` stays dynamic by design (searchParams).
  - **Caching is a CORRECTNESS change, so the leak gate was the hard gate.** A cached page is one copy served to everyone — a header holding an email would hand it to the next visitor. Proved from the artifacts rather than by reasoning: swept every prerendered `.html` **and `.rsc`** → **0 org names, 0 "Sign out", 0 staff-only Papers tab, 0 session emails** (the only addresses present are the deliberate public contact address and a form placeholder), and confirmed `/dashboard`, `/me`, `/account`, `/saved`, `/upload`, `/uploads`, `/superadmin` all still render per-request.
  - **Two silent breakages the change forced** (both now in Recurring pitfalls): `ExamPill`'s `router.refresh()` would have stopped working on a cached page (a refresh re-serves the same copy), and `useSearchParams()` in `QuestionList`'s bookmark button **bails a static prerender to client rendering**, which was silently costing the landing pages their caching until a `<Suspense>` boundary was added.
  - **Retro-invalidates a prior fix:** the 2026-06-27 egress work bumped `revalidate` 3600→86400 on 208 pages — but no page was ever ISR-cached, so that lever was almost certainly a **no-op** for full-route caching (the calmer `/browse` sitemap entry was the part that could have helped). Any egress improvement then should be re-attributed. **Owed:** a signed-in browser click-through — the authed header has no headless proof in this repo. See [[shell-component-decaches-site]] + [[next-caching-traps]] + [[public-landing-pages]].

- **2026-07-28 — Optional `[Exam Year]` source citation on the exported Question Paper (merged `bc77178`) + the download-dialog layout bug it exposed (`b2ac57e`).** A teacher can now tick "Cite the source after each question" and get the coaching-book citation — `[JEE Mains 2016]` — printed after each stem, before the options. **Off by default**, so every pre-existing export stays byte-identical. Shipped through the existing single seam: pure `formatSourceTag` → `buildQuestionPaper({includeSourceTag})` → `options.includeSourceTag` on `/api/export` (paper branch only) → a checkbox on both download surfaces (`/browse` DownloadDialog + the paper-builder `PaperDownload`). No migration, no query change — `QuestionRow` already carried `exam.name` + `pyqYear`.
  - **The load-bearing data finding: `pyq_year` is non-null on exactly the `question_kind='pyq'` rows and null on exactly the `practice` rows** (verified bank-wide: 4,069 JEE · 6,617 MHT-CET · 4,860 NDA · 1,539 NEET · 1,147 SSC all carry a year; all 11,375 practice rows carry none). So **a null year IS the "not a PYQ" gate** — a mixed paper tags its PYQs and never credits a practice question to a sitting it never appeared in, with no `question_kind` plumbing on the view-model.
  - **Exam + year only, and that was a data decision not a taste one.** `pyq_note` is a clean label only for NDA (`"NDA 1"`/`"NDA 2"`); within JEE the SAME column holds `"Paper 7"` AND `"29 June 2022"` AND `"8 Apr 2023"`, and MH-SSC-10 stores a 45-char provenance sentence. Uniform `[<exam.name> <pyq_year>]` is the one shape that reads correctly for every exam. Deliberately NOT `formatProvenance` (the web card's `Q# · disambiguator · year` chip), which would print `[Q12 · Apr · 2023]` on a printed paper.
  - **Placement detail worth keeping:** the tag attaches to the **last prose block** of the stem, so a stem ending in a GFM pipe-table gets it as its own paragraph rather than losing it inside a table cell — the same `parseTableBlocks` seam the render-contract golden tests already guard.
  - **Then the user's screenshot exposed a layout bug that was NOT mine but which my third checkbox tripped.** `DownloadDialog`'s scrolling body was missing **`min-h-0`**: a flex item's default `min-height:auto` refuses to shrink below its content, so the body never clipped, the dialog outgrew `max-h-[90dvh]`, and the `sticky bottom-0` footer rode UP over the tail of the content — swallowing "Group by subtopic" and stranding the new checkbox below the buttons. Fixed with `min-h-0` + dropping the `sticky` (the footer sits outside the scroll area, so it is always visible anyway and `sticky` only bought the overlap). Separately the 4-button footer never fit `max-w-lg`, drawing "Done" **outside** the dialog's left edge → `sm:max-w-xl` + `sm:flex-wrap` (same wrap fix on `PaperDownload`, which has the identical footer). See [[mobile-render-gotchas]].
  - **Process lesson, banked:** I reported the feature verified on a green gate, but **a dialog behind a click has no headless proof in this repo at all** — `/browse` compiles and renders anon, so the usual public-route curl says nothing about a modal's interior. Same family as the ƒ-page pitfall but a distinct trigger; the "Recurring pitfalls" bullet now covers interaction-gated components, and [[probe-must-reach-the-code]] is sharpened. The .docx itself WAS verified beyond fixtures (built the same 11 real bank rows both ways → 9 tags, both practice rows clean).

- **2026-07-27 — JEE Maths SUBTOPIC drift cleaned: 8 synonym merges, 127 → 113 subtopics, 3,687 q preserved exactly.** The 2026-07-24 reshape fixed JEE Maths at CHAPTER level but left subtopics, and the 2021 vs 2024+ ingest waves had classified the same material under different names. Merged (all within-chapter, so no `chapter_id` touched): Binomial Theorem's **4-way** remainder cluster (*Remainder and Divisibility* / *Divisibility and Remainders* / *Remainder Problems* / *Divisibility*) → 9 · AoD *Mean Value Theorem* → *Rolle's and Mean Value Theorems* 7 · PnC *Inclusion-Exclusion Counting* → *Counting with Inclusion-Exclusion* 5 · Probability's 3 *Random Variables …* buckets → *…and Distributions* 8 · Quadratic *Modulus Equations* → *Equations with Absolute Value* 24 · Mathematical Reasoning *Logical Connectives* + *Compound Statements* → *Tautology and Logical Connectives* 36 · Straight Lines *Orthocentre* + *Centroid* → *Coordinate Geometry of Triangles* 12 · Conics *Tangents to a Parabola* + *Normals to a Parabola* → *Tangents and Normals* 35. Integrity verified: **3,687 q preserved · 0 empty subtopics · 0 chapter/subtopic mismatches** (the 1 NULL `subtopic_id` is the pre-existing PRIVATE Paper-11 Matrices row, an untouched chapter). Reparent-then-delete order per [[fk-set-null-on-subtopic-drop]]; blast radius is the `/browse` dropdown only (JEE Maths has no notes/guide/mocks/concept-tags).
  - **The load-bearing part is the two clusters NOT merged — sampling stems refuted the drift hypothesis for both.** **3D Geometry's `The Plane` (70) vs `Line and Plane` (47)** looked like textbook drift (70/0 year cliff) but the names carve at a REAL joint: plane-only work (three planes, plane through a line of intersection, mirror image in a plane) vs line↔plane interaction (distance measured parallel to a line, foot of perpendicular on a line). The cliff is a **classification-preference shift** in the later ingest wave, not synonymy — merging would have destroyed a genuine distinction. **Binomial Theorem's coefficient trio** (*Coefficient Sums* 52 / *Coefficients and Series* 25 / *Coefficients* 17) genuinely overlaps, but a merge is the wrong remedy: *Coefficients and Series* is a catch-all holding **misfiled number-theory questions** (`3ⁿ+7ⁿ` multiple of 10) and *Coefficients* holds a remainder question — they need per-question reclassification, not a rename-merge. **Rule: a duplicate-looking subtopic name is a hypothesis; read stems from each before merging** (the catch-all-chapters pitfall, one level down). Both deferred in SUGGESTIONS.

- **2026-07-27 — NEW axis: the cross-exam SYLLABUS-FIT screen (migration 0062) — JEE Mains questions served as practice to NDA+CET students; first chapter screened 251 q → 239 usable · 12 dropped.** LWS teaches NDA and MHT-CET students and wants JEE Mains PYQs as *stretch practice*. So JEE is a **question SOURCE, not an audience**, and the only question that matters is: *can a student taught the NDA ∪ CET syllabus solve this?* That is prerequisite-coverage, **not** resemblance — an early framing measured "does NDA ask it this way", which would have wrongly discarded all 82 JEE parameter-consistency questions that NDA never *asks* that way but every NDA student can *answer*.
  - **The union is wide, so this is an EXCLUSION job, not an inclusion job.** NDA covers what CET drops (Height & Distance, Properties of Triangle, Binomial Theorem, Binary, Logarithms, deep Conics); CET covers what NDA drops (Mathematical Logic ↔ JEE's Mathematical Reasoning, Linear Programming, Cayley-Hamilton, Pair of Straight Lines, heavy Line & Plane). Together they cover nearly all of JEE Maths. **Matrices+Determinants: 239 of 251 pass (95%)**, and the 12 drops reduce to just **four tools** — similarity/conjugation (6), 3×3 Cayley-Hamilton (2), eigenvalues (2), 3×3 nilpotent shift + the XᵀAX skew test (1 each).
  - **Two sub-results worth keeping.** *All 82 linear-systems questions pass* — they look like the chapter's hardest ("has infinitely many solutions, then 13αβ =") but NDA teaches *Finding the parameter for consistency* as an explicit notes concept; JEE only wraps a composite output around it. And *all 104 non-system determinant questions pass, zero drops* — **NDA's determinant coverage is deeper than JEE's own notes chapter** (Vandermonde, cyclic, differentiating a determinant, sums of determinants, the full adjoint ladder).
  - **The data model is the load-bearing decision: store the EXCEPTIONS, not the verdicts.** A verdict row per question would be 251 rows to say "fine" 239 times. Instead: a 12-row exclusion table (0062) + a **reviewed-scope registry in TS** (`REVIEWED_CHAPTERS`, pinned by chapter **UUID** — the /notes+/guide name-joins are exactly what rots on a rename). This keeps **three states distinct**, which is the whole point: *passed* · *dropped* · *never looked at*. Collapsing the third into "passed" would hand students unvetted questions; into "dropped" would empty the bank. `fitCoverage()` surfaces it as an inline caveat instead of silence. It also matches the direction rulings actually move: the user loosened twice mid-session (trace IN, then 2×2 Cayley-Hamilton + nilpotent IN), and **loosening is mechanical here** — delete the rows carrying that `blocking_tool`.
  - **Verdicts judge what a question DEMANDS, not what our stored solution USES — and a keyword screen would over-reject ~3×.** `5c5efe8a` is solved in the bank via eigenvectors, but `(A−I)B = 0` with `B ≠ 0` forces `det(A−I) = 0` ⇒ `ad−bc = a+d−1`; `b29d23c8`/`2d6ac41f` are solved "by similarity" but `det(P⁻¹MP) = det M` follows from `det(AB)=det(A)det(B)` alone. All pass. This is why the screen cannot be automated.
  - **Surface + tooling:** `/browse` gains a **Syllabus fit** segmented control (`All JEE · NDA+CET · Excluded`) rendered **only when the exam is JEE Mains** — meaningless elsewhere, since a question already belongs to its own exam's students. The `Excluded` view exists so the teacher can audit my calls. `scripts/relevance/commit.ts` (dry-run default, validates every `blocking_tool` against `BLOCKING_TOOLS` and every id against the bank before writing, idempotent upsert) + `data/<screen>.json`. **Screening a new chapter needs BOTH steps** — commit its exclusions AND append the chapter to `REVIEWED_CHAPTERS`; the script prints the reminder. Verified live on `/browse` (251 / 239 / 12; bank-wide delta exactly 12; control hidden off-JEE; unscoped caveat renders) — the build alone proves nothing for a `ƒ` route. See [[syllabus-fit-screen]] + [[spec-narrower-than-artifact]].

- **2026-07-27 — Answer-key cross-check of 11 shipped MH-SSC-10 papers against the OFFICIAL solutions bundled inside their own source PDFs: 295 q → 279 AGREE · 14 BOOK-WRONG · 1 NO-BOOK-ANSWER · 1 BOTH-DEFENSIBLE · **0 OURS-WRONG** (merged `0853039`).** These papers shipped under the pipeline's "board QPs never ship an answer key" assumption, so every MCQ key was AI-DERIVED and every subjective answer AUTHORED, all `reviewFlag:true` awaiting a human check that never happened. A text-layer scan of all 69 Class-10 source PDFs found a `"Board Paper – <year> Solution"` section in **18** — the publisher's reproductions bundle the official key. 11 of those are already-ingested papers (Algebra 2016-2019, Geometry 2016-2019, Science I 2016/2018/2019); the Algebra/Geometry **2020** hits were FALSE POSITIVES (an inline `Solution:` belonging to a *complete-the-activity* question), so their answers stay derived.
  - **The finding is the asymmetry, not the count: not one of our 295 stored answers needed changing, while the publisher's key is wrong 14×.** Auto-applying the printed key would have CORRUPTED 14 correct answers. The brief's load-bearing rule — **the printed key is a PEER, not an ORACLE** — did the work; so did its corollary, *where a printed worked solution contradicts its printed final answer, the working is the better evidence* (3 of geo-2017's 4 errors are exactly that shape, self-evident from internal contradiction alone). Sharpest: **geo-2017 Q4(ii)** where the printed solution **fabricates its coefficients** (`4x = 12cosec+12cot` when multiplying gives `+16cot`) so the cot terms cancel — correct identity `(3x+4y)²−(4x−3y)²=625`, and the printed one fails numerically at θ=30°; **alg-2019 Q5(ii)** adds the two filling TIMES (times don't add, rates do) and is self-refuting; **alg-2017 Q3(ii)** factorises `3x²−12x` as `3x(x−12)`, and substituting its root 12 gives 208≠0.
  - **Applied: 14 `[Textbook answer-key error:]` + 2 `[Textbook note:]` brackets + 1 wording repair — all SOLUTION-ONLY, therefore hash-neutral by construction** (`solution` feeds neither `contentHash` nor `subjectiveContentHash`), so no recompute and no collision risk; verified idempotent. New `scripts/mh-ssc-10/`: `CROSSCHECK_BRIEF.md`, `dump-answers.ts` (**refuses to dump a paper with unanswered rows** — a cross-check against empty answers reports a meaningless all-AGREE, the bug that wasted a State-Board pass), `apply-errata.ts`, per-paper `data/<id>.crosscheck.json`.
  - **Two hazards banked into the brief mid-run, each of which would otherwise have manufactured false OURS-WRONG findings:** (1) **the PDF text layer silently DROPS MINUS SIGNS inside math zones** (a printed `x = −2` extracts as `x = 2`; also dropped from coordinates and determinants) → never call a sign disagreement without rendering the page; (2) **`render.ts` `rmSync`s the paper's `out/` dir** before rendering, destroying any `text.md`/`answers.md` dumps sitting there. Also: a Unicode match string (`∠`) silently found nothing where the DB stores LaTeX (`\angle`) and reported "already correct" — verify a no-op before believing it.
  - **Coverage gap found, NOT fixed (logged in SUGGESTIONS):** geo-2016 and geo-2018 are each missing their `Q5(ii)` construction sub-question (stored refs jump `Q5(i)`→`Q5(iii)`); geo-2017 and geo-2019 have theirs, so it is 2 specific questions, not systematic. Source + official solution both in hand; adding them is purely additive. See [[mh-ssc-10-ingestion]] + [[audit-probe-symmetry]].

- **2026-07-27 — Two student reports on a Class-10 Statistics question traced to a literal `\n`, which uncovered a year-old render-contract drift in the Word exporter (merged `cd97550`, `7bf32b9`).** The reports read as "typo-or-formatting"; the actual cause was that the stem and solution stored a **two-character literal `\n`** where line breaks belonged, so `parseTableBlocks` never saw line structure and the GFM pipe-table collapsed into raw pipes. Both are Statistics questions whose **data IS the table**, so they were unanswerable. Content was verified correct against the source PDF (values, structure, answers all match) ⇒ a meaning-preserving unescape, **not** a re-transcription.
  - **Root cause was two failures compounding:** the alg-2020 transcription agent double-escaped its newlines when writing its JSON (the committed source file is itself wrong — 23 of 27 rows, zero real newlines), and **`scripts/mh-ssc-10/commit.ts` never called `normalizeNewlines`** (7 of 12 commit pipelines didn't). Fixed at the choke point: `commitStaged` now **rejects** rather than silently normalises — see the write-boundary convention + [[reject-dont-normalise-when-hash-upstream]].
  - **The bigger defect the report uncovered:** `parseTableBlocks` was wired into the docx **stem** and **context** paths but never the **solution** path, so a table in any solution printed as raw pipes in every downloaded answer key — **123 PUBLIC rows bank-wide**, 5× the reported blast radius. The web renderer had the identical bug and was fixed 2026-07-06; the exporter was never brought along. Fixed via `solutionBlocks` + a **render-CONTRACT golden test** asserting the same fixture through both surfaces.
  - **Repaired 24 rows** (22 alg-2020 + 2 State Board Mathematical Logic), 4 collision-guarded `content_hash` recomputes, **0 collisions**; source JSONs fixed in the same pass so re-commit stays idempotent. Then `npm run audit:text` (new standing probe) found a **second, unrelated class** on its first run — 7 PUBLIC rows with pipe rows and no `|---|` separator — repaired 2026-07-27 with **two different remedies for one flag class**: the MHT-CET band-gap row is a real table missing its separator (restored), but the 6 Pariksha rows are label→text statement lists with **no header row**, where adding a separator would promote "Statement I" into a table HEADER — those were **linearised** per the NDA-Statistics house style (240 of its 241 rows linearise rather than tabulate). Bank-wide now 0/0.
  - **The probe took three attempts and the first two would have reported wrong numbers** — banked in [[stem-render-corruption-probes]]: Postgres `LIKE` treats backslash as its own escape char so `'%\n%'` silently matches the letter *n*; naive matching then flagged every `\neq`/`\nu`/`\nabla` (hundreds of false positives, incl. two matrix row-separators nearly reported as bugs); and excluding "followed by a letter" hid `\nThe`. Correct test: a backslash-n that is neither the tail of a `\\` pair nor followed by a **lowercase** letter.

- **2026-07-26 — MH-SSC-10 Science and Technology II COMPLETE: back-years 2016-2022 ingested (+204 q), the subject now spans all 10 available years (merged `07ac2db`).** Papers 2016 (24) · 2017 (34) · 2018 (24) · 2019 (41) · 2020 (41) · 2022 (40) → Science II 338 q / 337 PUBLIC / 53 figures.
  - **The load-bearing discovery: these back-years are NOT raster scans like the rest of the corpus — they are born-digital TYPESET reproductions with a real text layer** (4.7k–12.8k chars), so transcription is **hybrid** (text layer = ground truth for wording/options/numbering; rendered pages still needed for figures, flow-charts, boxed activities, reading order). New `scripts/mh-ssc-10/dump-text.ts` + a README rule: **always check for a text layer before assuming vision-only** (~0 chars ⇒ scan; a few thousand ⇒ hybrid).
  - **Source PDFs are BUNDLES and the bundling is unreliable — verify per file:** 2016/2017/2019 bundle their OWN official solution (so those keys are source-verified, not derived), but **`Science_II_2018.pdf` bundles the *Paper I 2017* solution** — wrong subject AND wrong year — and only 2 of its 8 pages are the question paper (marks reconcile to 40, so complete at 24 q).
  - **2016-2019 are OLD-syllabus:** Paper II was not Biology-only (chemistry half + the dropped *Control and Co-ordination* chapter) and the **MCQ block is Q1(B), inverted** from the modern layout; the Science II catalog gained 4 old-syllabus chapters following the identical block already established for Science I. `sci2-2017 Q3(1)` is held **PRIVATE** — it says "label the following diagram" and no such diagram exists anywhere in the PDF.
  - **All 30 new figure crops montage-verified individually**, which exposed a real tool limitation now in `scripts/lib/figures/README.md`: **snapCrop's `INK = 165` threshold cannot see pale art**, so it clips pastel line-work and light-gray blank chart boxes **while still reporting `ok`** — because `ok` only means the anchors sat in whitespace gaps, not that the crop is complete. Two agents hit it independently on different papers; one lost an entire blank box the question was asking about. See [[figure-snapcrop-verify]] + [[mh-ssc-10-ingestion]].

- **2026-07-25 — JEE Mains 2026 Maths ingested: all 19 Jan+April per-shift sittings → +475 q PUBLIC (380 MCQ + 95 NAT); JEE Maths 3,202 → 3,677.** A fresh `PYQs\2026\` folder (19 QP+`_ak` pairs) appeared. Ran the **SAFE lane** (the 2026 `_ak` files are full worked-solution docs with the answer key as the leading `**(x)**` token + clean sequential 1–75 numbering — the ideal number-matched case): per file `extract --numeric --layout2025` → `dump-maths` → 1 classify+VERIFY agent/file (25 Maths q each: classify into the live taxonomy + INDEPENDENTLY re-derive every key) → `assemble-safe` → `commit --subject=Maths` → `attach-solutions` → `cleanup-latex` → `scan-flip --flip`. Pilot-first (24 Jan S1), then batched the rest as background agents. **The load-bearing structural difference from 2021–2025: 2026 sittings are PER-SHIFT files of 75 q** (Physics 1–25 · Chem 26–50 · **Maths 51–75 = 20 MCQ + 5 NAT**), not the two-shift 180-q date files; `--layout2025` forces `shiftSize=75` so a standalone 75-block file maps Q51–75 → Maths correctly.
  - **5 genuine source answer-key errors caught + corrected by the SAFE verification** (apr05-s2 Q54 C→B, Q57 C→B, Q67 B→C; apr06-s2 Q58 C→B; apr05-s1 Q54 A→B) — in **every** case the official key LETTER contradicted the answer key's OWN worked solution (e.g. apr05-s2 Q67's AK literally writes "3 points of intersection" but marks (b)=2; apr06-s2 Q58's AK derives "x²−41x+420=0" but marks (c)). Adjudicated each with three independent grounds — my own derivation + the agent's + the AK's printed worked solution — before flipping (reconfirms [[audit-probe-symmetry]]: the AK key letter is a peer, not an oracle; the AK's own worked solution is the tiebreaker). All other 14 sittings: 0 key disagreements. Final **`audit:keys Maths 2026` = 380 MCQ, 0 flagged**; all 475 rows KaTeX-clean.
  - **Two durable pipeline fixes folded in (net-new work, no 360 needed):** (1) **trailing-backslash key parser** — 2026 pandoc renders the key as `**(c)\**` (a hard-break `\` before the closing `**`) on ~5–10 rows/file, which `parseAnswerTokens`/`SOLUTION_START`'s `\)\*\*` didn't match → those keys silently dropped to `no_answer_key`. Added an optional `\\?` before `\*\*` in both regexes (`scripts/jee/lib.ts`) — a **strict superset** (2021–2025 blocks without the `\` still match, verified by typecheck + 61 green tests incl. a new regression case). Recovered nearly all keys (per-file `no_answer_key` dropped ~30→5 across the batch), so `assemble-safe` could cross-check the agent vs the source key rather than blind-trust the derivation. (2) **stale-taxonomy guard** — the pipeline's `out/_maths_taxonomy.txt` handout predated the 2026-07-24 chapter reshape, so agents classified into RETIRED names (Integrals/Compound Angles/Trigonometry/Continuity and Differentiability) → commit would have re-created the dead chapters. **Regenerated the handout from the live DB** + added a per-file classification validator (checks every `(chapter,subtopic)` against a live-taxonomy JSON before commit, aborts on any mismatch). Net: JEE Maths chapters stayed at **27, 0 duplicates created, 0 empty**. **Lesson: always regenerate the agent classification handout from the live DB before an agent-classification batch — a stale handout silently re-creates renamed chapters** (a taxonomy-drift cousin of [[shipped-chapter-rename-downstream-sync]]).
  - **Edge cases:** 21 Jan S1's answer key was a **PDF** (pandoc can't OMML-extract) → parsed the `N. (letter)` tokens from the PDF text layer (PyMuPDF), pandoc-built a minimal `**(x)**` soln docx from them so the standard `extract` picked up the keys natively (all independently confirmed by the verifier). Two **duplicate-option-label** OCR defects (apr05-s1 Q52, jan21-s1 Q70: two `(b)`/`(d)`, no `(c)`) recovered via `optionOverrides` (all 4) + `answerOverride`. Two OCR-garbled set-builder stems (apr06-s1 Q53, jan23-s2 Q55) reconstructed via `stemOverrides` + `resync`. **Ops:** a mid-batch account **session limit** killed 3 agents (apr05-s1/apr06-s2/apr08-s2) — re-dispatched after reset; the memory's ≤3-concurrent-agent caution held only loosely (ran ~6–8, hit the limit at ~14 completions). Not yet committed at time of this entry. See [[jee-mains-ingestion]].

- **2026-07-25 — `audit:keys` structural probe extended to the JEE `pyq` bank as a backstop for the "should we formally audit JEE Maths?" question — 0 wrong keys across 2,642 MCQs, so no formal deep-read warranted.** The JEE Maths corpus (3,202 q) is COMPLETE but sits OUTSIDE the formal 9,546-q content audit; the question was whether to fold it in. Reasoned NO to a full deep-read (the two-lane ingest already re-derived SAFE keys and BLIND-solved broken ones — stronger than the "no-source-needed" audit's core work), YES to the cheap `audit:keys` structural backstop, which had never touched JEE (it was practice-MCQ-scoped). New **`scripts/jee/audit-keys.ts`** (scopes the probe to `exam_id`=JEE + subject + `question_kind='pyq'`, MCQ-only; reuses the practice `auditRow` core — added a `require.main===module` guard so the core is importable without firing the practice `main()`). **Result: scanned all 2,642 JEE pyq MCQs (Maths 2,128 · Physics 254 · Chemistry 260; the 963 Section-B NAT can't be structurally probed) → ZERO wrong answer keys.** JEE Maths → "structurally probed, clean." Flags all characterized as non-key-errors: (a) all 6 `SOLN≠KEY` were probe FALSE POSITIVES — `concludedLetter`'s `Hence…([A-Da-d])` matched the first letter of the WORD after "Hence" ("Hence **c**ontinuous/**b**oth/**b**akelite/**c**ut-off"); **fixed durably** by tightening `concludedLetter` in `scripts/practice/audit-keys.ts` with a trailing `(?=[)\.,;:\s]|$)` lookahead (a genuine "(B)"/"B."/"B is" conclusion always ends in a delimiter, so it's a strict FP reduction — re-verified the practice probe still fires 7 real `SOLN≠KEY`, no true-positive loss). (b) The 2 Maths `DUP_OPT` (Q71 2022-Jun27 = c/d both `2−log₂3`; Q152 2024-Apr08 = b/c both `25`) are **FAITHFUL reproductions of source-side duplicate options** — pandoc'd both source docx and the dup is IN the source; answer present + correctly keyed → no fix (fabricating a distractor would violate no-fabrication; the docx is the source of record — the [[keep-duplicates-recurrence-signal]] carve-out). (c) The remaining `DUP_OPT` (Physics 10, Chemistry 47, all legacy-2021) are an extraction-COMPLETENESS gap — mostly organic-structure questions whose OPTIONS ARE IMAGES never transcribed (empty option text → flagged as dup) — in the deliberately-skipped Physics/Chemistry subjects, NOT key errors. See [[jee-mains-ingestion]] + [[audit-probe-symmetry]].

- **2026-07-25 — Bank-wide PRACTICE answer-key + duplicate-option audit (triggered by an LWS Maths Mock 5 solution review) + a new permanent `audit:keys` probe — 151 practice questions corrected, all source-verified.** Reviewing the *LWS Maths Mock 5* draft paper (120 NDA-Maths *practice* MCQ) found 9 wrong-key/garbled questions — **all transcription damage, NOT book defects**: the vision ingest ([[practice-pdf-vision-ingestion]]) had scrambled option ORDER (so the book's answer-key *letter* pointed at the wrong VALUE), corrupted a stem symbol (`sinθ`→`sin18°`), and for one stored fabricated option values. Fixed via `scripts/practice/apply-source-fixes.ts` (stem/option-text edits + key move + collision-guarded `content_hash` recompute), then synced the committed transcriptions (option/solution edits + universal key overrides in `overrides.json`) so re-ingest is idempotent (**140/140 `content_hash` match verified**). **The systemic finding drove a bank-wide sweep:** a **zero-LLM Tier-1 probe** (solution-concludes-a-different-letter-than-key · duplicate options · not-exactly-one-correct) over the whole NDA-Maths practice bank flagged 159; **10 parallel agents source-verified every book-sourced flag** → **140 fixes** (66 option · 30 key · 19 stem · 15 solution · 7 struct-collapse) + 3 user-approved book-key overrides (book self-consistently wrong, math-correct with `[Textbook note]`) → bank flag count **159 → 17 faithful/multi-answer residual**. **~12 of the 30 key-fixes were genuine book answer-key errors** the book contradicts itself on (caught by its own worked solution) — the rest were the option-scramble. The probe was promoted to a permanent **`scripts/practice/audit-keys.ts` / `npm run audit:keys`** (MCQ-scoped; pure `concludedLetter`/`auditRow` helpers) so this class is caught going forward. Running it bank-wide surfaced **90 more** in the OTHER practice pipelines (Foundation/Pariksha/State-Board); source-verified all 90 → **ZERO wrong answer keys** — **54 were genuine Foundation transcription duplicates** (a distractor carried another option's exact text; restored the real distractor from the worksheet PDFs + patched transcriptions, format-preserved) and the rest were **FAITHFUL reproductions of source-side duplicate options** (the worksheet/book/coaching-PDF itself prints two identical options — Pariksha ×5, State Board ×2 verified against the Balbharati page, some Foundation) → no fix. 6 Foundation answer-correctness issues (wrong *derived* keys / a missing LWS-mock source) left flagged for a separate pass (SUGGESTIONS). Merges through `495c3b9`. See [[practice-pdf-vision-ingestion]] + [[audit-probe-symmetry]].

- **2026-07-24 — JEE Mains Maths taxonomy reshaped to match its cleaned siblings: 24 → 27 chapters (3 structural fixes), 0 data loss.** The JEE Maths chapters were **auto-created by the ingestion agents** (the ~79 JEE auto-created chapters are tracked separately, NOT in the formal 13-subject cleanup register). A three-way comparison against the *cleaned* NDA (31 ch) + MHT-CET (27 ch) Maths taxonomies showed JEE was **slightly UNDER-split with three mis-drawn boundaries**, not over-split. **Note: taxonomy is per-exam** — the three exams intentionally use different naming conventions (NDA `&`/abbrev, CET full-words + roman splits, JEE NCERT-style), so the goal was **structural parity, not literal renaming**. The three fixes (all pure `execute_sql` data reclassification — chapters/subtopics are rows, not schema):
  1. **Integration cluster → 3 chapters** (matching both siblings, which split indefinite/definite): renamed `Integrals`→**Definite Integration** (169); created **Indefinite Integration** (39) from the mislabeled "Integration by Substitution" subtopic — a LaTeX probe confirmed that subtopic was ~entirely *indefinite* (`\int … dx +c`, no limits), so the split was clean; a per-question scan then moved 5 genuinely-definite stems (real limits `\int_{a}^{b}`) back to Definite (`\int_{}^{}` empty-limits = indefinite, a false-positive guarded against). `Application of Integrals` (98) left as-is.
  2. **Trigonometry cluster re-carved to NDA's model**: `Compound Angles`→**Trigonometric Identities** (155, absorbing the scattered *Trigonometric Identities* + *Inequalities* subtopics from the old jumbled `Trigonometry` chapter); `Trigonometry`→**Trigonometric Equations** (45); new **Properties of Triangle** (11) + **Height & Distance** (15) carved out; `Inverse Trigonometric Functions` (62) untouched. ("Compound Angles" was a non-standard chapter name = just trig identities.)
  3. **Limits/Differentiation boundary fixed** (the boundary was mis-drawn — continuity orphaned): moved the *Continuity* subtopic (35) into `Limits and Continuity` (now Limits 85 + Continuity 35 = 120, matching NDA's *Limits & Continuity*); renamed `Continuity and Differentiability`→**Differentiation** (70).
  - **Blast radius near-zero, verified**: JEE Maths has no `/guide`, `/notes`, mocks, or concept/principle tags — a `grep src/` for the old chapter-name literals hit only NDA/CET *notes* files (their own taxonomy), zero JEE refs; the only surface that changed is the `/browse` filter dropdown labels. **Integrity (SQL, the real gate for a data reclassification): 3,202 q preserved exactly · 0 empty chapters · 0 chapter/subtopic mismatches · 0 null subtopics.** No code diff → no lint/test gate run (would only exercise unchanged code against the shared prod DB during the live sync-test churn observed this session). **Method reused**: the reclassification pattern (create targets → reparent subtopics AND their questions' `chapter_id` via CTE → verify `(chapter_id,name)` collisions + 0-mismatch → verify no empties) — see [[reclassification-sql-pattern]] + [[fk-set-null-on-subtopic-drop]]. **Deferred**: a stale MHT-CET `__sync_test_9590c572__` chapter (2 `happy` questions, created *today*) was NOT swept — its 5-q sibling cascade-vanished between two queries seconds apart = **live/recent sync-test churn**, which `global-teardown` reaps by org-cascade; sweep only if it survives the next full `npm test` ([[test-data-leak-org-signal]]). See [[jee-mains-ingestion]].

- **2026-07-24 — Post-ingest RENDER-CORRUPTION audit of the pandoc/BLIND-ingested bank (report-triggered): 3 JEE reports resolved + 4 corruption classes swept, ~290 rows fixed, all text-only.** A `/dashboard/reports` KaTeX render error (*"Can't use function '\\(' in math mode"*) on a JEE Conics question opened a systemic audit. **Four render-corruption CLASSES, each with a mechanical SQL probe** (except the last), rooted in imperfect source DOCX (OCR) + the BLIND/pandoc ingest — full probe library in [[stem-render-corruption-probes]]:
  1. **Scrambled `$`/`\\(` delimiter mix** — a stray `$` opens a `$…$` zone that swallows prose + inner `\\(`/`\\)` (illegal nested → the reported KaTeX error). Probe: a field matching BOTH `\\$` and `\\\\(`. Whole-bank sweep → **3 rows** (JEE 2023 Apr11 Q177, JEE 2023 Feb01 Q175, MHT_CET 2025 Apr26 Q94); rebalanced to `\\(…\\)`.
  2. **Plain-text escaped-underscore blank `\\_\\_\\_\\_`** — a fill-in answer-blank whose underscores were backslash-escaped. In PLAIN text `\\_` renders literally (broken), but INSIDE a math zone `\\(\\_\\_\\_\\_\\)` it renders correctly (valid in KaTeX math mode) → **only plain-text ones are broken**. Probe: strip `\\(…\\)` zones first (`regexp_replace(text,'\\\\\\(.*?\\\\\\)','','g')`) THEN test `~ '\\\\_'`. **269 rows** (JEE numeric answer-blanks); trailing run stripped, 49 math-zone blanks left untouched.
  3. **Truncated stem opening** (dropped lead-in) — probe: trimmed stem starts lowercase (`~ '^[a-z]'`). **15 rows** (e.g. *"of the planes…"*, *"et O be the origin"*); each lead-in source-verified from its docx via pandoc + cross-checked against the stored solution.
  4. **Mid-stem dropped symbol** (`PQ=kI₃`→`k₃`, `AQ²⁰²¹`→`A²⁰²¹`) — a symbol vanishes inside a well-formed math zone; **NOT mechanically detectable** → report-driven (all 3 JEE reports were class 3 or 4).
  **The load-bearing fix rule: the source DOCX is OFTEN ALSO corrupted** — 2 of the 3 reports traced to the docx itself (`PQ=k_3` and the dropped `Q` were in the source; several truncations start lowercase / `et` / `form` in the source too). So the stored **SOLUTION + the known official question** are the ground truth, not the source: pandoc-verify to recover STRUCTURE, then let the solution pin the values. All edits `text`-only → `content_hash` goes stale (harmless; dedup only bites on re-ingest). Class 4 stays report-triaged (user's call — no heuristic sweep). Reconfirms [[render-bug-isolate-pipeline-and-probe]] (enumerate the class with a probe before fixing). Durable follow-up logged in SUGGESTIONS: a JEE-ingest lint flagging lowercase-start + `$`+`\\(` + plain-text `\\_` at commit.

- **2026-07-24 — JEE Mains Maths INGESTION COMPLETE: every sitting on disk 2021-2025 ingested (~40 sittings) — JEE 1,271 → 3,715 q PUBLIC (Maths 1,624 → 3,202, +1,578).** One autonomous run (across two account-session-limit interruptions, resumed at each reset) closed out all of 2022 (11 sittings), 2023 (13), 2024 (10), 2025 (9). **The core is a two-lane completion pipeline chosen per-paper by a numbering triage** (`grep -oE '^[0-9]+\.' out/<id>_soln.md | sort -u | wc -l` vs the extractor's "N ordered blocks for 180"):
  - **SAFE lane** (extracted key trustworthy: sequential/by-number distinct≈180, OR positional with exactly 180 ordered blocks): 2 agents/paper classify + author a solution to the given answer + INDEPENDENTLY re-derive each key, flagging disagreements → `assemble-safe.ts` (uses the source key; surfaces flags). **This caught 5 genuine source-key errors** (e.g. 2023 jan29 Q71 B→A + Q176 −6→4, jan31 Q153 C→D + Q172 84→5), all agent-verified + fixed via override.
  - **BLIND lane** (broken numbering: extractor finds ≪180 ordered blocks → positional keys are shifted; OR no soln docx at all): 2 agents/paper solve every question FROM SCRATCH, IGNORE the extracted key, and return the DERIVED answer → `assemble-blind.ts` (uses the derived answer, never the source key). **The blind solvers repeatedly confirmed the source NAT keys were positionally shifted (+1 or +2) and many MCQ keys wrong** — so blind-solve was the correct + necessary call, not a fallback. It also recovered the 2 previously-"deferred" papers (2023 apr11, 2025 apr04 — whose positional keys had been verified wrong) and the fully **keyless 2024 Jan-31** (no soln docx → extracted the QP using the QP itself as a dummy soln to get stems+options, then fully AI-derived, the CDS/pariksha keyless precedent).
  - **New durable infra (`scripts/jee/`):** `dump-maths.ts` (Maths dump for the agents), `assemble-safe.ts` / `assemble-blind.ts` (build `papers/<id>.json` from agent outputs), `sol-clean.ts` (`cleanSolution` = normalize double-escaped agent LaTeX via the `\\(` tell + strip a stray trailing `\)`), `scan-flip.js` (delim/`\n`/NO-SOLUTION scan → flip PUBLIC, `--except=N` keeps a row PRIVATE), and a **`paper.skip[]`** field in `PaperData` (config + commit) that drops an agent-flagged corrupted question BEFORE the classification requirement (so a committable "ok" row can be dropped without a "no classification" throw). **Bug fixed:** `isCommittable` treated a `numericOverride` of **0** as falsy → silently dropped a legit NAT-answer-0 row; now `!== undefined` (+4 regression tests). ~1-4 genuinely corrupted questions/paper skipped (truncated stem, or derived answer matches no printed option).
  - **De-glue gotcha (reusable):** `cleanup-latex`'s function-name de-glue turns an English word beginning with a trig/`\det`/`\log` name INSIDE `\text{}` into a LaTeX command (`singular`→`\sin gular`, `singles`→`\sin gles`) → KaTeX rejects it → `attach-solutions` skips the solution (NO-SOLUTION). Workaround: reword (avoid "singular/singles/cosine/determinant/logic/…" inside `\text{}`) — the blind-agent prompt now warns against it. A proper fix (don't de-glue when the function-name is followed by lowercase letters continuing a word) is deferred; the MHT-CET `cleanupArtifacts` de-glue shares the risk. See [[jee-mains-ingestion]].

- **2026-07-22/23 — JEE 2021 Maths COMPLETE: Section-B NAT backfilled across all 11 standard papers (1-10, 18) + multi-year source located — JEE 1,162 → 1,271 q PUBLIC (Maths NAT 90 → 199).** The standard papers had only Section-A MCQ (the original MCQ-only ingest); this backfilled their 10 Section-B NAT each (Q81-90). **The pipeline was already built for it** (the 17,19-26 work added `--numeric-only`): `extract --numeric` keeps Section B as `status:numeric` with the answer auto-parsed from the soln doc; `commit --subject=Maths --numeric-only` inserts ONLY the numeric rows (leaving the already-committed+cleaned MCQ untouched — re-committing them would recompute the hash from the raw extract and duplicate); the PRIVATE-flip is scoped to `question_format='numeric'` so PUBLIC MCQ stay online. `attach-solutions` gained the same `--numeric-only`.
  - **The NAT-backfill loop (reusable for 2022-2025):** `extract --numeric` → dump Q81-90 stems+answers → parallel agents classify (into the existing JEE Maths taxonomy, handed a `_taxonomy.txt`) + author a solution to the known answer, returning `{chapter, subtopic, solution, stem?}` (a `stem` field ONLY when the extracted stem is OCR-garbled) → merge into `papers/<id>.json` → `commit --numeric-only` → `attach-solutions --numeric-only` → `cleanup-latex` → `validate-db` → SQL flip numeric PUBLIC. 10 parallel agents (2 waves of 5) classified+solved 100 NAT; every derivation reproduced the official answer.
  - **Source keys are wrong for NAT more than for MCQ — re-derive every one:** p8 Q86 soln rounded the exact term `15/4` to `4` (overridden to **3.75** — `numeric_answer` takes decimals); p9 Q86's key was mis-shown as `(d)` (a letter for a NAT → derived 4). p9 Q84 kept **PRIVATE** — it references a figure absent from source ("missing value in the following figure"). ~12 OCR-garbled NAT stems (piecewise/matrix) fixed via `stemOverrides`.
  - **`cleanup-latex` gained a `preClean` step** repairing piecewise/matrix stems' hard-break artifacts (`\\)`→`\)`, `\\]`→`\]`, backslash-space-before-close) — folded in from the p24 recovery; NAT stems with `\begin{matrix}`/`cases` need it and `validate-db` catches any that slip (6 broke on first commit → preClean fixed all → 0 broken). **A pre-commit KaTeX validator that only checks the *corrected* stems misses the raw extracted ones** — validate the DB, not just the overrides.
  - **A pure-data change broke a green test** ([[data-addition-breaks-test-invariant]], new): `browse-query`'s anon test assumed page 1 (newest, `created_at desc`) holds a 4-option MCQ; the 110 new 0-option NAT made the first MCQ position 111, so page 1 was all 0-option → red. Fixed by scoping the option-shape check to the MCQ-only MHT-CET seed. **The big discovery:** the JEE source **moved + expanded** — `C:\tmp\PYQPs\JEE_Mains\` is gone; the live source is **`C:\Vilas\LWS_Pune\JEE_Mains\PYQs\{2021..2025}`** with **all 5 years** (69 sittings: 2021×26 + 2022×11 + 2023×13 + 2024×10 + 2025×9), so "all years" is now a real ~2,000-question program. Compilations 11-12 confirmed **NAT-free** (pure MCQ). Commits `05a8c62`+`1abc4bc`+`94e8b11` on `main`. **RESUME: compilations 13-16, then 2022-2025 Maths (pilot one 2022 date-named sitting first).** See [[jee-mains-ingestion]].

- **2026-07-22 — JEE Mains 2021 Maths PYQ (Papers 17, 19-26) COMPLETE + the bank's first Section-B NAT format — JEE 771 → 1,162 q PUBLIC; merged `43fe79b`.** A strategy pivot from the full-paper June ingest to **Maths-only** ingestion (branch `jee-2021-maths-pyq`) that also added **Section B numeric-answer (NAT) questions** — previously skipped as out-of-schema. Each Maths paper = 30 q (20 Section-A MCQ Q61-80 + 10 Section-B NAT Q81-90). **NAT format (migration 0061):** `numeric` added to the `question_format` enum + `questions.numeric_answer`; NAT rows carry zero options, hash via `numericContentHash`, and render the numeric answer on `/browse` + the docx answer key. All 9 papers PUBLIC, **270/270 with solutions**.
  - **Recovery-after-loss (the session's trigger):** p17 + p24 data files were live in the DB but **lost from git** (never committed) after a technical issue, and their 60 Maths solutions were null. The DB was intact — recovery was git-commit + solution backfill, not re-ingestion. **Lesson: the durable per-paper artifact is `papers/<id>.json` (committed), NOT the DB or the gitignored `out/` — commit each paper's data file at ship, or a crash loses the authoring even when the rows survive.**
  - **The "all-`1.`" positional solution recovery (reusable — reconfirms + sharpens the Paper 8 case):** p17 + p24's soln docs had every block renumbered `1.` by pandoc, so the extractor keyed no Maths solution. Recovery = split the soln doc into ORDERED blocks (ignore the broken numbers, use sequence index), find the per-paper offset (p24 was uniformly **+1** — one missing Physics/Chem block → 89 total), then **cross-check every recovered block's leading answer token against the already-stored `answerOverrides`/`numericOverrides` (30/30) to PROVE block-position == question-position BEFORE writing.** p24: 27 source solutions recovered (KaTeX-repaired: hard-break `\\)`, leading empty zones, unicode underbrace) + 3 authored; p17's Maths bodies were empty (answer-key-only doc) → 30 authored fresh via parallel agents, every derivation reproducing the pre-verified official key. 4 OCR-garbled stems fixed from source or self-consistent reconstruction (p24 Q65 dropped-`p`, p17 Q82 restored-from-source, p17 Q70 + p25 Q81 reconstructed-to-match-key, p21 Q86 "intersection of normals"). validate-db: 0 KaTeX-broken, 0 leaks.
  - **`--no-verify` after a fresh manual gate PASS (reconfirms [[feedback-env-failure-vs-gate-failure]]):** the pre-push hook re-runs the ~4-min gate and the push timed out at 2 min. Since `npm run gate` had just printed `GATE_RESULT: PASS` on the identical tree, pushed branch + main with `--no-verify` (CI backstops). **RESUME (updated):** compilations 13-16, other subjects of 17+24 + clean 19-26, then 2022-2025 (not yet sourced). See [[jee-mains-ingestion]].

- **2026-07-22 — Public-quiz lead gate now SUPPRESSED for known visitors: signed-in students skip it entirely (auto-grade, NO lead); anon same-number returners get one-tap sticky consent (branch `feat/quiz-gate-known-visitors`, merged to main).** The `/quiz/[slug]` funnel showed its name+mobile+consent sheet to everyone on every submit. Scoped + built a "known-visitor" suppression (AskUserQuestion settled 3 product calls): **signed-in → skip the sheet, grade only, write NO `quiz_leads` row** (they're already a known account — a deliberate lead-reduction: their quiz activity won't appear in `/dashboard/leads`); **anon returner (localStorage identity) → keep the sheet but remember consent** so a same-number reveal is a single tap; anon first-timer → unchanged.
  - **The load-bearing constraint + technique (sharpens [[signup-gate-placement]] — the INVERSE of gate-placement is gate-SUPPRESSION):** the page is ISR-static and the answer key is only ever revealed by the submit POST (never shipped to the static page). So "auto-grade with no sheet" required an **authenticated grade-only branch in the SAME capture endpoint**, not shipping the key: `POST /api/public-quiz/submit` now reads `getSessionUser()` (wrapped in try/catch → anon on throw, mirroring `/api/export`; the route is already dynamic so the ISR page is untouched) and, when signed-in, validates only `{slug, answers}`, grades, returns, and **skips `recordLead`**. The page-level skip/continue/form decision is client-side via `useSignedIn()` (keeps ISR, the notes-gate precedent). **Rule: to suppress a capture gate for a known visitor, decide the skip CLIENT-side (preserve ISR) but keep the server-gated secret behind an authed branch of the same POST — never move the secret to the page.**
  - **Files:** new pure `src/lib/quiz/gate.ts` (`resolveQuizGate({signedIn,stored})→skip|continue|form` + `priorConsentValid`; owns the `StoredIdentity` type, now with `consentedAt`) + `tests/quiz-gate.test.ts` (7, TDD); `src/app/quiz/[slug]/QuizTaker.tsx` (adds `useSignedIn`, a `GatingSpinner` while auth loads, an `AutoGrade` component for the skip path, sticky consent in `Gate`, `consentedAt` stamped on save); `src/app/api/public-quiz/submit/route.ts` (auth branch). `qb:lead:v1` localStorage gained `consentedAt`. **No migration.**
  - **Verified:** typecheck clean · targeted lint clean · new gate tests green · the anon `public-quiz-submit` integration test still records a lead (anon path byte-identical) · **build green** (`/quiz/[slug]` still static). The 2 DB-integration files flaked under the gate's parallel load (404 = fixture-missing, the [[shared-db-test-flake]] pattern; passed isolated + on a clean full re-run). **Owed:** the authed skip + one-tap paths have no headless render — needs a signed-in browser click-through (a signed-in quiz submit creates no lead; an anon returner gets one-tap).

- **2026-07-22 — CBSE Class 12 Maths Ch.3 Matrices (115 q) + Ch.4 Determinants (92 q) ingested + PUBLIC via `scripts/ncert/` (merged `def23d8` + `f700208`).** Two more NCERT chapters through the proven vision pipeline (render → per-exercise vision agents ≤3/batch → merge → commit PRIVATE → blind MCQ re-derive + AK cross-check → dump-tosolve → 3 subtopic authoring agents → apply-solutions + `.mcq-verify.json` → sections.ts outline + backfill + `board:lint` + order_index → flip-public --with-mcq). **Matrices:** 25 solved + 79 subjective + 11 MCQ, 7 subtopics; **Determinants:** 19 solved + 66 subjective + 7 MCQ, 5 subtopics (the rationalised edition folds "Properties" into §4.2, so 5 not 6). **0 answer-key errata on either** — all 18 MCQs blind-re-derived AND cross-checked against `lemh1an.pdf` (18/18), all 145 subjective answers AK-verified by the authoring agents; NCERT's key is clean (Integrals had 2). Full detail + the reusable per-chapter runbook in [[ncert-ingestion]]. Durable lessons banked this session: (1) **an exercise's "Choose the correct answer" MCQ tail can spill past the exercise header onto the next page** — a transcription block cut at the header silently drops it (Matrices Ex 3.1 Q8–10 landed on the §3.4 page); list each block's items EXPLICITLY and tell each agent to capture the whole exercise incl. its MCQ tail. (2) **Parallel agents self-report their own row counts UNRELIABLY** (off by 1–3 every batch) — always diff authored ids vs the tosolve/expected id set, never trust the summary. (3) **The rationalised NCERT "Reprint 2025-26" trims exercises** (Matrices Ex 3.4 is now a single MCQ; §3.7's worked examples became "Miscellaneous Examples") — transcribe what's actually printed, don't assume the classic structure. (4) Two gate/push traps reconfirmed with new evidence: chaining `tail <gate.log> && git merge` merges on `tail`'s exit code not the gate result ([[gate-exit-code-masked-by-tail]]), and the pre-push `next build` worker OOM'd with **exit 134 (SIGABRT)** after a standalone `GATE_RESULT: PASS` → env failure → pushed `--no-verify`, CI backstops ([[feedback-env-failure-vs-gate-failure]]).

- **2026-07-21 — Downloaded Word papers rendered set-complement math as raw LaTeX; fixed with a construct-agnostic prettify fallback + an `audit:omml` probe (export-path only, website untouched) — merged `db2184f`.** A teacher-reported "rendering issue" on a downloaded paper traced NOT to the web renderer (KaTeX renders it fine — **web and docx are SEPARATE math paths**; always confirm WHICH path a render bug is on) but to the **docx export**: `mml2omml` (unmaintained, latest 0.5.0) THROWS on temml's MathML for **a superscript applied to a `\cap`/`\cup` group** — set-complement expressions like `(A∪B)'`, `(A∪B)^c`, `n(A∩B)^2`. The trigger is NOT prime-specific (`+`/`\cdot` groups are fine; a group with no superscript is fine; `(x^2+y)'` works but `(A∩B)^2` crashes — obscure mml2omml internals). `latexToOmml` caught the throw → returned null → the exporter emitted the **raw `\(...\)` LaTeX as text**. **Probe-first paid off:** building the enumerator BEFORE fixing showed the class is broader than my first (prime-only) hypothesis — a prime fix would have shipped the `^c`/`^C`/`^2` siblings still broken. MathML-surgery fixes proved fragile (stripping the `tml-prime` class / `mo`→`mi` still throws), so the fix is a **construct-agnostic graceful fallback**: `prettifyMathFallback` (`src/lib/export/ommlBuilder.ts`) turns any OMML-unconvertible zone into readable Unicode (`(A ∪ B)ᶜ`) instead of raw markup — it NEVER restructures math (no complement-vs-derivative-vs-exponent guessing, since `'` is complement in sets but derivative in calculus), so it can't corrupt math; an unmapped macro is left verbatim (never worse than today). Paired with **`npm run audit:omml`** (`findOmmlFailures` in `src/lib/export/ommlAudit.ts` + `scripts/audit-omml.ts`) which scans the bank → `generated-papers/omml-sweep.md`, so a FUTURE mml2omml crash surfaces at ingest, not when a teacher opens a broken paper (first run: **20 zones / 12 PUBLIC questions**, all NDA/State-Board set theory). Export-path only → web untouched; validated on all 20 real zones (0 backslash-macros remain); 18 new tests; full gate PASS. **A fixed zone STILL shows in `audit:omml`** (conversion still fails; only the fallback improved) — the probe measures convertibility, not fallback quality. **Owed:** a manual Word-download eyeball (no headless docx render exists). Replacing/forking the unmaintained mml2omml is the eventual root fix (roadmap). See [[docx-omml-export-pipeline]] + [[render-bug-isolate-pipeline-and-probe]].

- **2026-07-20 — Superadmin-gated the platform-wide dashboard cards + routes (multi-tenancy leak surfaced by onboarding a 2nd org).** Onboarding **Arjunaa Foundation** (tenant #2, admin `careervvmhss@gmail.com`) exposed that `/dashboard` was gating every card on `role === "ADMIN"`, but **six of the surfaces read platform-wide, not org-scoped**, so a second-tenant admin was seeing LWS Pune's (and every org's) data. The split is entirely in the data layer: org-scoped cards use the cookie-bound server client (RLS scopes to `org_id`) — **Members, Branches, Batches, Papers, Question reports, Concept reports** (correct, untouched); the leaky ones use the **service-role admin client** with no `org_id` filter, because the data itself has no org dimension — students/entitlements/feedback/activity live on `auth.users` (no org), and mocks/quizzes are superadmin-authored *global* content. So there was no ignored `org_id` column to add — the fix is to move those to **superadmin-only**, matching the 0056 "platform-level = superadmin" model.
  - **Gated 9 routes** (redirect non-superadmins → `/browse`, mirroring `/superadmin`): `students`, `students/[id]`, `entitlements`, `feedback`, `activity`, `mocks`, `mocks/[slug]`, `quizzes`, **`leads`**. `/dashboard/leads` (quiz-funnel names + mobiles) had no card but was a reachable URL in the same platform-wide class — gated too. Each page's 3-line `getSessionMember`+ADMIN gate → one `if (!(await getSessionSuperadmin())) redirect("/browse")` (none used `member` after the gate).
  - **Hid 6 cards** on `/dashboard` (`isAdmin` → `isSuperadmin`; the page already resolved `isSuperadmin`): Registered Students, Access, Daily Quizzes, Mock Performance, Feedback, Usage shape.
  - **Re-gated the 2 write surfaces** (hiding a card doesn't protect an endpoint): `POST /api/admin/entitlements` (`requireAdmin`→`requireSuperadmin`; `member.user.id`→`user.id` for `grantedBy`) + `setQuizPublicAction` (`getSessionMember`+ADMIN→`getSessionSuperadmin`).
  - **Why it's transparent to the operator:** `connect.lwspune@gmail.com` holds a **dual identity** — `platform_admins` superadmin AND LWS Pune org-ADMIN (verified in the DB) — so it still reaches `/dashboard` (member resolves) and still sees all six cards (superadmin passes), while the Arjunaa admin loses them. No pure-logic helper added (auth-gate swap + card toggles), so no new tests; the existing gate-helper tests + typecheck/lint/build cover it. **Honest gap:** auth-gated `ƒ` pages have no headless render path in this repo — gate LOGIC verified against real `is_superadmin` data, but the signed-in render was not driven E2E (browser check owed). See [[project-member-management]].

- **2026-07-18 — Download gate narrowed from "any signed-in account" → TEACHER (org-staff) only; non-staff get a "request teacher access" lead form (migration 0060).** The user's framing: a downloadable Word question paper + answer key is a **teacher artifact** (print it, hand it out), so students don't need it — they get the online product (preview, timed `/mock`, notes, saved, quizzes), and download is scoped to provisioned teachers. This *narrows* the 2026-07-11 gate but is not a reversal: the other student conversion levers (mocks, notes-practice, saved, reveal meter) stay intact, and SEO is untouched (download is a POST, browse/preview stay open).
  - **The change rode the existing single seam.** `resolveExportAccess({kind, isSignedIn, isStaff})` is the one gate the API route AND the DownloadDialog both read — flipping paper/key from "any signed-in" to **staff-only** (the matrix collapses: all three kinds now require `isStaff`; student → 403, anon → 401) propagated to enforcement + UI at once. The API route already resolved `isStaff`, so it began returning 403 to students with no route-logic change. TDD-first: the 3×3 `export-access` spec + the route-kind anon test updated before the flip.
  - **The dialog turns the denial into a lead, not a dead end** (user-chosen: teacher-lead CTA + request-access form + just-ship). Non-staff (anon OR signed-in student) see "Paper downloads are for teachers" + a **"Request teacher access"** button → `/request-access` (no sign-in prompt — signing in as a student wouldn't unlock it). Copy on the homepage/browse hero fixed from "sign in to download" → "teachers download papers".
  - **Lead capture (Slice 2):** `/request-access` public page + `POST /api/teacher-access` (IP rate-limited, validated, service-role insert, best-effort ops-notification email via the existing Resend layer) → `teacher_access_requests` (migration 0060, **RLS-on-no-policy = service-role-only**, the `platform_admins`/`entitlements` locked pattern — no anon read/write surface to spam). Pure `validateTeacherAccessRequest` (TDD): name required, **mobile required + email optional** (user's call — phone/WhatsApp is the primary channel for coaching teachers; mobile canonicalized via the shared `normalizeMobile`), DPDP consent. Superadmin triages at **`/superadmin`** (new "Teacher access requests" queue with status new/contacted/provisioned/declined) — the natural flow, since that's where a new org is onboarded + its teacher provisioned. **Why manual "contact us":** there's deliberately no self-serve teacher signup — teachers are provisioned by superadmin/admin, so a qualified lead + white-glove onboarding is the right shape at this scale.
  - **Verified:** full gate PASS (typecheck·lint·notes·test·build; new pure specs + a service-role RLS integration test for the locked table); `/request-access` smoke-tested live (renders, dev log confirms it compiled — not a middleware bounce), the API end-to-end proven (invalid→400 with field, valid→200, row landed with canonical mobile + status `new`, then deleted). **Honest gap:** `/superadmin` is auth-gated `ƒ`, so the authed triage render wasn't driven headlessly (same class as the project's other superadmin/admin `ƒ`-page gaps). NOT committed/pushed — awaiting the user's go-ahead. See [[project-teacher-access-gate]] + [[signup-gate-placement]].

- **2026-07-17 — NEET (UG) mock tests: 8 sittings PUBLIC at `/mock` (mock count 36 → 44), and the mock core generalized past its NDA-fixed-count assumptions.** The literal ask — "add mock of NEET PYQPs" — reduced to reconstructing each NEET sitting from the already-ingested bank, but the NDA-built engine broke on NEET four ways, all found by analysing the corpus BEFORE coding: (1) **sitting discovery collided** — `build.ts` discovered sittings by `(pyq_year, pyq_month)`, but NEET's `pyq_month` is null everywhere AND two sittings share a year (NEET 2024 + Re-NEET 2024; 2026 + Re-2026) → the pair would merge into one 400-q "sitting"; the real key is **`source_file`**. (2) **fixed-count contract** — NDA blueprints hard-assert `count===120`; NEET has two layouts (180 vs 200) and a content-split Biology block, so per-section exact counts fail everywhere. (3) **per-subject sections don't fit** — Biology is content-mixed Botany/Zoology (e.g. 2023 is 62/38, not 45/45) → **one "Biology" section spanning [Botany, Zoology]**, exactly the GAT multi-subject-section pattern; ordering by `source_row` recovers the true interleave (verified: 2024 row 114 is Zoology among Botany). (4) **slug collision** — null month made the two 2024 papers produce identical slugs → an **edition-aware slug** (`neet-2024` vs `neet-2024-re`).
  - **Two edge cases the user insisted be included** (verified against source, NOT assumed — the "dropped due to duplication" hypothesis held for only 1 of 4): **3 NTA officially-dropped/bonus questions** (2022 Q93 emf-no-option, 2022 Q128 all-5-true, Re-2026 Q26 vernier-bonus) — NTA awarded full marks to all, so they were modeled as a per-question **`grace` flag** (award full to everyone, `neg=0`, counted correct; graded via `gradeMock`, disclosed with an amber "awarded to all" badge + solution note) and flipped PUBLIC; **1 verbatim cross-bank duplicate** (2021 Q5, deduped at insert — its content survives as a Pariksha *practice* row) handled by a per-sitting **include-override** referencing the live twin at Physics slot 5, graded normally. The grace mechanism is the right pattern for any future exam with bonus/dropped questions (JEE/MHT-CET both have them) — model it as grace, never by flipping a key or excluding the question.
  - **Generalization kept the NDA path byte-identical** (still 36 mocks, strict count contract): section `count` became optional (soft-count when absent — NEET's short 199/198/179 sittings would just warn, though all 8 ended up complete after the 3 grace flips + 1 override); `buildMockPaper` derives totals + per-section counts from the actual rows placed (NDA's placed === declared); grace threaded snapshot→`gradeMock`→review verdict→UI. The `/mock` catalogue, which was exam-agnostic in data but hardcoded "NDA Mock Tests" in copy and grouped purely by year, now **groups by exam → year** (NDA + NEET no longer mix under a shared year) with neutralized copy, staying static-ISR for SEO. **Build path:** NDA keeps the year+month discovery loop; NEET iterates a `source_file`-keyed `NEET_SITTINGS` registry (grace numbers + include-overrides + expected count per sitting). Data-prep flipped the 3 grace rows PUBLIC (+ a bonus disclosure note on Q26); `--paper=neet --apply --publish` upserted all 8. Verified: correct counts/marks/durations, grace flags + override persisted, **zero dangling refs** across all 1,520 question refs, override at position 5. Gate PASS (46 mock unit tests incl. grace grading + edition slug + soft-count); `/mock` + `/mock/neet-2025` smoke-tested (real 200s). **Honest gap:** the auth-gated runner→submit→result flow (a real NEET attempt showing the grace badge on-screen) wasn't driven E2E — grace grading is unit-tested, the badge is presentational; same class as the project's other `ƒ`-page auth gaps. See [[mock-tests]].

- **2026-07-17 — JEE Mains 2021 Paper 12 (compilation) FINISHED + PUBLIC (53 of 54 q; Q54 PRIVATE) — JEE 718 → 771 q PUBLIC.** The last staged-PRIVATE JEE paper. A compilation (Physics Q1-18, Chemistry Q19-38, Maths Q39-68); 54 committable MCQ were committed PRIVATE but unverified.
