Finished via the **compilation-verification playbook** (now in [[jee-mains-ingestion]]): pull all committed rows → build a BLIND packet (stems+options, NO `is_correct`) split by subject → **3 parallel blind solvers** (Physics/Chem/Maths) independently derive answers + KaTeX-safe solutions → I adjudicate every answer vs the stored key, **independently re-derive all 20 Maths myself**, and **view all 6 image questions** (Q5/6/8 PV+binary-star stems; Q35/37/38 organic option-figs). **Result: 0 wrong keys.** Physics+Chemistry carried the compilation's OWN worked solutions (self-verifying, all support the key); the 20 answer-only Maths all reproduced independently; the 6 images all matched. The single agent-vs-key disagreement (Q26 phenol vs methanal) was a genuinely **ambiguous** question (both are shared monomers of novolac/bakelite) → kept the source key B with an honest note, NOT flipped. **Key lesson banked: "expect ~2 wrong keys/paper" (from P11) is a PRIOR, not a QUOTA — don't manufacture a flip to hit it; verify honestly and accept 0.** Solutions: 28 source (Phy+Chem) + 26 authored (Q18 broken-source; Q27/Q30/Q35/Q37/Q38; all 20 Maths) = 54/54, all KaTeX-safe + unicode-free. Garbled stems: **Q49** OCR `f''(b)/f''(a)` → `f''(2)/f''(1)` (gives f=x³−3x²−9x, local min −27; stemOverride, key C unchanged); **Q37** best-guess stem confirmed faithful against the reaction-scheme image; **Q54** stem truncated in source (options reference an undefined `a`; math verified the answer would be B if a=1, but shipping an incomplete stem or guessing `a` is wrong) → kept **PRIVATE**, mirroring P3 Q37 / P11 Q53. Pipeline: authored-solution JSONs written via the Write tool (never a heredoc — [[heredoc-backslash-eating]]) → `resync` (stems) → `attach-solutions` → `cleanup-latex` → `validate-db` (0 KaTeX-broken, 0 leaks) → SQL flip PUBLIC `where question_number <> '54'`. **Remaining JEE:** compilations 13-16 (same playbook), clean shifts 19-26, Maths-key-resolution 17/24; 2022-2025 not sourced yet. Merged `--no-ff`, pushed (full gate passed).

- **2026-07-17 — NEET (UG) 2021 + 2022 COMPLETE + PUBLIC (+397 q; NEET 1,139 → 1,536 across 8 papers) + an EXHAUSTIVE cross-check of every non-ingested code that caught 2 mislabeled source files.** A fresh source drop of NEET 2021 (5 codes) + 2022 (7 codes) appeared in `C:\tmp\PYQPs\NEET\`. Both ingested end-to-end through the proven `scripts/neet/` pipeline (config → render → 4 parallel vision agents Phy 1–50 / Chem 51–100 / Botany 101–150 / Zoology 151–200 → commit PRIVATE → spot-check → snapCrop figures + verify-gate → flip PUBLIC), merged to main `f428e40` (branch `neet-2021-2022-ingestion`). **2021 (Code M1, two-column, 50pp):** 199 PUBLIC (200 transcribed, 1 cross-year verbatim repeat correctly deduped by the per-exam `content_hash`) + 15 figures; Q172 dual official key `2/3*` stored primary (3)=ELISA + transparent note. **2022 (Code Q1, single-column, 69pp):** 198 PUBLIC + 2 PRIVATE + 18 figures; Q93 (chem emf) + Q128 (botany, no option covers all-true statements) are officially-dropped NA rows → placeholder key + `[Officially NO-ANSWER…]` note, held PRIVATE via `flip-public --except=93,128`; Q17/Q180 awarded-with-asterisk keys flipped PUBLIC with notes.
  - **The load-bearing discovery: file size ≠ has-key.** Both files are named `…qp Code…` and are ~2.2–2.7 MB (vs the ~8–10 MB solution booklets), which first read as "question-paper-only, no key → CDS-style derive-and-flag." OPENING one settled it: they're full Answers & Solutions booklets with official keys + worked solutions; the small size is just older/denser scanning. **Rule: never infer has-key from filename or file size — render a page and look.** (Also: 2021/2022 are the first NEET papers with a usable text layer — born-digital — so their keys were spot-checkable by grep, unlike the pure-scan 2023–2026.)
  - **Both years explicitly LABEL Botany/Zoology as separate printed sections** (2021: Botany pp29–38, Zoology pp39–50; 2022: Botany pp41–51, Zoology pp52–69) — cleaner than the content-mixed "BIOLOGY" banner of later papers; no per-question content-guessing needed for the split. Subject-block page ranges were mapped cheaply from the text layer's `get_text('blocks')` header positions before dispatching agents.
  - **Exhaustive non-ingested-code cross-check (the user asked to verify EVERY code, not sample).** Method: for each code, pull 3–5 distinctive plain-English stem fragments (text-layer grep for the 9 born-digital codes; vision agents in ≤3-agent waves for the ~37 scans) and `ILIKE` them against the ingested paper's `text`/`solution`/`options` for that year. **All ~44 non-ingested codes confirmed = the same question set reshuffled** (question order AND option order shuffle per code, so the correct *letter* differs but the correct *answer content* is identical — proven on 2026 code-12 Q1 = code-11 Q29, both "2.5 A, 2 A m²"). Every first-pass "miss" resolved to found on a looser fragment — pure wording/formatting/hyphenation differences (`spring-mass` vs `spring mass`, `abcda` spacing), never a missing question; two planted negative controls (a cross-year question + a garbage string) correctly returned not-found, proving the search discriminates. **So "one code per year captures 100% of the unique questions" is now verified, not just assumed.**
  - **The exhaustive pass earned its keep by catching 2 MISLABELED source files** (a filename can name the wrong year/exam): `neet 2024 qp code E4.pdf` is actually **NEET UG 2023** (cover: "7 May 2023, Code E4") and `neet 2026 qp code 70.pdf` is actually **NEET (UG)-2026 Re-Examination** (cover: "21/06/2026, Code 70", 180 q, 43 pp — hence its odd small size). In both cases the content is ALREADY in the bank under the correct year, so nothing is missing — but **verify a suspected-wrong file against the printed COVER page, never the filename.** Reconfirms [[neet-ingestion]] + [[figure-snapcrop-verify]] (montage-verified every crop myself; re-cropped Q3 wire-baseline clip + Q97 answer-leak on 2021, Q2 axis clip + Q43 dipole reposition + Q67 answer-margin on 2022). **COMPLETE for the source folder — nothing left un-ingested.**

- **2026-07-16 — State Board Ch.3 Indefinite Integration COMPLETE + PUBLIC (292 q; 12th Class-12 Maths chapter, 5th of Part-02) — the 2nd-largest State Board chapter, and the DIRTIEST Balbharati answer key found so far (28 book-key errors, 0 ours).** The 3-technique spine of integration (substitution · by parts · partial fractions), 56pp, through the proven `scripts/stateboard/` pipeline: config → render → **12 vision fragments** → merge (292 q) → commit PRIVATE (72 solved + 200 exercise-subjective + 20 MCQ across 7 subtopics) → blind MCQ re-derivation → 6 parallel solution-authoring agents → `apply-solutions.ts` → 7-agent answer-key cross-check → 33 errata → flip PUBLIC → sections + `board:lint`. MH-HSC-12 **1,959 → 2,251**; total PUBLIC **24,099**.
  - **ALL math is VISION** — the PDF text layer substitutes Sinhala glyphs (ධ න ඦ ν) for the ∫ sign, so stems/solutions are unreadable from text. Every one of the 12 blocks was transcribed by a vision agent reading the rendered PNGs.
  - **NOVEL structure, handled cleanly:** (1) exercises use **two-level numbering** — Roman groups `I./II./III.` (each with its own instruction line) containing `(i)…(x)` sub-items → each Roman group is a SET (`setLabel` `Ex <sec> <Roman>`, the instruction as `context`). (2) §3.2 splits into **three lettered exercise sub-blocks** 3.2(A)/(B)/(C), each with its own solved run (ref-prefixed `3.2A`/`3.2B`/`3.2C` to stay unique). (3) §3.3 has **two solved runs** (main + the §3.3.3 e^x[f+f'] sub-run that restarts at 1 → prefixed `3.3.3`; note `3.3 SolvedEx` does NOT prefix-match `3.3.3 SolvedEx`, 4th char `.` vs ` `, so `sections.ts` lists both). (4) **Miscellaneous has THREE parts** (I = 20 MCQ, II = 9, III = 20 subjective) — an agent caught part III, which the brief hadn't anticipated.
  - **Every exercise answer authored + VERIFIED BY DIFFERENTIATION** (sympy `diff(F)-integrand`, numeric fallback at interior points). This is load-bearing for integration: the by-parts agent **caught 2 of its own answers wrong on first pass** (an inverse-trig and an e^x[f+f'] integral) precisely because they didn't differentiate back. Both blind MCQ derivation AND the transcription agent's derivation agreed on all 20 keys, which also matched the book's own printed MCQ answer table.
  - **✅ GATE — cross-check over 220 keyed rows (7 agents, one per block): 192 AGREE · 28 BOOK-KEY-WRONG · 0 OUR-ANSWER-WRONG — the THIRD consecutive clean sweep on our side, and the dirtiest key yet** (Differentiation 9, Vectors 10, this 28). I independently re-adjudicated a 5-claim sample with my own sympy (our answer → integrand OK, book → integrand FAIL on every one) before writing errata. The 28 span every block: dropped `\log`s, sign flips, wrong coefficients, an `81→9` radicand typo, and **an angle `π/4` copied from the previous item where it should be `π/3`** — a very human key-setter slip. The sharpest is **Misc III Q.9: the book gives an arctan antiderivative when the Weierstrass denominator has real roots, so the answer is logarithmic — a wrong ANSWER TYPE, not just a coefficient** (and two author "maybe-equivalent" pre-flags, III.9 + III.11, turned out to be REAL book errors after numeric disproof — the agent correctly overrode my "treat as AGREE" hint). **The dominant mode was the opposite risk — equivalent forms.** 192 AGREEs include dozens of surface differences (`\log|A|-\log|B|`≡`\log|A/B|`, completed-square log-arguments written two ways, `t=\tan(x/2)` vs `t=\tan x` antiderivatives); the briefs leaned hard on `simplify(ours-book)==const ⇒ AGREE`, so no equivalent form was mis-flagged. **The `Ex 3.2(A) II(10)` stem-misprint suspicion RESOLVED as AGREE** — the book itself prints the same "ugly" 5-term answer (via `t=cos x`) equivalent to ours (via `t=sin x`); the worry was moot, exactly what the cross-check exists to settle. **33 errata items** (28 key errors + 4 printing typos + Q.7 duplicate-options + 3 solved-example printed-solution misprints from §3.2.3-5) → errata.ts now **151 items / 12 chapters**.
  - **"Activity" fill-in-the-blank solved examples — a new wrinkle vs Differentiation.** Where Ch.1's Activities lived in exercises (→ exercise-subjective), here several are embedded in the SOLVED-example flow: the book prints a worked example as a fill-in-the-blank activity (dotted blanks) WITH the final answer. Two agents handled them inconsistently (one filled the blanks, one preserved them). Since solved examples ship PUBLIC with the book's solution, a "model answer" full of `(....)` is broken → I **completed the blanks** on the 2 genuinely-incomplete ones (`3.2C SolvedEx.3`, `3.3 SolvedEx.10` which literally ended "refer previous example and solve it"), **sympy-verified** the completion (`d/dx[completion]-integrand==0`), and pushed the fixes to the live rows via a direct DB solution UPDATE (solved rows aren't touched by `apply-solutions`). A scan confirmed the other "incomplete" flags were false positives (`..... (i)` equation-numbering, `\ldots by LIATE` annotations, or terse-but-complete standard-result formulas).
  - **The heredoc/Python backslash trap bit ME, not just the agents.** Fixing the 2 incomplete solved examples via a `python -c` heredoc turned `\frac`→formfeed, `\therefore`→tab, `\because`→backspace (Python string escapes on top of the shell heredoc). Caught it immediately by checking the written bytes (`\f`/`\t`/`\b` present), redid it via the Write tool with **raw strings** (`r"""..."""`), and a DB-wide scan confirmed 0 control chars across all 292 rows. Reconfirms [[heredoc-backslash-eating]] — the rule is now "author LaTeX via the Write tool into a `.py`/`.json`/`.ts`, never inline `python -c`/heredoc," and it applies to the maintainer's own one-offs, not only subagents.
  - **No figures / no `solution_image`** (integrals are analytic; 0 stems reference a figure). Reconfirms [[state-board-ingestion]] + [[textbook-chapter-ingestion]] + [[audit-probe-symmetry]] + [[board-reader]] + [[heredoc-backslash-eating]]. Minor deferred: the Ex 3.1 block used `\csc` where other blocks used `\operatorname{cosec}` — both render in KaTeX, left un-normalised (cosmetic).

- **2026-07-16 — State Board Ch.2 Application of Derivatives COMPLETE + PUBLIC (161 q; 11th Class-12 Maths chapter, 4th of Part-02) — the SECOND consecutive 0-error-ours cross-check, and the book's answer key fails hardest where the arithmetic is loosest.** The direct sequel to Ch.1 Differentiation (every method from it, applied), 30pp, through the proven `scripts/stateboard/` pipeline: config → render → **14 vision fragments** → merge (161 q, 0 dup refs) → commit PRIVATE (35 solved + 116 exercise-subjective + 10 MCQ across 8 subtopics) → blind MCQ re-derivation → 5 parallel solution-authoring agents → `apply-solutions.ts` → answer-key cross-check → 19 errata brackets → flip PUBLIC → sections + `board:lint`. MH-HSC-12 **1,798 → 1,959**; total PUBLIC **23,807**.
  - **Subtopics map 1:1 onto the book's numbered sections** (Tangents & Normals · Rate Measure · Velocity/Acceleration/Jerk · Approximations · Rolle · LMVT · Increasing/Decreasing · Maxima & Minima). Two judgment calls: **angle-between-curves folded into Tangents & Normals** (1 "orthogonal" + 2 "angle between" mentions chapter-wide — too thin to stand alone, unlike the MHT-CET *notes* chapter where it earned a page), and **Velocity/Acceleration/Jerk kept at just 2 q** — the book gives §2.1.4 its own numbered section + solved-example block but **zero exercise coverage**. Verified this was the book's reality, not a misclassification: every motion-flavoured exercise question is correctly filed elsewhere by CONTENT (Ex 2.1 Q.7/Q.13 are related rates, Ex 2.4 Q.13 is max-height, Misc II Q.7 is LMVT in kinematics clothing). Kept per the shipped **Pair of Straight Lines "Angle Bisectors" 2-q precedent** — a thin subtopic is legitimate when the book teaches it as its own unit.
  - **UNLIKE Ch.1, the exercises are NOT method-pure** — Ex 2.1 fuses tangents/normals (Q1-6) with rate measure (Q7+), Ex 2.4 fuses monotonicity with extrema. So subtopics are assigned **per-question**, and the agent briefs said so explicitly ("choose by CONTENT, not position — the block genuinely mixes them").
  - **✅ GATE — 126 keyed rows cross-checked by 5 parallel agents: 105 AGREE · 14 NO-BOOK-KEY · 6 BOOK-KEY-WRONG · 1 BOOK-QUESTION-MISPRINT · **0 OUR-ANSWER-WRONG**.** Ex 2.1 (22/22) and Ex 2.4 (29 AGREE + 6 show-that) were clean sweeps on BOTH sides; **all 6 key errors cluster in Ex 2.2 Approximations** — the block where the book does the most hand-arithmetic. Two are proved by the key **contradicting itself**: Q.2(ii) keys `sin 29°30′ = 0.42423` while Q.2(iii) keys the *identical quantity* `cos 60°30′ = 0.4924`; Q.5(iii)'s value back-solves to `h = 13.93` (no such step) and contradicts the book's own worked Ex.6 using the same method. One is **self-refuting**: Q.1(i) keys `√8.95 = 2.9168`, but `2.9168² = 8.51`. Q.2(i) keys `sin 61° = 0.953` via `0.866 + 0.0875` — the correction term taken 10× too large. Plus **Misc II Q.21** keys `Min = 1` when `f(3π/2) = −1` is attained (the book evaluated only `x = π/2` from the `cos x = 0` branch and never checked the second root; its 1 is a LOCAL min). **I adjudicated all 6 myself** against a 6× crop of the printed key + my own mpmath re-derivation before writing a single bracket.
  - **The MCQ check was doubly independent and agreed twice.** `dump-mcq` strips `is_correct`; a blind verifier re-derived **A C B B D C D A D D** — identical to the transcription agent's independent derivation, and `mark-mcq-verify` (not the agent) stamped 0 mismatches. The verifier also corroborated sideways: on Q.9, option A lies on the curve but not the tangent, B on the tangent but not the curve — only D satisfies both. **The option-order trap did not fire** (no letter flipped).
  - **The book's printed SOLUTIONS are again far dirtier than its questions — 12 solved-example misprints vs 1 defective question.** Worst: **`2.4.3 SolvedEx.1`, FOUR corrupted lines in one example** whose sign/interval summaries contradict its own derivation two lines above (`f'(x) > 0 for x = 1−h ⇒ decreasing for x > 1`, right after deriving `f'(1−h) < 0`); as printed, both intervals around `x = −1` show `> 0`, which would make it not a maximum at all. Also `2.1.2 SolvedEx.1(iii)` prints `2x/2` where expanding gives `2x/3` (next line uses `2x/3`), `2.3.1 SolvedEx.2` factors `2x−4 = 2(x−4)`, `2.2.1 SolvedEx.3` prints `0.1750` where its own given constant is `0.0175`, `2.4.4 SolvedEx.5` prints `−bc/a` for `−bc/2` (confirmed at 5× zoom against three genuine `2` glyphs on the same line). **Every one is provable from the book's own internal inconsistency**, so no page-image round-trip was needed to adjudicate them. All transcribed faithfully + bracketed → `errata.ts` now spans **11 chapters / 118 items**.
  - **1 fix on our side, and it was an incompleteness not an error**: Ex 2.4 Q.4 widened `(-∞,-4) ∪ (12,∞)` → `(-∞,-4] ∪ [12,∞)`. The stem says plain "Increasing" (not "strictly"), `f'` vanishes only at the two isolated endpoints, so the closed form is correct AND the book is internally consistent about this (it prints open intervals only where the stem says "strictly"). Separately **Misc II Q.11** got a `[Textbook answer-key note:]` rather than an error bracket — the key omits the whole `(0,1)` branch where `f = x/log x` is also decreasing, but everything it *states* is true; calling that "wrong" would cost the errata report credibility.
  - **Two new durable lessons folded into the README** (both are "a tool returning nothing is not evidence"): (1) **a sympy `solve`/`solveset` MISS is the same trap as `simplify()` non-zero** — it returned no roots in (0,2π) for one Ex 2.3 trig row and dropped `5π/4` on another (principal-branch artifacts), which taken at face value would have manufactured 2 bogus `OUR-ANSWER-WRONG` findings; the agent settled them by testing the claimed value directly (`f'(3π/4)` ≈ 1e-41) and scanning numerically. (2) **The README's "the textbook is single-column" was flat wrong** — Differentiation is 60-of-64 two-column, this chapter 21-of-30. Worse, my *block-geometry heuristic* for per-page column layout mislabelled three pages (p-04/p-05/p-23), and **every time an agent caught it by actually looking at the page**; the README now says to tell agents "usually two-column — verify per page yourself" rather than hand them a derived map. Reconfirms [[state-board-ingestion]] + [[textbook-chapter-ingestion]] + [[audit-probe-symmetry]] + [[board-reader]] + [[heredoc-backslash-eating]] (the errata JSON was authored via Write, never a heredoc).
  - **19 `solution_image` diagrams — skipped at ingest, then BUILT on the user's call (same day).** The skip was defensible (no stem is unanswerable without a figure — the diagram is the SETUP, the answer is a number; the documented line is "build where the figure IS the answer", i.e. LP's feasible regions / App-of-Def-Integration's area regions), and the user overrode it. The build is the interesting part: it moved `render_solution_diagrams.py` from **coordinate-plots to PHYSICAL geometry**.
    - **New primitives:** `segments` (FINITE lines — the existing `lines` primitive is infinite + viewport-clipped, right for a plot and useless for a ladder; this was the core gap) · `axes:false` (a cone's cross-section has no X/Y axes) · arc `conics` via `t0`/`t1` in degrees · outlined `polys` · `rightangles` · caption **word-wrap** (it was one unwrapped line that silently clipped).
    - **`equal_aspect` is the load-bearing one:** `px()` maps xr/yr to the drawable box INDEPENDENTLY, so a semicircular window rendered as a squashed ellipse. It auto-expands (never crops) the deficient range until px/unit matches on both axes — verified exactly equal on all 18 geometry figures. **Default OFF**, so the 3 shipped chapters render byte-identically (verified; only LP changed, and only because its one over-budget caption now wraps — its LIVE image is untouched until a re-attach).
    - **Method:** 2 pilots hand-authored + rendered + inspected FIRST, so the 4 parallel spec-authoring agents got a proven contract rather than a guess; they emit `data/app-derivatives-12.diagram-specs.<group>.json` part-files (the builder globs them + errors loudly on a duplicate ref). **I then audited the geometry independently from the spec data** rather than trusting the agents' self-reports: every inscribed vertex exact on its circle (3-4-5 triples, `√6,√3`), the cone's semi-vertical angle exactly 30.000000°, both lamp-post rays exactly through the head. Where the optimum IS the answer it's drawn (cone-in-sphere `4r/3`, cylinder-in-sphere `2R/√3`, closed cylinder `h=2r`, sheet `k=3,x=5`); where the VARIABLE is the point it's deliberately generic (Q.13's rectangle non-square, since the square is the conclusion).
    - **Agents caught things I'd have missed**, all by looking at their own renders: `polys` draw AFTER `segments` so a closed outline silently overdraws a coloured highlight edge (label names a colour not on screen — now documented in the builder; the fix is segments-only, not layering); the `curves` label offset is hardcoded, not spec-configurable; dashed hidden edges read "closed" on an open-top box (solved with a double-line rim).

- **2026-07-15/16 — Mock-recommendation email: the project's FIRST outbound-email capability (migration 0059 · `src/lib/email/` · `scripts/email/`). SHIPPED + SENT: 31 students, 0 failures, campaign drained.** Ask: "mail every registered user about the next mock they haven't attempted, from a course they attempted earlier"; the sibling **English AI Tutor** (`Downloads/English_AI_Tutor`) is the reference implementation. Closes the long-standing ROADMAP gap *"Custom SMTP (Resend) — now a real need"* for this one campaign (magic-link + password-reset still ride on Supabase's built-in SMTP).
  - **The analysis changed the feature.** The literal ask reaches **~10 real students**: 41 students, only 13 have ever attempted a mock. And *"the course they attempted"* is **degenerate today** — all 36 published mocks are NDA, so an `exam_id` filter selects everything for everyone. The axis that carries meaning is **`paper_code`** (8 of 12 attempters have only ever sat a **GAT** paper; 2 only Maths). So the scope is exam **AND** paper — the exam leg is kept because it's correct once NEET/JEE mocks ship, not because it does anything now.
  - **The reach lesson was IMPORTED, not re-learned** ([[project-engagement-engine]]'s whole premise). The AI Tutor shipped a vocab nudge to a 5-student cohort and wrote down the regret: *"the bigger engagement gap is '66/77 students have never opened vocab practice' — that's an introduction-email problem, not a re-engagement problem."* PYQ Vault's mirror: **29 of 41 never attempted** (7 of them declared `nda` in `target_exams` at signup, then never sat one). Same build, one extra branch + template → **3× the reach**. User chose both cohorts: `next_mock` + `first_mock`.
  - **What we took from the sibling:** a PURE decision function with an injected `now` (their `src/lib/reminders.js`, 19 tests) · one-email-per-user-per-run (`claimed`-set) · **text + HTML always** (their v9 note: the plain-text alternative is *"the biggest deliverability win per Resend guidance"*) · raw-`fetch` Resend, no SDK · the 600ms throttle (their v8 bugfix after real 429s on the 2 req/sec free tier) · a no-send preview path (their `test_mode`).
  - **What we deliberately did NOT copy — their three weakest points:** (1) **no send log** — they stamp two timestamps on `profiles` AFTER the loop, so a mid-loop crash re-sends everything and there's no record of what went to whom; we log **one immutable row per attempt (sent OR failed)** with a **UNIQUE `dedupe_key`** (the 0052 lesson: a *partial* unique index isn't ON CONFLICT-targetable). **The key IS the policy**: `next_mock:{u}:{mock}` (never re-recommend the same mock → an ignored pick walks down the catalogue) · `first_mock:{u}` (once, ever — no mock id, so no nagging). (2) **no unsubscribe at all** — defensible for their closed school deployment with offline consent; **not** for PYQ Vault's public self-serve signup, so opt-out + one-click unsubscribe ship on day one. (3) **fetch-whole-tables-and-join-in-JS** — ours are paged service-role reads (the 1000-row cap).
  - **The dry run caught a live hazard on its first execution.** It picked `billing_paid_…@test.invalid` + `billing_expired_…@test.invalid` — fixtures the 2026-07-07 billing tests stranded in `auth.users`, which the roster derives from ([[test-data-leak-org-signal]], now proven to have a **second blast radius: outbound email**, not just inflated bank counts). `.invalid` is RFC 2606 reserved ⇒ **guaranteed hard bounce**, and bounce rate is what throttles/suspends a young sending domain. Fixed as a **property, not a skip-list**: `isUndeliverable()` in the pure core rejects reserved TLDs — it immediately caught our own `@example.com` test fixtures too, which is the guard working. **The durable lesson: any code path that emails a roster derived from `auth.users` must assume test fixtures are in it.**
  - **Unsubscribe is POST-only + smoke-tested, and that's load-bearing.** Gmail/Outlook link-scanners **prefetch** urls, so a GET that acted would silently opt out people who never clicked; `/unsubscribe/[token]` renders a confirm button, `/api/unsubscribe/[token]` (POST, service-role — the token IS the capability, no session exists) does the work, and `List-Unsubscribe`/`List-Unsubscribe-Post` (RFC 8058) give mailbox providers a native control. Both build `ƒ`, so the green build proved nothing — drove it live per [[probe-must-reach-the-code]]: GET 200 + *"Compiling /unsubscribe/[token]"* in the dev log (not a middleware bounce) + **`email_opt_out` still false after the GET** · POST flipped exactly one row · non-uuid → 400 · unknown uuid → 200 (not a probing oracle). *(Ops: a `rm -rf .next` racing the dev-server start produced a 500 on every route — the Windows zombie-`next-server` trap again; kill the PID holding the port and restart clean.)*
  - **A tsx script, NOT a cron** — the repo's batch precedent (`scripts/mocks/build.ts`, `scripts/activity/backfill.ts`), and the **engagement gate** says measure the usage shape BEFORE automating a mechanic (`/dashboard/activity` still reports `insufficient`). **Dry-run is the DEFAULT** and **writes nothing** (`ensureUnsubscribeTokens` upserts a profile row to mint a token, so it's deferred to the `--apply` path and the preview renders against a placeholder — the first cut minted rows on every dry run, which is why the whole roster suddenly had `student_profiles`); `--apply` must be typed. `--only=<email>` restricts to one address. Consent is opt-**out** (default false): these are product nudges to registered users, and opt-in would start reach at zero. The QA test account is suppressed **through the real mechanism** (`email_opt_out=true`), not a hardcoded branch. *(Roster addresses are personal data and this repo is PUBLIC — identify accounts by role here, never by address.)*
  - **Gotcha worth keeping:** `resend.ts`/`service.ts` are deliberately **NOT** `server-only` — that's a Next **build-time alias**, unresolvable under tsx (`MODULE_NOT_FOUND`), and the repo already solved this in `src/lib/quiz/assemble.ts`: *"NOT marked server-only precisely so the tsx script can import it"* + inject the client.   - **LIVE RUN (2026-07-16): 31 students · 11 next_mock + 20 first_mock · `sent=31 failed=0`**, staged `--limit=3` → rest. `pyqvault.com` verified in Resend (GoDaddy auto-configure; **independently confirmed from public DNS** — DKIM + the SPF *macro chain* `send → dc-…_spfm → include:amazonses.com` + MX `feedback-smtp.ap-northeast-1` — don't trust the dashboard's green alone; the delegated SPF chain is the leg that can silently break). **A pre-existing GoDaddy `p=quarantine` DMARC** means an auth failure lands in **spam SILENTLY** — the send log still says `sent`, so placement can't be inferred from `sent=31`. **Placement CONFIRMED same-day: 31/31 reached the INBOX**, Gmail reporting SPF PASS · DKIM PASS (`pyqvault.com`) · DMARC PASS, ~1s delivery. Re-run now reports `Recipients: 0` — every `first_mock` key permanently burned, the 11 `next_mock` behind the 7-day cooldown. **32 rows / 32 distinct students / 32 distinct dedupe keys — nobody got two.**
  - **Four things the LIVE run taught that the 75 tests could not.** (1) **The dry run WROTE**: `ensureUnsubscribeTokens` ran before the `--apply` check, minting `student_profiles` rows for every candidate (the whole roster had one where 16 did) — caught only by noticing a count that had no business changing; "dry run" now means *no writes*. (2) **The unsubscribe recorded no WHEN**: `student_profiles` has **no `updated_at` trigger** (the column DEFAULT only fires on INSERT), so the opt-out UPDATE left the creation timestamp — a row asserting "consent withdrawn" with no date, exactly the fact DPDP asks for. Surfaced only by two timestamps being 79 min apart in the wrong order. *(The same gap likely affects the other `student_profiles` writers — `saveOwnMobile`, the `/api/profile` PATCH — unaudited.)* (3) **The reply-to was off-brand**: `EMAIL_FROM` is send-only with no inbox, so replies bounced; adding `reply_to` then exposed that the address it pointed at (`connect.lwspune@…`) is the **TENANT ORG's**, on the most student-facing surface there is. (4) **A reply-to nobody is told about is dead weight** — the footer must invite the reply (the sibling app's reasoning: a student who can't sign in has no other channel).
  - **The brand leak was 4× bigger than it looked** (fix `375c3c6`): the public contact address was the tenant org's in **Footer (every page, directly under "From the team at PYQ Vault") · /privacy (the DPDP contact) · both anon report dialogs**. The 2026-07-12 sweep hunted the *"LWS" string* and an address doesn't contain it. **The DUPLICATION was the bug** — four copy-pasted literals with no single place to fix — so the remedy is `src/lib/brand.ts` + one `CONTACT_EMAIL`, consumed by all four *and* the email `REPLY_TO`. Verified in **rendered HTML** on `/`, `/privacy`, `/browse`, with the dev log checked to prove the routes compiled.
  - **Supabase Auth SMTP wired the same day (2026-07-16) — and it is NOT the same system as the Resend API above.** Our app POSTs `api.resend.com`; **auth mail (password-reset/magic-link/invite) is sent by SUPABASE**, configured with SMTP creds in its dashboard (`smtp.resend.com`:465, user `resend`, sender `noreply@pyqvault.com`). **Enabling SMTP does NOT lift the throttle** — the separate `GOTRUE_RATE_LIMIT_EMAIL_SENT` had to go **2/1h → 30**; the old ~2/hour cap WAS the bug. Verified live: `POST /auth/v1/recover` → `user_recovery_requested`, `status:200 error:null`, **duration 2.77s** = a real SMTP round-trip (**200 alone proves nothing — that endpoint always 200s to prevent email enumeration; the DURATION is the tell**). **⚠️ The stock reset mail lands in SPAM with Gmail's "might be dangerous" PHISHING banner — a CONTENT fault, not deliverability.** Supabase's `{{ .ConfirmationURL }}` always points at `<ref>.supabase.co` while the From says `pyqvault.com`; a reset email whose link domain ≠ From domain is textbook credential-harvesting. **The same-day campaign is the control that proves it**: identical domain/provider/hour, identical PASS/PASS/PASS, links to `www.pyqvault.com` → **31/31 inbox**. So never chase DNS/warm-up for this; fix = template → `{{ .SiteURL }}/auth/confirm?token_hash={{ .TokenHash }}` + that route + `/reset-password` + `/forgot-password` (= the ROADMAP "Password reset flow" item; magic-link + invite share the defect). **Deferred by the user** — nothing regressed: a student who forgot their password was already stuck.
  - **Roster hygiene the campaign forced:** 4 non-students suppressed via `email_opt_out` (the real mechanism — never a code skip-list) + **2 leaked `@test.invalid` billing fixtures DELETED from `auth.users`** (verified inert first: 0 attempts/activity/entitlements/bookmarks, created 83ms apart). **The structural finding: deleting an org silently PROMOTES its members onto the student roster** — `deriveStudents` = "any auth user with no `org_members` row", which was only ever a *roster* definition and is now also the *mailing list*; the ex-APJ org account surfaced as a student after the branch re-tenanting. Recurs on the next one. Addresses live in the private memory note — **this repo is PUBLIC, so roster addresses must never land in tracked docs** (a first draft of the SUGGESTIONS entry named all four and was caught pre-commit). See [[project-mock-recommendation-email]].

- **2026-07-15 — State Board Ch.1 Differentiation COMPLETE + PUBLIC (363 q; 10th Class-12 Maths chapter, the FIRST of Part-02's methods spine) — the largest State Board chapter yet, and the cleanest-authored/dirtiest-printed one to date.** 64pp (beats Vectors' 61pp), through the proven `scripts/stateboard/` pipeline: config → render → **13 vision fragments** → merge (363 q, 0 dup refs) → commit PRIVATE (97 solved + 254 exercise-subjective + 12 MCQ across 8 subtopics) → blind MCQ re-derivation → 6 parallel solution-authoring agents → `apply-solutions.ts` → answer-key cross-check → 24 errata brackets → flip PUBLIC → sections + `board:lint`. MH-HSC-12 **1,435 → 1,798**; total PUBLIC **23,646**.
  - **Subtopic arc is finer-grained than the MHT-CET Differentiation notes chapter (8, not 6)** because each textbook exercise block is *method-pure*: Chain Rule · Inverse Functions · Inverse Trig · Logarithmic · Implicit · Parametric · One-wrt-Another · Higher Order. **§1.2.1 "Geometrical meaning of Derivative" is motivating PROSE with no questions** → deliberately NOT a subtopic (verified: p12-13 flows straight into 1.2.2).
  - **The load-bearing lesson — a header scan is NOT a block map; sections start MID-PAGE.** My first band put Ex 1.2 at p28 because the regex found "1.3.1" on p29 — but 1.3.1 starts at **y≈531**, so the top of p29 was still Exercise 1.2. The validation agent flagged the boundary; re-deriving every block with **`get_text('blocks')` y-positions** recovered **31 rows (a whole Q.10 (i)-(ix) I hadn't listed)** — ~32% of that fragment, silently lost otherwise. **Always map blocks by (page, y), never by page alone** (same class as the Vectors gap-fill). Two agents independently caught straddles the page-boundary heuristic would have dropped (§1.3.3 Ex.6 sat above the Ex 1.3 header on p38).
  - **✅ GATE — answer-key cross-check vs the Part-2 ANSWERS section (p265-269), 6 parallel agents over 266 exercise rows: 208 AGREE · 48 NO-BOOK-KEY (show-that) · **8 BOOK-KEY-WRONG + 1 BOOK-QUESTION-MISPRINT + 1 INTERPRETIVE · 0 OUR-ANSWER-WRONG**.** Zero authoring errors — the first chapter with a clean sweep on our side. The method that made it trustworthy (now the standard): **a THIRD independent ground truth** — mpmath/sympy differentiation of the *actual stem* at 30-50 dps, testing **both** our answer AND the book's against it, because comparing the two to each other lets a shared error hide. **~20 apparent disagreements dissolved** on equivalence testing (`2\cdot4^x\log4` ≡ `4^{x+1}\log2`; single-branch book answers vs our documented ±branches). **`simplify()` returning non-zero is NOT evidence of inequality** — on Ex 1.3 that alone would have manufactured 3 false "our answer is wrong" findings (identities that hold only ON the constraint curve).
  - **The book's key is a PEER, not an ORACLE — and Misc I Q.4 proves it.** The transcription agent *predicted in advance*: "an answer key naming A or B here would be a book error; I'd expect C." **The book says B.** Three independent derivations say C; verified numerically on the non-trivial branch (C matches 7/7 at 25 sig figs, B 0/7). Option B is the correct expression **with its two brackets interchanged** — the question's own near-miss distractor, and *the book's key-setter fell for it*. Every agent tested the rival hypothesis "our transcription is wrong" by **rendering the source page and looking** (Ex 1.3 Q.5(iii): a flipped `(x⁴−y⁴)/(x⁴+y⁴)` would have vindicated the book — the page plainly prints the other way; hypothesis rejected).
  - **The book's printed SOLUTIONS are far dirtier than its questions: ~16 misprints in the 97 solved examples vs ~1 defective question in 266.** Worst: **`1.3.1 SolvedEx.1 (iii)` corrupts `(2x+3)`→`(3x+2)` across four consecutive lines into a WRONG printed final answer** — shipping it unflagged would have published a wrong answer. All transcribed **faithfully as printed** + bracketed. **25 errata items** (the most of any chapter) → `errata.ts` now spans **10 chapters / 99 items**.
  - **New durable pipeline artifacts** (fold-the-learning-in, each closing a bug that bit a *previous* chapter): **`dump-mcq.ts`** (blind dump — omits `is_correct` so step 4 stays independent; **guards that every MCQ carries 4 options**, the NCERT `options:[]` bug) · **`dump-review.ts`** (**refuses to dump unless every row carries real answer text** — the Line-and-Planes `has_solution`-boolean bug that made a cross-check meaninglessly all-AGREE) · **`dump-subjective.ts`** (partition authoring by ref prefix) · **`mark-mcq-verify.ts`** (computes `matches_current` *here*, so the verifier can stay blind) · **`apply-errata.ts`** (idempotent bracket prepend at the START — `errata.ts` scans `startsWith("[")`, so an appended bracket is invisible; mirrors DB↔source JSON so they can't drift).
  - **Zero figures / zero `solution_image`** — verified 0 of 363 stems reference a figure; only 1 of 254 authored rows flagged `diagramWouldHelp` (Misc II Q.1's corner sketch) and the prose stands complete, so skipped per the Vectors/Line-and-Planes precedent. **Ops:** ~6 agents died on API stalls/watchdogs — **12-page vision ranges stall, 5-9 pages succeed**; the fix is smaller ranges + **write the output file EARLY/incrementally** (a dead Misc II agent finished all its verification then lost it at the final write). A replacement **audited the dead agent's leftover script and discarded it** — it only tested OUR answer, never the book's, so its lost "all pass" claim wouldn't have been evidence anyway. The heredoc-eats-backslashes pitfall fired twice; both agents self-diagnosed. Reconfirms [[state-board-ingestion]] + [[textbook-chapter-ingestion]] + [[audit-probe-symmetry]] + [[board-reader]] + [[heredoc-backslash-eating]].

- **2026-07-15 — Paper editor renders questions like the bank (reuses `/browse`'s `QuestionCard`); Add-questions panel → a sheet (merge `25d57c8`).** Triggered by a paper review: the editor showed **raw LaTeX** (`\(Q_1 = 20\)` verbatim) in a 2-line clamp with no options. Three findings, one root cause.
  - **The render bug:** `PaperEditor` dumped `{p.text}` into a `<p className="line-clamp-2">` with **no `KatexRenderer`** — so every math question in every paper had been broken since the builder shipped. `AddQuestionsPanel` had the same bug, so you were picking questions out of raw markup too.
  - **The data was never there:** the editor loaded the slim `QuestionPreview` shape (id/text/taxonomy — the deliberately-light shape built for the **cart-preview** payload), which carries **no options**. So "show options" was a data change, not a CSS change. Fixed by swapping to `queryQuestionsByIds` (full `QuestionRow`, already the `/saved` precedent) + reusing `QuestionCard` **wholesale**. User chose to include solutions in the payload; bounded by the 200/paper export cap on a `force-dynamic` admin page. `queryQuestionPreviewsByIds` **stays** — `/api/cart/preview` is still its rightful user.
  - **The layout WAS the readability bug.** The add panel sat in a permanent `lg:grid-cols-2` column, i.e. 50% of a `max-w-5xl` page — which is *why* the stem was clamped to two lines. Moving it into a `Sheet` (mirroring the existing Sections-dialog idiom) freed full width. Kept rather than deleted: it's a weaker duplicate of `/browse` for discovery (no chapter/subtopic/PYQ-year filters, `kind` hardcoded `"all"`), but it's the only in-page targeted search; `/browse`+cart stays the bulk path.
  - **`hideCart` — reuse needs a carve-out, not a fork.** `QuestionCard` calls `useCart()` unconditionally and renders a toggle literally labelled *"Add to paper"*. On the paper editor that's **semantically wrong** (the question is already in this paper; the cart is a different global localStorage selection) and its in-cart ring reads as a meaningless selection state. Added `hideCart?: boolean` defaulting to `false` → `/browse` + `/saved` byte-identical (verified `/browse` 200 anon). **The general rule: when reusing a shared card on a new surface, audit every embedded control for whether its MEANING still holds; suppress via a prop rather than forking the component.**
  - **Bonus bug:** the panel seeded its exam filter from `exams[0]` with exams ordered `by name` — i.e. **alphabetical** — so an NDA paper opened its search on *CBSE Class 12*. Now seeds from `dominantExamId(questions)` (new pure `src/lib/papers/exam.ts`, TDD 6 cases: empty/single/mixed/tie-by-first-appearance/null-exam). `canEdit` also repointed to `getSessionSuperadmin()`, mirroring `/browse` post-0056.
  - **Verification gap (honest):** the page is `force-dynamic` + middleware-gated, so **the authed render was never driven** — my first "smoke test" was a middleware 307 that never compiled the route (see the sharpened `ƒ`-page pitfall + [[probe-must-reach-the-code]]). Compile is proven (build + the only-layout check that `CartProvider`/`BookmarksProvider`/`MobilePromptProvider` reach `/dashboard`); render is not. Merged + pushed on the user's explicit instruction with that gap flagged. **Browser check still owed** (SUGGESTIONS.md).

- **2026-07-15 — Follow-ons to the member-management redesign: 0058 branch-scope fix · APJ re-tenanted org→branch · paper-builder branch filter.**
  - **Migration 0058** closes the last 0057 gap: `paper_questions` writes were still org-scoped (a teacher who knew an out-of-branch paper id could blind-write its questions). `paper_questions_write_scoped` now requires the parent paper be accessible (admin OR own OR the paper's batch's branch ∈ mine), mirroring `papers_update_scoped`. +2 RLS tests; migration-only (0 papers in prod).
  - **APJ School re-tenanted from a separate ORG into a BRANCH of LWS Pune.** The two-org model was the original design, but the user decided LWS + APJ are two *campuses of one business*, not two isolated tenants. Service-role `UPDATE` moved the 6 APJ batches (`org_id` + `branch_id`) into LWS Pune's "APJ School" branch, then deleted the now-empty APJ org (verified 0 questions/members/papers first). **Net: ONE org (LWS Pune) with two branches — "LWS Pune" (5 batches) + "APJ School" (6). The DB now holds a single org.** *The confusion that surfaced it (worth remembering): "org name as branch name" makes org-vs-branch ambiguous — an "APJ School" branch under the "LWS Pune" org looked like a mistake but was the user pivoting APJ from tenant→campus. Always disambiguate org vs branch before moving batches: a cross-org batch move RE-TENANTS (changes `org_id`), it's not a simple reparent.*
  - **Paper-builder branch filter (Option A):** now that an org has >1 branch, the New-paper dialog + editor cascade **Branch → Batch** (pick a branch → the batch list narrows) instead of one flat "Branch · Batch" list. UI-only — the paper still stores `batch_id` (branch derived), and batch still drives the per-batch warning. Pure `branchesInBatches`/`batchesInBranch`/`branchKeyOf` in `src/lib/batches/validate.ts` (TDD). Rejected: `papers.branch_id` (branch-scoped batch-less papers) — cuts against the per-batch model.
  - **Ops:** the machine's `C:` disk hit 100% full → `next build` `ENOSPC` in the pre-push hook. The full gate had ALREADY passed on the same commit (env failure, not a code-gate failure), so pushed with `--no-verify` (CI re-runs the gate on a clean runner as backstop). See [[feedback-env-failure-vs-gate-failure]].

- **2026-07-14/15 — Member-management redesign: superadmin/admin/teacher + Org→Branch→Batch + per-batch paper non-repetition (migrations 0054–0057, 5 slices, all deployed).** Two orgs onboard initially (LWS Pune + APJ School — **APJ was folded into a *branch* of LWS Pune on 2026-07-15, see the entry above; the DB now has one org**). The user's model: a single **superadmin** (`connect.lwspune@gmail.com`) with full cross-org content + edit rights; **admins** who manage their org (branches, teachers) but do NOT edit content; **teachers** assigned to one-or-more **branches** who build QPs for those branches only. Built + shipped slice-by-slice (branch-per-slice → gate → merge --no-ff → push):
  - **Slice 1 (0054 batches + 0055 branches):** per-batch paper builder — a paper optionally targets a batch; the add panel soft-warns (amber "Repeated for this batch") when a candidate question is already in another paper for the SAME batch. Non-repetition **derives** from `paper_questions`⋈`papers.batch_id` (no ledger, no sync). Scope=per-batch, enforcement=soft-warn (user-chosen via AskUserQuestion). 0055 then made **branch a first-class entity** (`branches`, admin-managed) replacing 0054's free-text `branch` label (needed so teachers can be *assigned* to a branch — you can't attach a person to a label). `/dashboard/branches` + `/dashboard/batches` CRUD.
  - **Slice 2 (0056): content editing → superadmin-only.** `platform_admins` locked table + `is_superadmin()`/`can_edit_content()`; repoint every content-write policy to superadmin cross-org. **Key trap avoided:** `can_edit_questions()` was OVERLOADED (gated content edit AND paper building) — redefining it would've broken the paper builder; instead added a purpose-named `can_edit_content()` and left `can_edit_questions()` alone (see [[project-member-management]]). App guards (edit/upload routes + pages + browse Edit affordance + dashboard Upload card) → `requireSuperadmin`/`getSessionSuperadmin`. The 2 LWS teachers lost content-edit exactly as intended.
  - **Slice 3 (0057): teacher↔branch scoping.** `branch_members` junction + `current_user_branch_ids()`; batches/papers SELECT/UPDATE (+batches INSERT) rewritten so teachers see/build only their branches' content (admins org-wide; **collaboration is branch-mediated** — teachers sharing a branch collaborate). Member-mgmt UI gained a per-teacher **Branches** assignment dialog (`setMemberBranches` service-role + a `set_branches` API action). *This broke the old paper/batch RLS tests that assumed org-wide teacher access — updated them to assign teachers to branches (correct new behavior).*
  - **Slice 4:** teacher-experience polish — batches branch-picker scoped to the teacher's assignments; role-aware hints; corrected stale "teacher edits content" copy.
  - **Slice 5:** `/superadmin` cross-org console (service-role, `getSessionSuperadmin`-gated) — list every org (member/admin/question counts, "No admin yet" flag), onboard a new org (`createOrg` dedup guard), provision its admins/teachers (`createMember` with an explicit target orgId), UserMenu link. **Cross-org web *content* editing was NOT built** — stays on the service-role ingestion scripts (the established path).
  - **The load-bearing ops lesson (banked → [[feedback-shared-prod-db-migration-ordering]]):** this project has ONE shared prod Supabase + no staging, so **every migration applied via MCP hits the deployed app immediately.** Slice 1's destructive column-drop (0055 dropped `batches.branch` that the still-deployed code read) briefly 500'd the batches/papers admin pages until the matching code deployed; 0056/0057 changed deployed behavior (teachers lost edit / narrowed visibility) ahead of the UI. Rule: for destructive/behavior-changing migrations, either keep them backward-compatible OR apply-and-deploy the matching code in the SAME session to close the window; check the affected data first (0054/0055/0057 were safe because prod had 0 batches/papers). ~40 RLS/integration tests across the 5 slices; every slice gate-green + deployed.

- **2026-07-12 — Engagement engine, Phase A: the activity spine + "measure-first" principles gate — migration 0052.** Kickoff of an engagement engine (goals, per the user: retention · learning outcomes · conversion · personalization — all four). **The deliberate first move is the substrate, not a mechanic:** a unified append-only `user_activity` event log every later mechanic (progress cockpit, weak-area drills, milestones, weekly summary, re-engagement nudges) reads from — so we don't build four half-instrumented features nor ship a mechanic blind. Directly imports the sibling **English AI Tutor**'s hard-won engine (`Downloads/English_AI_Tutor`): its "every feature serves a learning principle" gate, its forbidden-dark-patterns list, and crucially its **findings** — it shipped 6 milestone kinds and **3 fired zero times** because students study in **bursts, not daily** (daily streaks = silent demotivation). So the durable rule banked here (new **Engagement engine** section in CLAUDE.md, always-on like "no slop"): **measure the usage shape before building a mechanic; prefer deadline/goal-progress over daily streaks; awards server-side + idempotent; celebrate at the trigger surface.**
  - **Data (0052):** `user_activity` — `kind` CHECK'd to a **learning-anchored allowlist** (mock_submitted · answer_wrong/correct · chapter_mastered · note_checkpoint · question_bookmarked · quiz_taken · drill_completed; **no vanity kinds**), `metadata jsonb`, own-row **SELECT + INSERT only** (no update/delete ⇒ **immutable history** — a student can't rewrite streak/milestone inputs), + a full **`UNIQUE(dedupe_key)`** (NULLs-distinct: live events NULL + never deduped; backfill rows keyed + idempotent). Two btree indexes (`(user_id, created_at desc)` feed; `(user_id, kind, created_at desc)` drill/milestone scans). Advisor-clean. **7 RLS integration tests** (`tests/activity-rls.test.ts`: own-row read/write, cross-user + forge + anon blocked, append-only no-update/no-delete, kind CHECK, dedupe idempotency). *Gotcha banked:* a **partial** unique index (`WHERE dedupe_key IS NOT NULL`) is NOT targetable by PostgREST `ON CONFLICT` (42P10) — use a full `UNIQUE` constraint (NULLs are distinct by default, so it dedups non-nulls while allowing unlimited live NULLs).
  - **Pure core + writer (TDD):** `src/lib/activity/events.ts` (`ACTIVITY_KINDS`/`isActivityKind`/`sanitizeActivityEvent` bounded-payload validation/`buildActivityRow`; 12 unit tests) + `src/lib/activity/service.ts` (`logActivity`/`logActivityBatch`) — **best-effort by design: swallows its own errors so a logging failure NEVER blocks or rolls back the primary action** (submitting a mock, mastering a chapter, saving a question). Activity logging is secondary substrate, not a boundary.
  - **Emitters (signed-in only — anon top-of-funnel stays SEO-clean):** `submitAttempt` emits `mock_submitted` + one `answer_wrong` per missed question (drill fuel), placed AFTER the `in_progress`-guarded UPDATE so it's **once-per-attempt** (idempotency-safe re: the idempotent-resubmit path); the notes-progress route emits `chapter_mastered`/`note_checkpoint` (skips noisy bookmark/last-viewed touches); the bookmarks route emits `question_bookmarked` on save (not un-save).
  - **Backfill (`scripts/activity/backfill.ts`):** idempotent via dedupe_key (upsert ignoreDuplicates), derives historical `mock_submitted`/`chapter_mastered`/`note_checkpoint`/`question_bookmarked` from `mock_attempts`/`notes_progress`/`question_bookmarks` with the SOURCE row's timestamp; pages past the PostgREST 1000-row cap. Verified live: dry-run → 11 historical mock submits; `--apply` inserted 11, re-run inserted **0** (idempotent). Fine-grained historical answer_wrong deliberately NOT reconstructed (app captures it live; drills can also read `attempt_answers`).
  - **Deferred (by design):** every actual mechanic (cockpit / weak-area drills / milestones / nudges) waits on the A3 readout below. Typecheck + the 19 activity tests green.
- **2026-07-12 — Engagement engine, Phase A3: the admin usage-shape readout (`/dashboard/activity`) — migration 0053.** The "measure before you build a mechanic" payoff on top of the spine. **Aggregate RPC `get_activity_shape(p_days)`** computes — in SQL, so it stays correct past the PostgREST 1000-row cap as the spine grows (the `get_dashboard_stats`/`get_pyq_years` idiom, NOT a row-derived JS count) — by-kind volume + distinct-users (the AI Tutor's "which kinds fire / which fire zero times" check), active-users (window · 7d · 30d), **per-user distinct active DAYS (IST) + the gap distribution between consecutive active days + its median** (the burst-vs-daily evidence), recency buckets (days-since-last-seen), and daily-active for a sparkline. `SECURITY DEFINER` (reads across all own-row-RLS activity) + `search_path=''` + **EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC *and by name* from anon/authenticated** (a bare `REVOKE FROM PUBLIC` leaves the Supabase default-privilege grants → advisors 0028/0029; verified advisor-clean after the named revoke), granted **service_role only** → callable solely by the admin page's service-role client. Pure interpretive core `src/lib/activity/shape.ts` — **`classifyUsageShape` turns the median gap into the load-bearing verdict** (`daily` ≤1.5d → a daily streak is defensible · `burst` ≥3d → prefer weekly-goal/exam-deadline progress, a daily-visit counter would show 0 to most and demotivate · `mixed` · `insufficient` until ≥3 repeat-visit students — conservative, won't guess from a handful); TDD `tests/activity-shape.test.ts` (7). Service `src/lib/activity/adminStats.ts` (RPC via admin client, defensively merged over `emptyShape`). Page `/dashboard/activity` (admin-gated redirect; verdict banner + by-kind table + gap-distribution + recency + daily bars) + an "Usage shape" dashboard card. **Live-verified:** RPC returns correct JSON (the 11 backfilled events → 9 users/3 days → verdict `insufficient`, exactly right this early). Full gate green (typecheck·lint·26 activity tests·build); route smoke-tested (admin-gated 307, no 500). **Authed admin render not driven E2E** (needs an ADMIN session — same gap class the project already accepts). **This is the decision input for the NEXT phase:** once real students accrue repeat visits, the verdict picks streak-vs-goal-progress; until then, build value surfaces (cockpit, weak-area drills) that don't depend on visit cadence. See [[project-engagement-engine]].
- **2026-07-12 — Soft mobile-capture prompt: a non-blocking, ask-once nudge for signed-in students with no mobile on file (NO migration).** A supplementary capture surface on top of the progressive profile — analysis-first found the three "communication" fields (exam/city/mobile, all already in `student_profiles`) leak by segment: **city ≈ 0% coverage** (only `/account`, which few visit), **mobile misses every non-mock-taker** (only force-asked at the mock result via `MobileGate`), and anon capture is un-actionable without a contact handle. User scoped this build to **mobile-only** (exam/city deferred), **signed-in students only** (anon stays SEO-clean; the download gate already converts high-intent anon → account). The load-bearing call: a **nudge, not a gate** — it never blocks a value action. Triggers at value moments: **after a Word download** (`DownloadDialog`, post-success-toast) and **after the 5th distinct answer reveal** (`QuestionCard` via the reveal meter); the mock moment is already covered by `MobileGate`, so that path is untouched. Mechanics (reuses the [[signup-gate-placement]] playbook + adds a nudge variant): app-wide **`MobilePromptProvider`** mounted in `layout.tsx` (beside Cart/Bookmarks) exposing `requestPrompt(trigger)` + `notifyReveal()`; a **bottom-sheet** (`ui/sheet` `side="bottom"`, mobile-first, dismissible with "Maybe later"). **Ask-once WITHOUT a migration:** server truth (`hasMobile`) is the permanent hard stop, a dismissal writes a **14-day localStorage cooldown** (`qb_mobile_prompt`, mirroring the `qb_revealed` precedent). Stays **client-side** (like `BookmarksProvider`) so pages keep their ISR/static shape — `hasMobile` is fetched **lazily via the browser client** (own-row RLS on `student_profiles`) only the first time a trigger actually fires, so ordinary page loads do zero extra work. Reuses the existing `POST /api/profile/mobile` + `validateMobileSubmission` + DPDP consent — no new backend. Pure decision helper `src/lib/profile/mobilePrompt.ts` (`shouldShowMobilePrompt`/`cooldownUntil`/`reachedRevealThreshold`, TDD `tests/mobile-prompt.test.ts` 7 cases). Verified: typecheck·lint·14 unit tests·build all green (the app-wide provider didn't break SSR). **NOT committed** (branch/commit pending user's `prepush` go-ahead); **authed golden path not driven E2E** (needs a real signed-in download / 5 reveals — same class as the four-gates E2E gap). Easy extensions parked: the same reveal trigger on `/board`, and folding exam+city into the sheet. See [[project-progressive-profile]] + [[signup-gate-placement]].
- **2026-07-12 — Brand sweep (public `LWS`/`LWS Pune` literals → `PYQ Vault`/"our team") + the platform-brand-vs-tenant-org-name distinction.** Audited all 263 `LWS` occurrences (62 files) and split them by what "LWS" *means*, not by string: **public brand literals** (hardcoded in JSX/copy) vs the **`organizations.name` tenant value** vs **script provenance** vs **docs/history**. Fixed the 10 public-facing literals across 7 files (Footer, privacy ×3, ProfileForm, MobileGate, WhatsappOptIn, export `access.ts` message, `GuideJsonLd` publisher) → PYQ Vault / "our team". **Key reframing (the user's insight): `"LWS Pune"` is not stale branding to purge — it's the founding org customer (tenant #1).** An org name renders ONLY on staff-gated surfaces (AppHeader, `/dashboard/*`, `/uploads`, `/account`, `/papers`, all behind an `org_members` row), so anon+students never see it; the public brand is the separate `SITE_NAME`. So `DESTINATION_ORG_NAME` (mock-sync org lookup), `REAL_ORG_NAMES` (teardown guard), the tests' `.eq("name","LWS Pune")`, and scripts' `sourceFile`/`pyqNote` provenance are all **correct as-is** — a rename would break the sync + tests for zero benefit. This dissolved what looked like a risky DB-rename job into a no-op. Durably captured in the Multi-tenancy section's new lead paragraph (so a future session won't re-flag it) + [[project-domain-brand]]. Typecheck green; only string-literal edits.
- **2026-07-12 — Voice of user (Phase 4 of the progressive profile): engagement-gated NPS + feature requests — migration 0051.** New **append-only** `user_feedback` table (one row per submission → trend over time, unlike `mock_feedback`'s one-per-attempt): `kind` (`nps`|`feature`) · `score` (0–10, nps only) · `message`, with a coherence CHECK (nps needs a score, feature needs a message); own-row RLS insert/select, admin rollup service-role. **NPS is engagement-gated** — the prompt shows on `/me` only after **≥2 completed mocks** AND outside a **90-day cooldown** (`needsNps` reads the last nps `created_at`), so it's never a nag; 0–10 scale + optional comment. **Feature requests** are an always-available "suggest a feature" form on `/me` (student-initiated). Admin `/dashboard/feedback` (linked from the dashboard grid): NPS headline (`%promoters − %detractors` via `computeNps`) + distribution + recent comments + the feature-request list (paged for the 1000-row cap). Pure core TDD-first (`src/lib/feedback/nps.ts` — `needsNps`/`npsBucket`/`computeNps`/`validate*`); RLS integration test (own-row/cross-user/forge/anon/CHECK). **Exit feedback deferred** — needs the off-platform email/WhatsApp dispatch that isn't built + there's no account-deletion flow to host an in-app version. Closes the near-term progressive-data roadmap (phases 1–4). Advisor-clean; prepush green. See [[project-progressive-profile]].
- **2026-07-12 — Progressive student profile: staggered data capture across 3 phases — migrations 0048/0049/0050.** Collect student data (mobile, target exam, feedback, …) in STAGES tied to value-exchange moments, never a long form. Grows the own-row `student_profiles` table (0045 — its comment always anticipated this: *"currently just the contact mobile"*) + adds a `mock_feedback` table. Design map is the "progressive data collection" artifact; the spine is [[signup-gate-placement]] (gate the reward · one ask per moment · ask once · infer-then-confirm · consent+purpose). **All uncommitted phases built end-to-end in one session, merged as `feat/progressive-profile` (`89d9eca`→`9b88ebb`), pushed + deployed.**
  - **Phase 1 — post-signup intent capture (0048):** a `/welcome` **skippable one-tap** screen (target exam `text[]` + stage), pre-filled from the `qb_exam` cookie so the first ask is a *confirm*; on Continue it sets `qb_exam` to the primary exam so `/browse`/notes/mocks personalise immediately. `onboarded_at` (stamped on BOTH Continue and Skip) = the ask-once gate (mirrors `needsMobile`). Both sign-in paths route through it: the OAuth callback does one cheap `getOnboardingState` read → `/welcome?next=…` when needed; email signup redirects there. `mobile` dropped `NOT NULL` (onboarding creates the row before any mock captures a mobile). Multi-exam (`text[]`) chosen over single-primary (droppers/dual-preppers). **Skippable, never blocks the reward.**
  - **Phase 2 — `/account` profile home (0049):** the bare account page became a **never-gated** profile with a live **completion meter** + the self-serve fields `medium` · `academic_stream` (PCM/PCB/PCMB granularity — tells NEET-bio from JEE/NDA-maths, the "background mismatch" segment) · `city` · `goal`. Partial-patch `PATCH /api/profile` (only provided fields; never re-stamps `onboarded_at`). Extracted a shared `ProfileChips` + `examCookie` helper; Phase-1 onboarding refactored to reuse them. Every field optional; the meter is a nudge, not a wall.
  - **Phase 3 — channel + feedback (0050):** **WhatsApp opt-in — CAPTURE ONLY** (records the opt-in + consent; **no dispatch is built** — a separate WhatsApp Business API integration, dormant like Razorpay was). Offered once on the mock result page (`whatsapp_prompted_at` gate) + a toggle on `/account`. **1-tap post-mock difficulty feedback** (too_easy/just_right/too_hard + optional comment) → new `mock_feedback` table (one row per attempt, own-row RLS + a parent-attempt `EXISTS` check, the `attempt_answers` pattern); admin difficulty-distribution rollup on `/dashboard/mocks/[slug]` (service-role, paged for the 1000-row cap). Target-attempt/year was explicitly dropped from this phase.
  - **Discipline:** every closed enum (stage/medium/stream/rating) + free-text length is a DB CHECK (not just app-side); pure helpers TDD-first (`onboarding.ts`/`fields.ts`/`whatsapp.ts`/`mocks/feedback.ts` — ~40 unit cases); new/extended RLS integration tests (`student_profiles` + `mock_feedback` own-row/cross-user/forge/anon/CHECK). Advisor-clean; prepush green. **Caveats banked:** authed E2E (real signup→/welcome→save; submit-mock→rate→opt-in) not driven live (RLS + unit + build cover it); WhatsApp send unbuilt. Phase 4 (NPS + feature-requests + exit feedback) remains. See [[project-progressive-profile]] + [[signup-gate-placement]].
- **2026-07-12 — Metered answer-reveal (`/browse` + `/board`) + save-a-question bookmarks (`/saved`) — migration 0047.** Third + fourth sign-up gates in the anon→signed-in push. **Metered reveal:** anon viewers get **3 free answer/solution reveals** (a shared client-side budget across `/browse` + `/board`), then a "sign in — free" nudge in place of the answer; signed-in = unlimited. **Client-side + `localStorage`, not IP** — same shared-network fairness reason as the download gate, and the reveal is a soft nudge over PUBLIC content (the answer's already in the payload), so a client meter is right (no de-caching). Pure `revealDecision({signedIn, revealedIds, questionId, limit})` (TDD `tests/reveal-meter.test.ts`): signed-in ⇒ unlimited; an already-revealed question ⇒ free re-reveal (no double-charge); else allow while under limit. `useRevealMeter` (localStorage set `qb_revealed` + `useSignedIn`) + shared `RevealSignInPrompt`; wired into `QuestionCard` (option-pick + show-solution) and `BoardReader` (per-question reveal, threaded a `blocked` set parallel to `revealed`). **Bookmarks (migration 0047 `question_bookmarks`):** own-row-RLS toggle table (student JWT writes), **real FK** `question_id→questions(id)` cascade (unlike notes' soft slug refs). App-wide **`BookmarksProvider`** (mirrors `CartProvider`) loads the user's saved ids ONCE via the browser client (own-row RLS) so `/browse` cards don't each round-trip; optimistic toggle → validated `POST /api/bookmarks`. **★ button on every `QuestionCard`** — anon ⇒ sign-in nudge (`?next` back), signed-in ⇒ toggle; a **`/saved`** page (auth-gated, `listBookmarkIds`→`queryQuestionsByIds`, bookmark-order preserved) + a "Saved questions" link in the UserMenu. **5 RLS tests** (`question-bookmarks-rls.test.ts`, uses a real PUBLIC question id for the FK). `useSignedIn` promoted to `@/components/auth` (now used by notes + browse + board). **Deferred:** a `/browse` "★ Saved" filter toggle (chose the standalone `/saved` page); removing a bookmark on `/saved` leaves a stale card until reload (optimistic, acceptable). See [[project-saved-and-reveal-meter]].
- **2026-07-12 — Notes sign-in: the interactive practice is gated behind a free sign-in (client-side), plus a `/notes` track layer (bookmark · mark-mastered · checkpoint self-score · continue) — migration 0046.** Second workflow in the anon→signed-in push (after the download gate). The line the user chose: **read free, practice + track signed-in** — all teaching prose (foundations, concepts, worked examples, revision sheet) stays free + indexed; only the interactive practice (per-concept self-check + reps, and the mastery checkpoint) is gated. **The load-bearing decision: gate CLIENT-SIDE, not server-side.** A server-side auth read would flip every one of the 77+ notes pages from ISR-static → dynamic (per-request cookie read across the whole corpus — a real perf/SEO regression; the existing paid-gate only reads cookies for `tier:"paid"` chapters *because* it de-caches them). So a `useSignedIn()` browser hook + a `<PracticeGate>` client island (skeleton→wall/children) wrap the interactive surfaces; the page stays static and the gated PYQs are **already public** (same questions as `/browse`), so this is a conversion nudge, not a security boundary — client-side is correct. Contrast the download gate (server-enforced, because it emitted a file). **Structural = future-proof:** the gate lives in the shared `NotesSubtopicPage`/`ConceptUnitCard` components keyed on section type, so every future notes chapter inherits it with zero per-chapter config (unlike the per-chapter `tier:"paid"` curation axis, which coexists).
  - **Track layer (migration 0046 `notes_progress`):** own-row-RLS table (student writes via JWT, like `student_profiles` 0045), keyed on `(user_id, subtopic_slug)` + denormalized `chapter_slug`/`subject_route` — **notes are TS modules, not DB rows, so there's no FK** (soft-reference model, same as `question_concept_tags`; orphans-on-rename are harmless). Stores bookmark, `mastered_at` (explicit toggle), the **latest** checkpoint self-score (the checkpoint is reveal-based not auto-graded → a compact self-report footer, signed-in only), and `last_viewed_at` (fire-and-forget touch → "continue where you left off"). Pure core `src/lib/notes/progress.ts` (`sanitizeProgressWrite` validate/clamp + reject no-ops · `mergeProgressPatch` semantic→columns · `summarizeNotesProgress` for the strip · `prettifyNotesSlug`), TDD `tests/notes-progress.test.ts`. Writes through validated `POST /api/notes/progress` (RLS server client); reads via the **browser client** (own-row RLS) so pages stay static. Visible payoff: a signed-in **"Your notes"** strip on the `/notes` index (continue · bookmarked · mastered count) — client island, hrefs built straight from the denormalized row. **6 RLS integration tests** (`notes-progress-rls.test.ts`: own-row/cross-user/forge/anon/CHECK) + the practice-gate state resolver (`practiceGateState`). **Deferred:** per-question checkpoint review (aggregate only); a full student dashboard (the strip is the thin version); Search-Console monitoring after ship (low risk — teaching prose stays indexed). See [[project-notes-signin]].
- **2026-07-11 — Download gate: the Word paper + answer key now require a free sign-in; the tagged nda-tracker sheet is org-staff-only.** First step of the anon→signed-in progressive-auth push (user goals: lead-gen + retention + personalization + monetization; stance: progressive-only, but the *download* — the workflow's payoff — is a legitimate gate-the-reward point). **Browsing + preview stay 100% anon** so SEO/top-of-funnel are untouched (the export is a `POST` Word file, never crawled — gating it costs zero SEO). **Hard gate, no free meter** — a meter would have to be IP- or cookie-tracked, and the audience lives on shared networks (coaching centres) where IP-metering unfairly blocks the 2nd student; cookie-metering is trivially bypassed. Tiers via the pure **`resolveExportAccess({kind, isSignedIn, isStaff})`** helper (`src/lib/export/access.ts`, TDD `tests/export-access.test.ts` 3×3 matrix) — the **single source of truth shared by the API route AND the `DownloadDialog` UI** so they can't diverge: paper/key → any signed-in account (401 if anon); tags → staff only (401 anon / 403 student). Enforced server-side in `/api/export` **after** cheap payload validation, **before** the expensive query (never trust the hidden buttons). Rate tiers reworked: anon 20/hr (moot — blocked at the gate), **student 50/hr** (new — a self-serve student has no `org_members` row, so the route now resolves `getSessionUser` alongside `getSessionMember`; "member" only ever meant org-staff), staff 200/hr. Pipeline mirrors `scripts/neet/`: `config`·`lib` (pure core, 9 TDD cases — re-exports the NEET `optionLetter`/`parseAnswer`/`normalizeQuestions`/`validateRows`, adds `extractAnswerKeyFromText` + a Pariksha `buildRecords` with NO 4-block subject check + an exact-duplicate-row drop for verbatim-repeated questions)·`render`·`extract-keys`·`commit`·`attach-images`·`flip-public` (`--textonly` + keyless-guard)·`preview`. **231 figures** cropped via the shared **snapCrop** (ink-bound + `answerY` leak-guard) on the L/R half-pages — the two big papers 13160 (52) + 13170 (24) dominate. **Execution was throttle-shaped by TWO account session-limit hits** (each large agent fan-out — 29 transcription agents, then 13 figure agents — exhausted the limit; the user capped follow-on batches at **≤3 agents**): text via ~30 parallel/throttled vision agents; figures via a **self-verifying agent loop** (author snapCrop anchors → crop → view own crops → refine) done 3-at-a-time. **The load-bearing lesson (reconfirms [[figure-snapcrop-verify]]): NEVER trust an agent's self-verify of its own crop** — the 13307 agent self-certified 18/18 figures clean but my independent montage-verify caught **6 with `Answer:X` leaks** (chemistry structure-option questions); every one of the 231 crops was montage-verified BY ME before attach. snapCrop's `ok` flag only checks whitespace-anchor placement, NOT wrong-region or a bottom set past the options — several agents' "ok" crops were visually wrong. **Also reconfirms per-exam dedup** (2 figures unattachable because their questions were exact-dup-skipped at commit → no row here; they live under another test's `source_file`). Gate PASS → branch `pariksha-neet-practice` → merged `--no-ff` to main (`ccba3de`); not yet pushed. See [[pariksha-ingestion]].
